# The four architecture phases, and the one acceptance that is a loss

Decision taken at round 256, from a direct instruction to split the 84 pipeline errors into independent
architecture phases rather than compile them out. This file is the contract for those four phases. Each
phase is independent: it can be started, measured, and reverted without touching the other three.

    state at the decision point   TOTAL 152   axis1 73 / axis2 73 / axis3 0 / cascades 6
    measurement                   measure.sh under M2, transcript md5 recorded per run

Shared acceptance line for every phase, unchanged from stages 1-28:

    target axis DOWN, axis1 and axis3 byte-identical, cascades NOT UP

A rise in axis1 or axis3 means the change moved errors between buckets instead of removing them, and is a
failure regardless of the total.

## The one accepted loss: Hud, 5 errors

26.2's net.minecraft.client.gui.Hud carries **64 private fields** (measured, javap -p). There is no shape
to reproduce and no extraction path that does not amount to writing a second HUD. These 5 errors are
accepted as lost. They stay in the count and the count is not claimed to be zero.

## Phase A -- pip, 31 errors

    26.2 types (all absent from 1.21.1, measured zero entries):
        net.minecraft.client.gui.render.pip.PictureInPictureRenderer        abstract class, 3 private fields, 6 methods
        net.minecraft.client.renderer.state.gui.pip.PictureInPictureRenderState   interface, 0 private fields, 10 methods
        net.neoforged.neoforge.client.gui.PictureInPictureRendererRegistration    record, 2 private fields, 7 methods
        net.neoforged.neoforge.event.RegisterPictureInPictureRendererEvent

    1.21.1 has:  GuiGraphics, BlockEntityWithoutLevelRenderer   (both present)
    1.21.1 lacks: the whole pip framework -- render/pip has zero entries

The 26.x pip framework renders a 3D thing into a rectangular region of a GUI. 1.21.1 can do that with
GuiGraphics pose plus a BlockEntityWithoutLevelRenderer, which is how 1.21.1 vanilla draws 3D items, but
there is no framework to hook: no registry, no render state, no bake step. So this phase is not a rename
and not a compat class. It is:

    A.1  decide the 1.21.1 extension point: a Forbric-owned registry of "draw this region with this
         renderer", driven from wherever the merged base currently asks the pip framework.
    A.2  implement the registry and one renderer that draws through GuiGraphics + BlockEntityWithoutLevelRenderer.
    A.3  bridge: a 26.x-shaped PictureInPictureRenderer/Registration on top, so the kernel's existing call
         sites keep their names.

    A.4  accept when axis1 and axis3 are unchanged and TOTAL drops by the 31.

CHEAPEST FIRST ACTION. Do not start at A.1. First run the shape probe the same way stage 28 did: take the
31 error sites, list exactly which members of the four types they touch. In the two phases that succeeded
in stage 28 the call sites used ONE member out of a large type. If pip is the same, A.2 shrinks to one
renderer. That probe is a single scripted javap pass and it decides whether this phase is a week or a month.

## Phase B -- class transformation, 21 errors

    net.neoforged.fml.classloading.transformation.{ClassProcessorSet,ClassTransformer,TransformingClassLoader}
    net.neoforged.fml.loading.mixin.FMLMixinClassProcessor
    net.neoforged.neoforgespi.transformation.{ClassProcessor,ClassProcessorIds,ProcessorName}

    measured: FMLMixinClassProcessor, ClassProcessor, ProcessorName, ClassProcessorSet -- all zero on 1.21.1
    1.21.1 has: cpw.mods.modlauncher.api.ITransformer, ITransformationService, ITransformerVotingContext,
                TransformingClassLoader, ClassTransformer

THIS PHASE HAS A DESIGN DECISION BEFORE ANY CODE, and it is the same kind of decision stage 1.1 demanded.
KernelFmlTransformerView (180 lines) does not bridge a capability. It builds a FAKE OBJECT GRAPH -- "exactly
that path and nothing beside it" -- so that one mod's mixin plugin (libjf_unsafe_v0) can walk
TransformingClassLoader -> classTransformer -> processors -> processors.get(ClassProcessorIds.MIXIN) ->
FMLMixinClassProcessor.transformer and wrap that in its own AsmTransformer.

    B.1  read libjf_unsafe_v0's mixin plugin and answer: on 1.21.1, does it need that walk to succeed, or
         does it take a different path when the classes are absent? This has never been read. Until it is,
         "excluding this phase loses nothing on 1.21.1" is an UNVERIFIED claim and must not be acted on.
    B.2  if the walk is needed: build the same graph against 1.21.1's real ModLauncher classes, and then
         the transformer's descriptor match has to be re-derived per line, because the merged base's
         onResourceReload descriptor differs between lines (stage 8).
    B.3  if the walk is not needed: exclude KernelFmlTransformerView from the 1.21.1 source set and record
         that libjf takes a different path there.

    B.4  accept when TOTAL drops by 21 AND B.1's answer is written down with evidence.

## Phase C -- tooltips, 18 errors

    net.neoforged.neoforge.common.tooltip.{TooltipAppender,TooltipLocation,ItemTooltipHandler}
    net.minecraft.world.item.component.TooltipDisplay                          (vanilla 26.x, NOT on 1.21.1)
    net.neoforged.neoforge.event.RegisterTooltipAppendersEvent

    measured on 1.21.1:  TooltipProvider present but WITHOUT the nested TooltipContext
                         DataComponentType present
                         TooltipFlag present
                         TooltipDisplay ABSENT  (the component tooltip system is 1.21.2+)
    shapes on 26.2:      TooltipAppender interface, 1 method
                         TooltipLocation enum, 4 constants (HEAD, POST_CUSTOM, PRE_ITEM_INFO, TAIL)
                         TooltipDisplay record, 3 private fields
                         ItemTooltipHandler class -- the ordering pipeline, private static lists

    C.1  TooltipAppender and TooltipLocation are reproducible for near-zero cost: an interface with one
         method and an enum with four constants. Do them, with the stage-28 discipline (compat package,
         Forbric prefix on the name, two table rows, measure).
    C.2  TooltipDisplay has to be DESIGNED, not copied: its job is per-item "hide these lines" flags. On
         1.21.1 there is no component-based tooltip so nothing consumes those flags. Decide whether to
         reproduce the flag semantics or to carry the record as a value object with no consumer, and write
         down which -- a value object with no consumer is a stub, and stubs are forbidden by the objective.
    C.3  ItemTooltipHandler is the pipeline. It is why the kernel file is 223 lines. Its 1.21.1 edition
         means reimplementing appender ordering on top of ItemTooltipEvent, and the observable order a mod
         sees will differ from 26.2's. That difference has to be recorded, not smoothed over.

    C.4  accept when TOTAL drops by 18 and the C.2 decision and the C.3 difference are both written down.

NOTE ON WHAT THIS PHASE PRESERVES. TooltipAppender does not exist in NeoForge 1.21.1, so no 1.21.1 mod can
use it. What phase C preserves is Forbric's own Fabric-to-NeoForge tooltip bridge: Fabric's tooltip
ordering hooks stop working without it. It is not mod compatibility and should not be sold as such.

## Phase D -- FuelValues, 14 errors

    net.minecraft.world.level.block.entity.FuelValues                         (vanilla 26.x, NOT on 1.21.1)
    kernel user: KernelFabricFuel, 29 sites

    measured on 1.21.1:  AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity.getFuel(): Map<Item,Integer>
                         AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity.isFuel(ItemStack)
                         NO FuelValues, NO Builder, NO DataMapHooks.populateFuelValues

    shapes on 26.2:      FuelValues class, 1 private field, 9 methods, has Builder + vanillaBurnTimes

WHAT THE KERNEL ACTUALLY DOES, and why "the data maps cleanly" was wrong: it does not read the fuel map.
It attaches to a BUILDER pipeline -- populateFuelValues hands it a builder, it adds entries, then build().
1.21.1 has no such pipeline.

    D.1  decide the 1.21.1 extension point: a Forbric fuel table built at datapack load, reachable where
         1.21.1's furnace asks for fuel. 1.21.1's getFuel() is a static map built from vanilla's list, so
         either it is rebuilt on reload or a lookup is interposed.
    D.2  implement that table and wire Fabric's fuel additions into it, which is the actual purpose of
         KernelFabricFuel (a Fabric mod's fuel never went into a furnace on the merged server).
    D.3  bridge: a 26.x-shaped FuelValues/Builder on top so the kernel's 29 sites keep their names.

    D.4  accept when TOTAL drops by 14, axis1/axis3 unchanged, and a reload rebuilding the table is
         demonstrated or explicitly recorded as not demonstrated.

## The per-site remainder, about 63 errors

Not part of these four phases. Every file with exactly one missing type has been assessed:
InteractionInformation (done, 872337f), ForgeLayer (done, 5931a24), ListenerKey and UnbakedModelParser
(descriptor-bound), VanillaClient/ServerListeners (private static data table that javap cannot reveal).
The rest are individually diagnosed in stage 27 and stage 28 and none of them is a rename.

## What is NOT in this plan

The kernel jar and the launch. Both are in stage 6 of the objective and both are blocked on TOTAL
reaching 0, which the four phases above do not reach: 31+21+18+14 = 84, leaving 152-84 = 68. Reaching zero
also requires the per-site remainder and the 5 Hud errors, and the 5 Hud errors are an accepted loss.

So this plan does not end in a jar. It ends at 68, or lower if the per-site remainder shrinks. That has to
be said at the start rather than discovered at the end.

# Phase D: the extension point, measured (round 258)

The first action for this phase was a shape probe, on the theory that stage 28's two wins came from
probing before writing. It paid: this phase is not the pipeline the earlier estimate assumed.

## What the 14 errors actually are

Two files, seven lines, not fourteen sites:

    KernelFabricFuel.java   179 lines   6 error lines   (the type, Builder, vanillaBurnTimes, and three reflective reads of Builder's private field)
    KernelFuelValues.java    98 lines   2 error lines   (burnDuration(ItemStack, int, RecipeType, FuelValues))

KernelFabricFuel does not model fuel. It interpolates into 26.2's builder pipeline: apply(Builder) adds
Fabric's fuels, throughVanillaReturnHooks(FuelValues) hooks vanillaBurnTimes' return. KernelFuelValues
arbitrates burn duration between Forge and NeoForge.

## 26.2's Builder, exactly

    private final HolderLookup<Item> items;
    private final FeatureFlagSet enabledFeatures;
    private final Object2IntSortedMap<Item> values;      <- the kernel reflects into this
    Builder(HolderLookup.Provider, FeatureFlagSet)
    Builder add(TagKey<Item>, int) / add(ItemLike, int) / remove(TagKey<Item>)
    FuelValues build()

## 1.21.1's extension point, measured present

    net.minecraft.world.item.ItemStack#getBurnTime(RecipeType)                       present
    net.neoforged.neoforge.common.extensions.IItemExtension#getBurnTime(ItemStack, RecipeType)   present
    net.neoforged.neoforge.event.furnace.FurnaceFuelBurnTimeEvent                    present
        (ItemStack, int, RecipeType), setBurnTime(int), implements ICancellableEvent
    net.minecraftforge.event.furnace.FurnaceFuelBurnTimeEvent                        present
    net.neoforged.neoforge.registries.datamaps.builtin.FurnaceFuel                   present

TWO CORRECTIONS TO WHAT THIS DOCUMENT SAID EARLIER, both from guessing instead of measuring:

1. "1.21.1 has no Builder pipeline, so build one." It has an extension point, a different one:
   FurnaceFuelBurnTimeEvent plus ItemStack.getBurnTime. The shape differs; the capability does not.

2. "AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity.buildFuels(ObjIntConsumer) is the registration hook." It is not. buildFuels
   is vanilla's own fuel list -- it feeds each vanilla fuel OUT through the consumer -- and getFuel() builds
   its private volatile fuelCache by passing in a map-putting consumer. It is a read entry point, not a
   write one, and fuelCache has no public mutation API.

## So D.1 is decided

    1.21.1 extension point   FurnaceFuelBurnTimeEvent  (subscribe, set the burn time)  +  ItemStack.getBurnTime for queries
    not                      a Builder pipeline, and not reflection into fuelCache

D.1  compat value object ForbricFuelValues over Map<Item,Integer>: isFuel, burnDuration, fuelItems,
     vanillaBurnTimes(Provider, FeatureFlagSet), and a Builder with add/remove/build plus a PUBLIC values
     field so the kernel's three reflective reads become direct field access. About 60 lines, and it is
     net less reflection than 26.2's path uses.
D.2  the real work: subscribe FurnaceFuelBurnTimeEvent and answer from the Fabric-registered table, which is
     the actual job of KernelFabricFuel -- a Fabric mod's fuel never reached a furnace on the merged server.
     This is a real implementation against a real 1.21.1 event, not a stub.
D.3  bridge: the 26.x-shaped Builder on top of the value object, so the kernel's existing call sites keep
     their names.
D.4  accept when TOTAL drops by 14, axis1 and axis3 unchanged, and the event subscription is either
     exercised or explicitly recorded as not exercised.

## Revised size

Earlier stages costed this phase as a pipeline build. With the measured extension point it is roughly:
one ~60-line value object, one event subscriber, one Builder shim, and the table rows. Not a week. The
estimate that it was a pipeline came from reading "getFuel(): Map<Item,Integer>" as "no extension point",
which is the same error as reading "zero references to ForgeLayeredDraw" as "ForgeLayeredDraw does not
exist" (stage 28).

# Phase B: the LibJF source was read, and it settles the question (round 273)

The contract said phase B must not be touched until libjf_unsafe_v0's mixin plugin had been read, because
"1.21.1 does not need that impersonation" was an unverified claim. The mod is not in this workspace (only a
`libjf-translate-v1.neoforge.mods.toml` test fixture exists), so it was fetched from upstream:

    https://git.jfronny.dev/JfMods/LibJF/src/commit/44f844afe2cba23ebf38787d78e09c271a4de66d/
        libjf-unsafe-v0/src/main/java/io/gitlab/jfronny/libjf/unsafe/MixinPlugin.java

## What onLoad actually does

    public void onLoad(String mixinPackage) {
        try {
            TransformingClassLoader cl = (TransformingClassLoader) Thread.currentThread().getContextClassLoader();
            Field f1 = TransformingClassLoader.class.getDeclaredField("classTransformer");
            Field f2 = ClassTransformer.class.getDeclaredField("pluginHandler");
            LaunchPluginHandler ph = (LaunchPluginHandler) f2.get(ct);
            FMLMixinLaunchPlugin plugin = (FMLMixinLaunchPlugin) ph.get("fml-mixin").orElseThrow(...);
            Field f3 = FMLMixinLaunchPlugin.class.getDeclaredField("transformer");
            AsmTransformer.INSTANCE.delegate = (IMixinTransformer) f3.get(plugin);
            f3.set(plugin, AsmTransformer.INSTANCE);
        } catch (Throwable e) {
            LibJf.LOGGER.error("Could not initialize LibJF ASM", e);
        }
    }

Every other method of the plugin is inert: shouldApplyMixin returns **false**, getMixins returns **null**,
preApply and postApply are empty, getRefMapperConfig returns null. So the plugin does no mixin applying at
all -- its entire purpose is the ASM hook install above.

## The answer, and it is decisive

THE WHOLE INSTALL IS WRAPPED IN catch (Throwable). A ClassCastException, a NoClassDefFoundError, a
NoSuchFieldException -- all of them are swallowed, one log line is written, and LibJF continues loading.

So on 1.21.1, where TransformingClassLoader does not exist, this plugin fails SOFTLY. Excluding
KernelFmlTransformerView therefore does not break LibJF and does not crash anything. The behavioural cost is
exactly:

    LibJF's ASM patches do not apply on 1.21.1, and LibJF logs "Could not initialize LibJF ASM".

That is one optional feature of one mod not working, self-reported. It is not a crash and not a silent
corruption.

## A second finding: the kernel comment describes a DIFFERENT LibJF version

KernelFmlTransformerView's own comment says the walk is
`classTransformer -> processors -> processors.get(ClassProcessorIds.MIXIN) -> FMLMixinClassProcessor.transformer`.
The upstream source at 44f844a walks
`classTransformer -> pluginHandler -> pluginHandler.get("fml-mixin") -> FMLMixinLaunchPlugin.transformer`.

Those are different object graphs. So the 180 lines of impersonation were written against a different LibJF
build than the one that was read. That does not make them wrong for the build they targeted, but it does mean
they cannot be validated from this source, and it further weakens the case for carrying them onto a line
where the real classes are absent anyway.

## So B.1 is decided

    decision   EXCLUDE KernelFmlTransformerView from the 1.21.1 source set
    evidence   the upstream plugin is fail-soft on Throwable, so absence costs a logged error, not a crash
    not done   the actual exclusion mechanism, which has not been found yet: normalizeRuntimeJava rewrites
               files in src/runtime/java but has no exclude step. A Gradle-side source-set or per-line
               exclusion has to be found or added, and that is the next step.

The claim in earlier stages that "excluding this phase loses nothing on 1.21.1" is now upgraded from
UNVERIFIED to VERIFIED-WITH-A-COST, and the cost is written down rather than waved away.

---

# After the four phases: 65 left, classified (round 281-283)

The four phases are done at TOTAL 65 (down from 152), and this section is the route map for the rest,
because walking into the remainder one error at a time produced two dead ends before it produced a fix.

## By symbol kind, all 65 parsed from the transcript

    class / interface / package   27
    method                        20
    variable                       7
    (no symbol attributed)        11

## The 27 type-ish errors, split by what can actually be done

CHEAP, about 2-4 of them. CuboidModel (1) plus its package name (1) is the only pair not yet ruled out.

NOT CHEAP, about 23, and each for a measured reason:

    Hud                  5   accepted loss; 64 private fields on 26.2
    FluidModel           4   vanilla 26.x render abstraction; zero entries on 1.21.1
    WeightedList + Weighted  5   paired, and 1.21.1's family is WeightedRandomList/WeightedEntry
    AnvilCraftEvent      2   no 1.21.1 equivalent event exists
    ListenerKey          2   descriptor-bound
    UnbakedModelParser   1   descriptor-bound
    VanillaClientListeners / VanillaServerListeners  2   private static data tables, invisible to javap
    TypedInstance         2   SEE BELOW -- the trap of this batch

## TypedInstance is the trap, and it is worth naming

    net.minecraft.core.TypedInstance<T>   interface, one abstract method typeHolder(), six defaults
    kernel use, KernelShears:31:          ((TypedInstance) stack).is(item)

A three-line compat interface makes that compile. It also guarantees a ClassCastException at runtime, because
1.21.1's ItemStack does not implement it and cannot be made to without a mixin. So TypedInstance looks like the
cheapest thing in the list and is actually in the "compiles and then throws" class -- the single failure mode
this whole port has been avoiding. Same reasoning ruled out ValueInput: it is an interface with EIGHT abstract
methods plus two nested types, used as a parameter type in two files, so it is a read API and not a name.

## The 20 method errors, which are the real wall

    getKeyEvent()                              5   in a file that hits 9 rename rules
    getVanillaResult / getXpCost / setXpCost   3   in a file that hits 13
    getFluidInteraction, getFogData, getFovScale, getItemBlockingWith, getLocation,
    getMouseButtonEvent, getMouseButtonInfo, getRegistries, getSpawnReason,
    fireFarmlandTrampleEvent, loadTagsForRegistry, location()   1 each, files hit 6-13

Each needs either an overlay -- whose cost IS that file's rule count, 6 to 13 hand-translations -- or a
semantic rewrite. The AnvilUpdateEvent three are the clearest example: 26.2 splits "cost" into getXpCost plus a
VanillaResult record that captures the pre-event values, and 1.21.1 has neither, because its event does not
record what it was constructed with. A compat VanillaResult could only read the event's CURRENT values, which
turns the kernel's untouched comparison at :254 into a self-comparison that is always true. That is a silent
semantic break, so the correct fix is to capture three values before Forge's event is posted -- i.e. code, in
a 13-rule file.

## So the honest shape of 65 -> 0

    accepted loss                      5   Hud
    possibly cheap                     2-4
    needs a pipeline or a rewrite     16
    needs an overlay, 6-13 rules each 20   the wall
    variables                          7
    unattributed                      11

Two of the earlier estimates in this document were optimistic and are corrected here: "about 11 cheap types"
was really 2-4, and "40-80 rounds for the remainder" did not price the 20 overlays at 6-13 translations each.

The four phases reached 65 with 11 measured changes, every one passing the acceptance line, every one pushed.
Getting from 65 to a jar is not more of the same work; it is a mechanical long run plus one design decision
about Hud that has not been made.

# Round 287: an overlay that was net-zero, and was reverted

KernelItemTooltips hits only TWO rename rules across its 98 lines, so it looked like the cheapest overlay in
the tree: two hand-translations for one error. Attempted, measured, reverted.

    baseline 9b28c5a   TOTAL 62  axis1 26  axis2 30  cascades 6
    with overlay       TOTAL 62  axis1 27  axis2 29  cascades 6

Net zero, and one error MOVED from axis2 into axis1. That is a re-bucket, which the acceptance line treats as
a failure regardless of the total, so the overlay file was deleted and the tree re-measured back to
62/26/30/6 and committed at 9b28c5a with no overlay in it.

The two edits that were tried, so the next attempt does not start from nothing:

    1. the signature's TooltipDisplay became net.forbric.compat.tooltip.ForbricTooltips.Display
    2. the constructor call dropped its sixth argument:
         new ItemTooltipEvent(stack, player, tooltip, flag, context, display)
       became
         new ItemTooltipEvent(stack, player, tooltip, flag, context)
       because 1.21.1's ItemTooltipEvent takes five arguments and 26.2's takes six.

Something ELSE in that file then broke on a Minecraft symbol, and there was no room to diagnose it. The
likely reason is that the file uses the display value beyond the signature, or that replacing the type
changed an inference somewhere the two edits did not reach. So: this file is NOT a two-translation overlay.
Whoever retries it should read all 98 lines and count the uses of the display argument before assuming two.

# Why 26.2 and 1.21.1 can disagree on a CONSTRUCTOR'S ARITY

This is the first case in the port of a type whose constructor takes a different NUMBER of arguments on the
two lines, and it matters because it rules out the usual compat trick. A compat subclass cannot fix it: its
super(...) call has to be valid on both lines, and six arguments are only valid on one. So arity differences
need either an overlay or a per-line factory, and neither is a table row.

---

# Round 306: the KernelGameClientEvents overlay, fully specified and not yet done

Five errors share one file and one method. This is the best ratio left in the port, and the spec below is
complete -- there is no unknown left in it. It is written down rather than executed because it is 14 edits plus
9 rule deletions in a single action, and a half-finished overlay would hide the verified 56 behind a number
nobody can trust.

## The two-line difference

    1.21.1  InputEvent$Key:  Key(int,int,int,int) + getKey/getScanCode/getAction/getModifiers
    26.2    InputEvent$Key:  Key(KeyEvent,int) + getKeyEvent(): KeyEvent + the same four ints

So 26.2 gained a packaged KeyEvent that 1.21.1 does not have, while both lines keep the four raw ints.

## The five call sites

    :73   ForgeHooksClient.onKeyInput(event.getKeyEvent(), event.getAction())
    :97   ForgeEventFactoryClient.onScreenKeyPressedPre(event.getScreen(), event.getKeyEvent())
    :103  ForgeEventFactoryClient.onScreenKeyPressedPost(event.getScreen(), event.getKeyEvent())
    :109  ForgeEventFactoryClient.onScreenKeyReleasedPre(event.getScreen(), event.getKeyEvent())
    :115  ForgeEventFactoryClient.onScreenKeyReleasedPost(event.getScreen(), event.getKeyEvent())

:73 is different from the other four, and the reason matters: ForgeHooksClient is a FORGE class, and 1.21.1's
onKeyInput takes four ints, so that line becomes

    ForgeHooksClient.onKeyInput(event.getKey(), event.getScanCode(), event.getAction(), event.getModifiers())

The other four go to ForgeEventFactoryClient, which is the KERNEL's own class, so a four-int overload can be
added there instead of rewriting all four lines -- or all four can take the same four-int form. That choice is
open; the arity difference is not.

## All nine rules this file hits, every one of them unique to this file

    net.minecraftforge.client.event.ScreenEvent.Init.Pre.BUS
    net.minecraftforge.client.event.ScreenEvent.Init.Post.BUS
    net.minecraftforge.client.event.ScreenEvent.Render.Pre.BUS
    net.minecraftforge.client.event.ScreenEvent.Render.Post.BUS
    net.minecraftforge.client.event.InputEvent.InteractionKeyMappingTriggered.BUS
    net.minecraftforge.client.event.ViewportEvent.RenderFog.BUS
    net.minecraftforge.client.event.ViewportEvent.ComputeFogColor.BUS
    net.minecraftforge.client.event.ComputeFovModifierEvent.BUS
    getDeltaTracker

Eight are the .BUS form and translate mechanically to MinecraftForge.EVENT_BUS. The ninth is already documented
in the table itself, at line 250-257: 26.2 has Minecraft#getDeltaTracker and NO getTimer; 1.21.1 has getTimer and
NO getDeltaTracker; DeltaTracker exists on both lines with the same shape. So the overlay spells getTimer.

BECAUSE ALL NINE ARE UNIQUE TO THIS FILE, the overlay must DELETE all nine rows in the same change. Overlaid
files are written verbatim and skipped by the rewriter, so those rows would match nothing afterwards, and the
orphan guard at build.gradle:539 would fail the build. This is the same trap as stage 23 / commit 80e3aa7,
which deleted eight rows for the same reason.

## The order

    1. copy KernelGameClientEvents.java into src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1/java/net/forbric/kernel/runtime/
    2. rewrite the eight .BUS forms to MinecraftForge.EVENT_BUS
    3. rewrite getDeltaTracker to getTimer
    4. rewrite the five getKeyEvent() call sites
    5. delete the nine rows from 1.21.1.tsv
    6. measure: expect 56 -> 51, axis1 and axis3 unchanged, cascades not up

If axis1 or cascades rises, one of the fourteen edits is wrong, and the four-criteria line is what will say so.

# Round 315: the chained-call scan, and what it actually found

Round 314 found that recipe.id().identifier() becomes recipe.id().location() after the rename table, and that
ResourceLocation has no location() -- because on 26.2 id() returns a ResourceKey and .identifier() unwraps it,
while on 1.21.1 id() returns the ResourceLocation directly. That raised a question the port had never asked:
does a rename rule stay correct when it fires on a CHAINED call, where the receiver's type may differ between
lines?

The scan: 120 non-overlaid files x 85 rules, matching only the chained form (a word character, a dot, then the
token). 29 of the 85 rules match in that position. Of those 29:

    28 are benign.  Either a type RENAME (Identifier, EntitySpawnReason, EntityTypes, AbstractBoat,
    ContainerStorage, ...), where rewriting a type name at a chained position is exactly right, or a match
    inside one of the compat classes' own javadoc (FuelValues, TooltipDisplay, PictureInPictureRenderer,
    ClassProcessorSet, FluidModel, UnbakedModelParser, ...), which is cosmetic.

    1 is a real hazard: identifier() firing in KernelNeoRegistries.

Together with the KernelRecipeSync case from round 314, that makes two places, not a class of problem. So TSV
correctness does NOT need a new checking dimension -- it needs human judgement on two call sites. That removes
a risk this document had started to assume was systemic.

## A side effect worth recording, because it is self-inflicted

Seven of the eight compat class files have rename-rule tokens inside their own javadoc, so normalizeRuntimeJava
rewrites their documentation too. The classes still compile and behave correctly -- the earlier invariant check
confirmed that the class NAMES are safe, which was the point of the Forbric prefix -- but the prose no longer
says what it said. ForbricUnbakedModelParser's javadoc, for instance, now reads as if it were describing itself.

That is not a functional defect. It is a documentation defect in documents whose entire purpose is to record
things accurately, which is why it is written down here rather than left to be noticed.

---

# Rounds 318-320: one win, one false positive, one semantic gap

## The win: KernelRecipeSync, 56 -> 55, cascades 6 -> 5

The rule identifier() -> location() is correct where the receiver is a ResourceKey and wrong where it is already
a ResourceLocation. 1.21.1's RecipeHolder.id() returns the location directly, so the overlay spells recipe.id().
Six edits for one error, which I had refused a round earlier as the worst ratio in the port -- wrongly, because
the edit count missed that cascades might fall and that a redundant row might fall with it. The redundant row:
net.neoforged.neoforge.network.payload.RecipeContentPayload was the only row that file used and nothing else used
it, so overlaying would have orphaned it; and it was redundant anyway, because the pattern's lookbehind does not
exclude a dot, so the simple-name row already matches the fully-qualified form. Deleted, 85 -> 84 rows.

That is the third time in this run that a cost estimate made from one number was wrong in the same direction.

## The false positive: KernelNeoRegistries was never broken

Round 315's scan reported two chained-call hazards. The second, key.identifier() at KernelNeoRegistries:155, has
NO error in the current build -- which proves the receiver is a ResourceKey on both lines and the rename is
correct there. So the scan reported 29 syntactic matches and exactly ONE was a real defect.

The scan's limit is worth stating precisely: it detects that a rule fires at a chained position, which is a
SYNTAX fact. It cannot detect whether the receiver's type is the same on both lines, which is the SEMANTIC
premise that decides correctness. Turning 85 rules into 2 candidates was useful; calling those 2 "hazards" was
not. Same mistake as round 249, where I "corrected" a correct conclusion on a guess.

## The semantic gap: KernelFluidBreathing is not a rename

    kernel use, :34:  entity.getFluidInteraction().isEyeInFluidMatching(entity, (subject,type,height) -> ...)

    26.2      getFluidInteraction() returns an object with isEyeInFluidMatching(entity, InFluidPredicate)
              InFluidPredicate.test takes THREE arguments: entity, FluidType, height
              it asks whether the EYE is in a matching fluid
    1.21.1    no getFluidInteraction at all
              LivingEntity has isInFluidType(BiPredicate) -- TWO arguments
              isInFluidType asks about the BODY, not the eye

Three differences at once: a missing holder object, a predicate arity, and eye-versus-body. Eye and body are not
interchangeable for a drowning check. So this one needs a design decision plus a recorded behaviour difference,
not a substitution -- and it is the third member of that class alongside AnvilUpdateEvent (which must capture
pre-event values) and CuboidModel (which cannot take an empty shell without silently emptying every model).

## Where 55 stands

    Hud, accepted loss                     5-6   not doable
    needs a semantic decision              >=3   fluid eye/body, anvil pre-values, cuboid deserialise target
    descriptor-bound or private data table    5   needs design
    compiles-then-throws or silent-wrong      4   refused on purpose
    BUS runtime risk                          3   needs a runtime scheme
    two-line signature comparison          ~20   overlay, 2-14 translations each
    other                                  ~12   per-error diagnosis

The cheap work is finished. Everything left costs either a design decision or a full overlay run, and the
architecture phases that carried this port from 152 to 55 are done.

---

# Round 322: the four cheapest single-error files, all measured, none a rename

I picked the four files with the FEWEST rename rules -- 2 to 3 each -- on the theory that rule count is the cost.
All four were inspected. Not one is a rename.

    KernelFinalizeSpawn:41        forge.getSpawnReason() on type FinalizeSpawn
                                  FinalizeSpawn is a 26.x event; 1.21.1 calls it FinalizeSpawnEvent. Whole
                                  different type, so the fix is a type swap plus whatever the event calls differ.

    KernelLootBridge:80           TagLoader.loadTagsForRegistry(ResourceManager, WritableRegistry)
                                  A static method whose signature changed. Needs a two-line signature
                                  comparison, not a substitution.

    KernelGameScreenMouseEvents:107   neo.getMouseButtonEvent() on type Pre
                                  Same shape as the getKeyEvent group: 26.2 packages the raw fields into an
                                  object that 1.21.1 does not have, so the call has to be rebuilt from
                                  getButton/getAction/getModifiers.

    KernelForgeConditions:226     anonymous class does not implement getAllTags(ResourceKey)
                                  1.21.1's ICondition.IContext declares an abstract method that 26.2 does not,
                                  so the anonymous implementation has to GROW a method.

So the four cheapest things left cost: a type swap, a signature comparison, a field reconstruction, and an added
method. Four different kinds of work, none of them a table row.

That settles the shape of 55: rule count predicted the WRITE volume and nothing about the READ volume. Every one
of these needs its two lines javap'd, its semantics decided, and only then an overlay. The cheap work is done,
and the claim in the phase contract that "the four phases bring this to 68" was optimistic about what 68 would
be like to finish.

---

# Round 325: "packaged into a record" is a PATTERN, not eight separate errors

Three of the remaining error groups are the same shape, and until this round they were being handled one at a
time. 26.2 packages a tuple of raw fields into a record with a getter; 1.21.1 keeps the raw fields.

    KeyEvent         26.2  InputEvent$Key(KeyEvent, int) + getKeyEvent(): KeyEvent
                     1.21.1  InputEvent$Key(int, int, int, int), no getKeyEvent
                     kernel calls it five times, in a file with nine unique rules

    MouseButtonInfo  26.2  InputEvent$MouseButton$Pre(MouseButtonInfo, int) + getMouseButtonEvent()
                     1.21.1  InputEvent$MouseButton$Pre(int, int, int), no getMouseButtonEvent
                     kernel calls it once, to build a Forge event

    VanillaResult    26.2  AnvilUpdateEvent.getVanillaResult(): VanillaResult
                     1.21.1  AnvilUpdateEvent has no such thing, and no record of what it was built with
                     kernel calls it three times

THE FIRST TWO ARE FIXABLE AND THE SAME WAY: rebuild the caller's arguments from the raw fields instead of passing
the packaged object. getKeyEvent() becomes getKey(), getScanCode(), getAction(), getModifiers(); getMouseButtonEvent()
becomes getButton() and getModifiers(). Six errors, one shape, one method of repair.

THE THIRD IS NOT FIXABLE THE SAME WAY, and the reason is the useful part: VanillaResult's job on 26.2 is to
remember what the event looked like BEFORE anything modified it. 1.21.1's event does not remember that, so a compat
VanillaResult could only read the CURRENT values and the kernel's untouched comparison at :254 would become a
comparison of the event with itself -- always true, and silently so. So "packaged record" splits into two classes
depending on whether the record carries data the line still HAS (recoverable) or data the line never KEPT
(not recoverable).

Six of the remaining errors are in the recoverable class and have a settled repair. Three are in the
non-recoverable class and need the caller to snapshot values before the event is posted -- which is a code change
in a 13-rule file, not a substitution.

Not done: fixing the six needs two overlays (nine unique rules and two), and this session no longer has the room
to complete one and verify it on both lines. The specification for the larger one is already in this document.

---

# Rounds 330-332: the KernelGameClientEvents overlay was attempted twice and failed twice. READ THIS BEFORE RETRYING IT.

An earlier section of this document says the overlay spec for this file is "completely determined". That claim is
WRONG in a way worth spelling out, because following it wastes two rounds. Measured:

    baseline (no overlay)                             TOTAL 54   axis2 29
    attempt 1: three ints = key, scanCode, ACTION     TOTAL 65   axis2 40   (+11)
    attempt 2: three ints = key, scanCode, MODIFIERS  TOTAL 61   axis2 36   (+7)
    both reverted                                     TOTAL 54   axis2 29

So the third int is not the problem -- changing it moved the damage by four points and left it clearly negative.

WHAT IS VERIFIED CORRECT, and therefore is not the cause:

    1.21.1 ForgeEventFactoryClient.onScreenKeyPressedPre(Screen, int, int, int)
    26.2   ForgeEventFactoryClient.onScreenKeyPressedPre(Screen, KeyEvent)
    1.21.1 InputEvent$Key carries getKey/getScanCode/getAction/getModifiers
    26.2   InputEvent$Key adds getKeyEvent(): KeyEvent
    the eight .BUS rows DO target net.minecraftforge.common.MinecraftForge.EVENT_BUS (read from the table)
    the table's getDeltaTracker row DOES target getTimer (read from the table)

So every individual substitution matched what the table and javap say, the overlay rewrote all of them, all nine
rows were deleted in the same call, the orphan guard passed -- and the result was still eleven errors worse than
simply leaving the file alone.

WHAT I DID NOT DIAGNOSE, stated plainly rather than guessed at: the cause. The plausible candidates I could not
distinguish are (a) the eight .BUS targets are Forge buses and the NeoForge event types do not line up on this
line the way the row implies, (b) Minecraft#getTimer's return type differs enough that the four downstream uses of
it break, (c) the four onScreenKey* callbacks have changed return-value semantics. Each is a guess, and two rounds
of guessing already cost two reverts.

WHY THIS FILE IS THE HARD CASE, structurally: it hits NINE rows, every one of them unique to it. So overlaying it
means hand-reproducing nine rule applications, and a single wrong reproduction is invisible until the measurement
comes back worse. Every overlay that has succeeded in this run touched two or three rules. The ones at nine were
skipped for exactly this reason, and now there is evidence rather than suspicion.

VERDICT: not to be hand-overlaid. Five errors stay. If it is ever ported, the route is to make the kernel call a
COMPAT class instead of the Forge class, so the table keeps doing the renaming and the overlay is never needed.

---

# Rounds 335-337: three kinds of "not a rename", and a scope check that came back small

## Round 335 gave one error back

KernelForgeConditions: 1.21.1's ICondition.IContext declares getAllTags(ResourceKey) abstract and 26.2's does not,
so the kernel's anonymous context left an abstract method unimplemented. Adding it in the overlay, delegating to
the object it wraps, took 54 -> 53. It had to be an overlay because 26.2's IContext has no getAllTags, so the
shared source cannot carry it.

That overlay touched two rules and NEITHER was unique to the file, so it needed zero row deletions and zero
hand-translated rule applications. KernelGameClientEvents, by contrast, touches nine rules and all nine are
unique. Three data points now say the same thing: overlay cost tracks the number of UNIQUE rows, because a unique
row must be deleted and its effect then hand-reproduced, and one wrong reproduction is invisible until the
measurement comes back worse.

## Two more probes, two more "not a rename"

KernelLootBridge -- TagLoader
    26.2    static loadTagsForRegistry(ResourceManager, WritableRegistry<T>) that fills the registry
    1.21.1  no such static at all; TagLoader is instance-based (load / build / loadAndBuild) and 1.21.1 does this
            inside ReloadableServerRegistries instead
    Using loadAndBuild and writing the map into the registry yourself is NOT equivalent: the vanilla path also does
    dependency ordering, cross-tag resolution and pending handling. So this needs a decision about where the hook
    goes, not a substitution.

KernelSpawnerFinalize -- ValueInput
    26.2    net.minecraft.world.level.storage.ValueInput / ValueOutput, with TagValueInput and eight inner types
    1.21.1  neither exists; 1.21.1 still passes CompoundTag around
    And the kernel's signature takes ValueInput as a parameter CAPTURED FROM the vanilla call, which on 1.21.1
    receives a CompoundTag, so a compat class would not just stand in for the type -- it would change the type of
    a value the kernel captures, which is exactly the failure its own message at :55 describes.

## A scope check, because ValueInput looked like it could be everywhere

It is not. ValueInput appears in exactly two files under src/runtime/java -- KernelSpawnerFinalize (7 mentions)
and KernelForgeCapabilities (2, and that file has no errors, so they are prose). Of the 21 files that still carry
errors, KernelSpawnerFinalize is the only one that mentions it. So the data-API gap is two errors in one file,
not a layer-wide problem. Checking this before assuming was worth one probe.

## Three kinds of "not a rename", now distinguished

    shape not recoverable          Hud, ListenerKey, the two Vanilla*Listeners
    extension point differs        KernelFluidBreathing (eye vs body, 3-arg vs 2-arg predicate), KernelLootBridge
    whole layer differs            KernelSpawnerFinalize (ValueInput vs CompoundTag)

The third kind was not distinguished earlier in this document, and it matters because the first two are one type
or one method while the third is an abstraction the kernel is written against throughout.

# Rounds 340-342: one more error back, and a limit of overlays that is worth knowing

## Round 340: the same IContext fix in a second file, 53 -> 52

KernelModifiableDataViews had the identical problem as KernelForgeConditions -- an anonymous
net.minecraftforge...ICondition.IContext implementing only getTag, which 1.21.1's interface makes insufficient.
The overlay adds getAllTags delegating to the wrapped source. That file hits ZERO rename rules, so the overlay
needed no rule translation and no row deletion.

It took three attempts because I inserted with regex re-concatenation instead of by line. The first emitted
"}@Override" on one line; the second left a stray closing brace because the regex ended at the method's own
brace. Both manifested as eight syntax errors, and both times my grep for TOTAL found nothing.

What broke the loop was the harness saying, verbatim:

    REFUSING TO SPLIT ... 8 error blocks but no `symbol:` line and no `does not exist` in the whole transcript

That sentence distinguishes a SYNTAX error from a SYMBOL error, and it is the only place in this tooling that
says so. Reading it as "look at the braces, not the types" saved a round. Every future failure with this shape
should be read that way first.

Durable lesson: insert into brace-structured text by LINE, never by re-concatenating a regex match. Four of this
run's PowerShell-string mistakes have been of the re-concatenation kind and none of the line-based ones failed.

## Round 342: KernelGameBlockEvents cannot be fixed in an overlay, and the reason is structural

The remaining error is real and diagnosable. 26.2 names NeoForge's event BreakBlockEvent (a top-level class);
1.21.1 names it net.neoforged.neoforge.event.level.BlockEvent$BreakEvent. The table rewrites BreakBlockEvent to
the bare simple name BlockEvent.BreakEvent -- and the kernel ALSO uses MinecraftForge's
BlockEvent$BreakEvent, written out literally, in the same file. The two have the same simple name on 1.21.1 and
different packages, so the bare rewrite resolves against whichever BlockEvent is imported, which is Forge's.

So the rewrite is right in direction and wrong in spelling, and there are two obvious repairs:

    change the row's TARGET to the NeoForge fully-qualified name    -> the import line becomes a nested-class
        import, and then the file's LITERAL BlockEvent.BreakEvent uses bind to NeoForge's instead of Forge's, so
        the Forge side breaks in exchange
    change the shared source to spell both sides out                -> touches src/runtime/java and therefore the
        26.2 build, which is off limits without a full 26.2 re-verification

And it cannot be repaired in the overlay at all, for a reason that generalises:

    AN OVERLAY CONTAINS POST-RULE TEXT. When a rule maps A to B and the file already contains B literally, the
    overlay can no longer tell which B came from A. Here BreakBlockEvent (NeoForge) and BlockEvent.BreakEvent
    (Forge) are BYTE-IDENTICAL after rewriting.

I tried qualifying both sides and measured: still 52, with the Forge side now wrong as well. Reverted.

So this is a fifth kind of defect, distinct from the four already recorded: not a name that is mutually exclusive,
not a receiver whose level differs, not a redundant row, not a row wrong in one place and right in another -- but a
row whose TARGET collides with a name the file already uses. It is unfixable from the overlay layer by
construction, and it stays as one error.

---

# The user's round-353 decision: drop what has no counterpart, bridge what is only a name

The instruction was: drop what genuinely has no match, and explicitly list which mods or which features in which
mods become unusable; where it is only an API name difference, try to bridge it.

That re-opening changed the picture substantially. Several of the "no match" verdicts recorded earlier in this
document were **wrong**, and wrong in a specific way: they were made about the TYPE, not about the MEMBER the
kernel actually calls. `net.minecraft.core.TypedInstance` really does not exist on 1.21.1 -- but the kernel only
used it for `is(Item)`, which `ItemStack` has on both lines. The class was absent; the capability was not.

Result of that pass: **50 -> 22**.

## Bridged (the member exists on both lines, under a different name or arity)

| site | 26.2 | 1.21.1 | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KernelShears` | `((TypedInstance) stack).is(item)` | `((ItemStack) stack).is((Item) item)` | `ItemStack#is(Item)` public on both |
| `KernelItemTooltips` | `ItemTooltipEvent(...5 args, TooltipDisplay)` | same, five arguments | javap, both lines |
| `KernelFinalizeSpawn` | `FinalizeSpawn#getSpawnReason()` | `getSpawnType(): MobSpawnType` | javap, both lines |
| `KernelForgeSpawnPlacements` | `SpawnPlacementRegisterEvent.BUS` | `FMLJavaModLoadingContext.get().getModEventBus().post(...)` | event is `IModBusEvent` on this line |
| `KernelForgeReload` | `EventHooks.onResourceReload(3 args)` | two arguments | javap, both lines |
| `KernelGameClientEvents` | `InputEvent$Key#getKeyEvent()` | `getKey()/getScanCode()/getAction()/getModifiers()` | javap, both lines |
| `KernelGameClientEvents` | `ScreenEvent.Key*#getKeyEvent()` | `getKeyCode()/getScanCode()/getModifiers()` | javap, both lines -- note `getKeyCode`, not `getKey` |
| `KernelGameClientEvents` | `InputEvent$MouseButton#getMouseButtonInfo()` | `getButton()/getAction()/getModifiers()` | javap, both lines |
| `KernelGameClientEvents` | Forge `ViewportEvent.RenderFog(type, camera, tick, FogData, colour)` | `(mode, type, camera, near, far, tick, shape)` | reassembled from the members this line has |
| `KernelGameClientEvents` | Forge `ComputeFovModifierEvent(player, fov, scale)` | `(player, fov)` -- no scale on this line at all | javap, both lines |
| `KernelModelFormats` | `cuboid.CuboidModel` | `renderer.block.model.BlockModel` | both `implements UnbakedModel` |

## Dropped, with what it costs

- **HUD contextual-info bar.** `net.minecraft.client.gui.Hud` is a NEW 26.x class, not a rename of `Gui`:
  `Minecraft.gui` is `net.minecraft.client.gui.Gui` on both lines, and 26.2 is the one that gained a `hud` field.
  Impact: a mixin that asks the kernel for the HUD's contextual-info state gets `null` on 1.21.1.
- **NeoForge's native reload-listener names.** `VanillaClientListeners` / `VanillaServerListeners` / `ListenerKey`
  are 26.x-only. Impact: every reload listener is treated as "foreign" and given a synthesised key. Listeners
  still run and sort; they just do not carry the name NeoForge's own dependency edges were written against.
- **MinecraftForge's `AddReloadListenerEvent` on the server reload.** `ForgeEventFactory.onResourceReload` on this
  line wants a `RegistryAccess` where 26.2 wants the listener list, and `ReloadableServerResources` exposes only
  `getRegistryLookup(): HolderLookup$Provider`. Impact: **a traditional-Forge mod that registers a JSON data loader
  from `AddReloadListenerEvent` has no listener registered on 1.21.1, so its custom data folder loads nothing.**
- **MinecraftForge's `MobSpawnEvent.FinalizeSpawn` from the `BaseSpawner` path.** The eight-argument overload only
  existed to accept a `ValueInput`, which is 26.x (`ValueInput`/`ValueOutput` replaced `CompoundTag`). Impact: a
  Forge listener cannot veto or rewrite a spawner's spawn data.
- **MinecraftForge fluid client-extension models.** `IClientFluidTypeExtensions#getModel` is a 26.x hook, and 1.21.1
  still draws fluids through `LiquidBlockRenderer` with no per-type model. Impact: a Forge mod's custom fluid
  renderer is not consulted; NeoForge's model is used.
- **Fabric non-drowning refills when the native query was skipped.** `LivingEntity#getFluidInteraction()` is 26.x.
  `isInFluidType(BiPredicate)` exists here but takes two arguments where `InFluidPredicate` takes three, and asks
  about the BODY where `isEyeInFluidMatching` asks about the EYE — not interchangeable for a drowning test.
  Impact: no refill is granted in that path rather than granting one on a wrong check.
- **`TagLoader.loadTagsForRegistry` bridge.** No static entry point on this line; the method now throws rather than
  silently skipping the load.

## Done and measured

    1.21.1 compileRuntimeJava     BUILD SUCCESSFUL    327 classes    0 source errors
    1.21.1 assemble               BUILD SUCCESSFUL    jars written
    26.2   compileRuntimeJava     BUILD SUCCESSFUL    295 classes    (= documented baseline)
    26.2   test                   5635 / 78 failures / 0 errors / 846 skipped
        and the 78 are exactly 76 Windows TempDir IOException plus precisely the two known real failures
        (ForgeRegistrationA2ContractTest.clientGateSeedsF6AndBothScriptsParse,
         KernelRuntimeClassesTest.everyGameSideClassTheBootNamesIsInTheRegistry)

The 26.2 numbers are identical to the contract's, so the hard constraint is satisfied by measurement.

## Artifacts, now line-distinct

    forbric-kernel-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT-1.21.1.jar            7,348,757
    forbric-kernel-runtime-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT-1.21.1.jar      626,306   (34 net/forbric/compat classes)
    forbric-kernel-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar                   7,346,635
    forbric-kernel-runtime-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar             600,321   (0 compat classes)

Before archiveClassifier was set, `assemble` on either line overwrote the other's jar under one name -- measured by
the runtime jar's size changing from 626,306 to 600,321. Same failure shape as the unversioned forge-runtime.jar
this project already has a DANGER note about.

## NOT DONE: install and launch. This is a missing input, not a missing step.

Nothing in this repository is a launchable Minecraft installation:

  forbric-kernel/.dev/staged-1211/run/    only build carriers -- merged-base, forge-runtime, neoforge-runtime.
                                          It DOES hold both patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar and the 26.2 one, plus
                                          forge-runtime-interop.jar, so it can feed a launcher's MERGED/STAGE vars.
  forbric-kernel/.dev-test/client/        mods/ plus two test-set JSONs. No libraries, assets or natives.
  .firstgen-backup/run/                   launch scripts only.
  forbric-kernel/run/                     launch-kernel-client.sh and ~30 canary scripts; no libraries/assets.

launch-kernel-client.sh needs MC_DIR (default is a macOS path, so it will not resolve here) with
versions/26.2/26.2-natives and assets/, and it hardcodes patched-mc-merged-26.2.jar though MERGED/FORGE_RT/NEO_RT/
STAGE are all overridable. It also re-runs `gradlew jar` before launching, which on the default line would rebuild
the 26.2 jar.

So launching needs either a path to an existing Minecraft installation, or permission to create one by downloading
the version manifest, client jar, libraries, natives and asset index. Neither has been provided, and
`tools/dev.py prepare` is not an option -- it writes the unversioned forge-runtime.jar noted above.

## Two independent blockers, stated plainly

  1. LAUNCH   blocked on a Minecraft installation directory. Not attempted, so nothing is claimed about
              bootability, mixin application, or whether any mod loads.
  2. PUSH     nine commits are local. `git push` fails with
                schannel: failed to receive handshake, SSL/TLS connection failed
              and with the openssl backend it demands credentials on /dev/tty, which this shell has not got.
              Pushes through 49f544d did succeed earlier, so the remote and the branch are fine; what changed is
              either the cached credential or the network path. Not worked around by rewriting history or by
              force-pushing anything.

## Addendum: a Minecraft installation DOES exist here, but neither target line

    C:\Users\Administrator\AppData\Roaming\.minecraft
      versions   1.21.10  1.21.11  25w44a  26.1.1  26w14a  LabyMod-4-1.20.1  (+ jre_manifest.json,
                 version_manifest_v2.json)
      libraries  present
      assets     present
      1.21.1     ABSENT
      26.2       ABSENT

So the earlier statement needs correcting: the blocker is not "no installation", it is "no installation OF
1.21.1 or 26.2". What exists is exactly the two things needed as anchors -- a libraries tree and an assets tree --
plus a version manifest, which is where the 1.21.1 entry would be read from.

To reach a launch on 1.21.1 four artifacts have to be fetched that are not present:

    the 1.21.1 version JSON          (the manifest is already here: version_manifest_v2.json)
    the 1.21.1 client jar
    the 1.21.1 library set           (many jars will already resolve from the present libraries tree)
    the 1.21.1 LWJGL natives         (no *-natives directory exists under any version here)

Asset objects are content-addressed and shared, so the present assets tree very likely already covers 1.21.1 once
its index is fetched -- that one is a cheap check, not a download of the whole asset set.

tools/dev.py already contains stage_minecraft / libraries / stage_assets / fetch, which is the code that does
exactly this. It is NOT used through `prepare`, which is forbidden here because it writes the unversioned
forge-runtime.jar. Whether the helper functions can be driven directly, without going through prepare, has not been
investigated.

This is a download authorisation question, not an engineering one, and it is left to the user rather than being
started unasked.

## What a launch would actually cost, measured rather than guessed

Fetched exactly one file, into this repository's own build directory rather than into the user's Minecraft
installation: forbric-kernel/build/x11/mc-1.21.1/versions/1.21.1/1.21.1.json (38,408 bytes), from the URL the
local manifest already lists:

    https://piston-meta.mojang.com/v1/packages/cbf5dea4844dc07cd8981fddb6333de5c79c5fbd/1.21.1.json

It parses, and it settles the question:

    1.21.1          id 1.21.1, mainClass net.minecraft.client.main.Main, assetIndex 17
    libraries       97
    downloads.client  26,836,906 bytes
    downloads.server  51,627,615 bytes

Checked all 97 library artifacts against the user's libraries tree:

    43 present
    54 MISSING, all version-pinned and all incompatible with what is installed:
       gson 2.10.1 (installed 2.11-era), guava 32.1.2-jre (installed 33.3.1-jre),
       authlib 6.0.54 (installed 7.0.61), datafixerupper 8.0.16 (installed 9.0.19),
       netty 4.1.97.Final (installed newer), icu4j 73.2, oshi-core 6.4.10, text2speech 1.17.9, ...

So a launch is not "one small metadata file". It needs the 54 missing library jars, and for a client also the
natives. Every one of those is a download.

NOTE ON 26.2: it is NOT in the local manifest at all -- the manifest lists 1.21.11 and 26.1.1, not 26.2 -- so the
26.2 line cannot be launched from this installation by any route. Only 1.21.1 can, and only after those downloads.

Nothing has been downloaded except that one JSON, and it was written under build/x11, so the user's .minecraft is
untouched. Whether to fetch ~54 jars is the user's call, not one to take on their behalf.

# Launch, measured on both lines

## 26.2: reaches "Done"

    Done (1.913s)! For help, type "help"
    [Forbric/EventMux] bridged 20 ServerTickEvent.Pre / 20 Post to MinecraftForge
    Successfully initialized permission handler neoforge:default_handler
    Successfully initialized permission handler forge:default_handler
    [Forbric/Tick] 4900 tick(s): mean 50.00ms, at twice the budget or worse 0 (0.0%)

Both game-event families running in one dedicated server, tick loop holding 50 ms. The launch path is proven end to
end, which is what makes the 1.21.1 result below interpretable.

## 1.21.1: four launcher defects, then one descriptor, then a class-init NPE

Four defects were in the LAUNCHER, not the kernel, and each was found by running it:

  1. classpath length    java "Argument list too long" -> the classpath is handed over in an @argfile
  2. path separator      ':' was hardcoded where Gradle and python emit os.pathsep. On Windows the first entry
                         became one bogus path and java answered ClassNotFoundException for a class that was on
                         the path. 114 entries in the argfile, mixed ':' and ';'.
  3. --libraryPath       the same ':' in a second place, surfacing as InvalidPathException "Illegal char <:>"
                         from KernelBoot.libraryJars
  4. jar selection       `ls | head -1` would have launched the other line's jar; and the launcher's own
                         `gradlew jar` carried no -Pforbric.mcLine, so it compiled 26.2 sources against a 1.21.1
                         classpath (100 errors). MC_LINE now parameterises versions/<line>/<line>.json, the
                         merged-base name and the gradle property.

Then one KERNEL defect, measured rather than guessed:

    1.21.1  ServerModLoader.load()V     and MinecraftServer.Main javap -c, offset 453,
                                        invokestatic .../ServerModLoader.load:()V
    26.2    ServerModLoader.load(Z)V

The call site exists on both lines; only the descriptor moved, so the (Z)V trigger matched nothing and the kernel
refused to boot. SERVER_TRIGGERS gained a NeoForge ()V row pointing at the no-arg hook that already existed.

WITH THAT, 1.21.1 RUNS REAL WORK. The log shows the capability composition, the radians/degrees constant repair,
the duplicated-lambda cleanup (4 on Entity, 3 on LivingEntity, 1 on Level, 1 on RegistryFriendlyByteBuf), the
registry alias repair, the fluid-type contract repair, and the creative-tab / portal / damage-seam anchor passes all
executing on this line.

## Where it stops now, and why the next step is not guessable from here

    java.lang.reflect.InvocationTargetException
    Caused by: java.lang.ExceptionInInitializerError
    Caused by: NullPointerException: Cannot invoke "java.util.Set.add(Object)"
                because "net.minecraft.core.MappedRegistry.KNOWN" is null

KNOWN EXISTS ON BOTH LINES -- checked, not assumed:

    1.21.1  private static final Set<ResourceLocation> KNOWN
    26.2    private static final Set<Identifier>       KNOWN

So this is not a missing field and not a rename. It is a class-initialisation ordering failure: an
ExceptionInInitializerError with the NPE underneath means some class's static initialiser reads KNOWN before
MappedRegistry's own <clinit> has assigned it -- the classic circular-init shape. Finding WHICH initialiser is the
work, and it needs the full stack trace, which the log truncated to five frames.

The 26.2 relaunch has NOT been re-run since the SERVER_TRIGGERS row was added, so "26.2 still reaches Done" is
true of the previous commit, not of the current tree.

## The MappedRegistry.KNOWN failure, traced to the frame

Full trace, from the run's logs/latest.log (the console had truncated it to five frames):

    FireBlock.<clinit>                      FireBlock.java:36
      BlockStateProperties.<clinit>         BlockStateProperties.java:127
        EnumProperty.create                  EnumProperty.java:67 / :71  (getEnumConstants)
          NoteBlockInstrument.<clinit>      NoteBlockInstrument.java:9
            SoundEvents.<clinit>            SoundEvents.java:11
              SoundEvents.register           SoundEvents.java:1622 -> 1626 -> 1638
                Registry.register            Registry.java:119 / :123
                  NamespacedWrapper.register NamespacedWrapper.java:76
                    MappedRegistry.markKnown MappedRegistry.java:458
                      NPE: Set.add on null KNOWN

So this is NOT a circular initialiser and NOT a missing field. MappedRegistry IS initialised -- its <clinit> must
run before any subclass's, and NamespacedWrapper goes through it. What the trace shows is a Forge-side registrar
(Net.minecraftforge.registries.NamespacedWrapper) calling MappedRegistry.markKnown, a member the MERGED base carries,
and KNOWN being null at that moment.

That points at the merge rather than at 1.21.1: KNOWN and markKnown are not both present in vanilla 1.21.1's
MappedRegistry -- they come from one of the two modding families -- and the merged <clinit> that should assign KNOWN
is not doing so on this line. The same class of defect the kernel already repairs elsewhere, and it names them when
it does:

    [Forbric/MergedBaseCompat] CompoundTag.builder() -- 1 method added
    [Forbric/Capabilities] LivingEntity.<init> assigns handlers again (1 constructor)
    [Forbric/MergedBaseCompat] Entity turns radians into degrees by vanilla's folded constant again

So the next step is a MergedBaseCompat repair of the same shape: make MappedRegistry.KNOWN non-null before any
registration reaches markKnown. That is a targeted compat repair, not a laundry list.

WHY THIS IS INTERPRETABLE AT ALL: the same kernel, injectors and launcher reach "Done (1.913s)!" on 26.2. The 1.21.1
failure is therefore a specific merged-base difference, not a broken architecture.

Everything above the failure also ran on 1.21.1 and said what it did -- capability composition (+8 methods on
Entity), the duplicated-lambda cleanup (4 on Entity, 3 on LivingEntity, 1 on Level, 1 on RegistryFriendlyByteBuf),
registry alias repair, registries forced public, fluid-type contracts repaired (2), attribute validation held --
plus TEN anchor skips each naming its own cost (untracked fluid eyes, forge damage seams x2, vanilla damage read,
forge part tracking, registry alias parity, forge creative tabs, portal spawn, EntityType.finalizeSpawn).

## MappedRegistry.KNOWN: the merge kept the field and the reader but dropped the initialiser

PROOF, from javap -c on both staged merged bases. Nothing here is inferred.

26.2 MappedRegistry.<clinit>:

    putstatic LOGGER     (org.apache.logging.log4j.Logger)
    putstatic MARKER     (org.apache.logging.log4j.Marker)
    new       java/util/LinkedHashSet
    putstatic KNOWN
    return

1.21.1 merged MappedRegistry.<clinit>:

    putstatic LOGGER     (org.slf4j.Logger)
    return

and inside the same class file:

    :31    private static final Set<ResourceLocation> KNOWN       <- declared
    :1275  getstatic KNOWN                                        <- read
    :1281  getstatic KNOWN                                        <- read
           no putstatic anywhere                                  <- never assigned

So the byte merge took 1.21.1's <clinit> (which has no KNOWN, because vanilla/1.21.1 never had that field) while
keeping 26.2's field declaration and 26.2's markKnown(), which dereferences it. KNOWN is therefore null forever and
markKnown throws on the first registration that reaches it:

    NamespacedWrapper.register (NamespacedWrapper.java:76)
      MappedRegistry.markKnown (MappedRegistry.java:458)
        Set.add on null KNOWN
          first reached from SoundEvents.<clinit> during Bootstrap.bootStrap

THE REPAIR IS EXACTLY ONE STATEMENT, and 26.2's own <clinit> is the template: assign KNOWN to a fresh
LinkedHashSet before any registration runs. It belongs in the MerkedBaseCompat family beside the three repairs that
already exist for the same class of defect -- CompoundTag.builder() gaining the one method the merge dropped,
LivingEntity.<init> regaining its handler assignments, Entity regaining vanilla's folded radians/degrees constant.
Each of those was a field-or-body that survived in one half of the merge and not the other; this is the same shape
with a static initialiser.

NOT ATTEMPTED: writing that repair. It needs to be written, then the 1.21.1 server relaunched to see the next
obstacle, then 26.2 relaunched to prove it did not regress -- three runs, and this session is out of room for them.
A repair written and not run would be worth less than this proof.

## How to write the KNOWN repair: the three registration points, read out of the file

Writing it without these would have meant guessing at the framework, so they are recorded here rather than left to
be rediscovered. All in src/main/java/net/forbric/kernel/transform/ForbricMergedBaseCompatTransformer.java:

  1. line 96   REPAIRS -- a List<String> of repair names. convertRadiansWithVanillasFoldedConstant and
               restoreDoublePrecisionToTheRandomSources are entries 11 and 12 of it. The new name goes here or the
               repair exists and is never called.

  2. line ~140  the claim/anchor registration, of the shape
                   out.add(fixed("convertRadiansWithVanillasFoldedConstant",
                                  "net/minecraft/world/entity/Entity", ...));
               This is what declares WHICH class the repair is contracted to touch. For the KNOWN repair the class is
               net/minecraft/core/MappedRegistry.

  3. line 291-302  the dispatch inside the transform, one line per repair, of the shape
                   changed |= claim(reporter, "convertRadiansWithVanillasFoldedConstant",
                                    convertRadiansWithVanillasFoldedConstant(node));

And the method shape, verbatim from the radians repair at 1908-1936:

    private static boolean convertRadiansWithVanillasFoldedConstant(ClassNode node) {
        if (!node.name.startsWith("net/minecraft/")) return false;   // cheap early out
        ... mutate node.methods ...
        if (folded == 0) return false;                              // nothing found => not a claim
        ForbricLog.info("[Forbric/MergedBaseCompat] %s ...", node.name.replace('/', '.'), folded);
        return true;                                                // only true when it changed something
    }

## What the KNOWN repair should do, precisely

Target: net/minecraft/core/MappedRegistry.
Find the MethodNode named "<clinit>" with descriptor "()V".
Scan it for a PUTSTATIC whose field owner is this class and whose name is "KNOWN".
If one exists, return false -- the merge is fine and there is nothing to do.
If none exists, PREPEND (so it runs before any other static initialisation in that class):

    NEW      java/util/LinkedHashSet
    DUP
    INVOKESPECIAL java/util/LinkedHashSet.<init>()V
    PUTSTATIC net/minecraft/core/MappedRegistry.KNOWN:Ljava/util/Set;

then log and return true. LinkedHashSet for the same reason 26.2 uses it: the order registrations arrive in is the
order something may later report, so a HashSet would be a silent behavioural difference rather than just a container
choice.

Verified on the two staged bases with javap, so this is a description of the actual bytecode and not a hypothesis:

    26.2    <clinit>: putstatic LOGGER; putstatic MARKER; new LinkedHashSet; putstatic KNOWN; return
    1.21.1  <clinit>: putstatic LOGGER; return
            and the class file declares KNOWN plus two getstatic reads, with no putstatic anywhere

NOT WRITTEN: the method itself. It needs the three registrations above plus a build plus a 1.21.1 relaunch plus a 26.2
relaunch to prove no regression, and this session ran out of room before that. The proof above is worth more than a
half-written repair, and the three registration points are recorded precisely so the next pass does not have to
rediscover the framework.

## The next obstacle: a bare class name where a descriptor belongs

With the KNOWN repair in place the boot reaches:

    ClassFormatError: Method "<init>" in class net.minecraft/world/level/chunk/LevelChunk
      has illegal signature "(Ljava/lang/Class;net/minecraftforge/common/capabilities/ICapabilityProviderImpl;)V"

The second parameter is a BARE fully-qualified name where a field descriptor must be `L...;`. That is a substitution
that replaced a generic variable with a class name but did not wrap it.

WHAT WAS RULED OUT FIRST, by measurement rather than by reading:

  * Both lines' LevelChunk constructors are byte-identical, so nothing in the merged base introduced this:
        1.21.1 and 26.2 both have
          (Level, ChunkPos)
          (Level, ChunkPos, UpgradeData, LevelChunkTicks, LevelChunkTicks, long, LevelChunkSection[],
           LevelChunk$PostLoadProcessor, BlendingData)
          (ServerLevel, ProtoChunk, LevelChunk$PostLoadProcessor)
  * The E5 repair in ForgeCapabilityCompositionTransformer (:435-487) is NOT the source. Its constants are right
        LEVEL_CHUNK            = net/minecraft/world/level/chunk/LevelChunk
        LEVEL_CHUNKS_PROVIDER  = net/minecraftforge/common/capabilities/CapabilityProvider$AsField$LevelChunks
    and the two descriptors it writes are built with the L...; wrapper:
        :470  INVOKESPECIAL <provider>.<init> "(L" + LEVEL_CHUNK + ";)V"      -> well formed
        :471  PUTFIELD capProvider "L" + LEVEL_CHUNKS_PROVIDER + ";"
        :477  GETFIELD capProvider "L" + LEVEL_CHUNKS_PROVIDER + ";"

WHERE IT ACTUALLY POINTS. The class's own comment at :115 records that the state carrier's AsField carries
`<init>(Class, B)` and that 26.2's is a base with ... . The malformed descriptor is `(Ljava/lang/Class;` followed by
the concrete `ICapabilityProviderImpl` -- exactly `(Class, B)` with B substituted by a class name and NOT wrapped in
L...;. So the defect is in whatever substitutes B, in ForgeCapabilityCompositionTransformer or ForgeCapabilityProtocol,
not in E5.

NEXT ACTION, precisely: find where that substitution is written and wrap the name -- `L` + internalName + `;`. Then
rebuild, relaunch 1.21.1, confirm the ClassFormatError is gone, and finally relaunch 26.2 to confirm the KNOWN repair
and this one left that line alone.

NOT ATTEMPTED THIS ROUND: the substitution fix. The diagnosis above narrows it to two files and to `<init>(Class, B)`;
the write and its three verification runs did not fit.

## Fixed and verified: the malformed LevelChunk constructor

ForgeCapabilityCompositionTransformer:692 built a descriptor by concatenating an internal name where a field
descriptor belonged:

    "(Ljava/lang/Class;" + PROVIDER_IMPL + ";)V"

PROVIDER_IMPL is net/minecraftforge/common/capabilities/ICapabilityProviderImpl with no L...; wrapper, so the string
came out as

    (Ljava/lang/Class;net/minecraftforge/common/capabilities/ICapabilityProviderImpl;)V

and the verifier rejected it the first time a LevelChunk was constructed. Every other descriptor in that file already
wraps it; this one did not. Fixed to "(Ljava/lang/Class;L" + PROVIDER_IMPL + ";)V".

MEASURED: the ClassFormatError is gone from the 1.21.1 run. Boot now reaches a different obstacle.
