## 1.21.1 is now stopped by the kernel refusing to guess, which is the right place to stop

    LinkageError: Unresolved stateful ancestor composition for net/minecraft/world/level/entity/boss/EnderDragonPart
      retained net/neoforged/neoforge/entity/PartEntity, removed net/minecraftforge/entity/PartEntity
      No protocol proved the final definition; rebuild the base or register an AncestorComposition

EnderDragonPart cannot inherit both families' PartEntity, and the kernel will not pick one silently. To clear it,
either rebuild the merged base (out of scope -- that path is the forbidden prepare) or register an
net.forbric.api.AncestorComposition.

WHAT THAT TAKES, read from the API rather than guessed:

    @FunctionalInterface
    public interface AncestorComposition {
        record Requirement(String owner, String retainedSuperclass, String sourceSuperclass) { }
        boolean proves(Requirement requirement, byte[] finalDefinition, Function<String, byte[]> resources);
    }

and it is registered with loader.registerAncestorComposition(composition), then registered on the transform chain
(KernelBoot:517-518 shows the existing capability composition doing both). A proof is only honest if it
demonstrates that the final definition and the real source state protocol are both accounted for -- so it needs
both families' PartEntity shapes read first, which is why it was not written blind at the end of this round.

NOT ATTEMPTED: that proof. Writing one without reading both PartEntity classes would be exactly the kind of guess the
kernel is right to refuse.

## The EnderDragonPart composition: the requirement cannot be edited, and the proof is legitimate

WHERE THE REQUIREMENT COMES FROM, read out of the jar rather than assumed:

    unzip -p patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar META-INF/forbric/required-ancestor-compositions.tsv

    net/minecraft/world/entity/boss/EnderDragonPart
        net/neoforged/neoforge/entity/PartEntity
        net/minecraftforge/entity/PartEntity

The file lives INSIDE the merged base jar, written by MergedBaseBuilder, and the whole base is 5 rows. So it cannot be
edited to remove the requirement without rebuilding the base -- the forbidden path. The only route is to register an
AncestorComposition, which is what the kernel's message offers as its second option.

THE PROOF IS LEGITIMATE, not a rubber stamp, because the two ancestors are the same class shape. javap on both:

    net.neoforged.neoforge.entity.PartEntity<T extends Entity>
        private final T parent;
        public PartEntity(T);
        public T getParent();
        public Packet<ClientGamePacketListener> getAddEntityPacket(ServerEntity);

    net.minecraftforge.entity.PartEntity<T extends Entity>
        private final T parent;
        public PartEntity(T);
        public T getParent();
        public Packet<ClientGamePacketListener> getAddEntityPacket(ServerEntity);

Identical member for member, and EnderDragonPart on 1.21.1 extends the neoforged one and nothing else. So the merge
did not lose state by dropping the forge parent: the retained parent carries the same state protocol, and a proof can
say exactly that -- read both definitions through the resolver, assert that the state-bearing members (the parent
field, the T-taking constructor, getParent, getAddEntityPacket) match, and return true only then.

WHAT IT TAKES TO WRITE:

    @FunctionalInterface
    public interface AncestorComposition {
        record Requirement(String owner, String retainedSuperclass, String sourceSuperclass) { }
        boolean proves(Requirement requirement, byte[] finalDefinition, Function<String, byte[]> resources);
    }

registered with loader.registerAncestorComposition(composition) AND chain.register(TransformPhase.COREMOD,
composition) -- KernelBoot:517-518 shows the capability composition doing both, and
ForgeCapabilityCompositionTransformer:132 shows the proves() call it answers. RequiredAncestorCompositions:20 is the
only caller: it asks every registered proof in turn and refuses to define the class if none returns true.

WHY IT WAS NOT WRITTEN: it needs a new class, a registration in KernelBoot, a build, a 1.21.1 launch and a 26.2
launch -- five steps. Writing the proves() body without running it would put an unverified assertion into the one
place in this system that exists to refuse unverified assertions. That is the wrong trade.

## Session end state

    1.21.1  compile 0 errors, jar 7,348,757 bytes, boot reaches the EnderDragonPart composition guard
    26.2    compile 295 classes, tests 5635/78/0/846, server Done + 4300 ticks (re-verified after all changes)

## Obstacle 10: Forge's item-component hook is reached during Bootstrap, before FML exists

Traced to the frame from the run's logs/latest.log:

    Foods.<clinit>                                    Foods.java:12
      FoodProperties$Builder.usingConvertsTo          FoodProperties.java:109
        ItemStack.<init>                              ItemStack.java:234 / :250
          Item.components                             Item.java:114
            ForgeHooks.gatherItemComponents           ForgeHooks.java:1279   <- the merge wired this in
              ForgeEventFactory.<clinit>              ForgeEventFactory.java:220
                ModLoader.get                        ModLoader.java:136
                  ModLoader$LazyInit.<clinit>        ModLoader.java:140
                    new ModLoader()                  ModLoader.java:95
                      this.loadingModList.getErrors()   NPE: loadingModList is null

WHAT IT MEANS. Constructing an ItemStack during Bootstrap -- which Foods does for its stew bowls -- now asks for the
item's components, and the merged body of Item.components routes that through MinecraftForge's
ForgeHooks.gatherItemComponents. That hook's class initialiser touches ForgeEventFactory, which calls ModLoader.get(),
and FML's ModLoader cannot exist yet because mods have not loaded. So a vanilla static initialiser reaches the mod
loader three levels down through a hook the merge inserted, and the first food that converts to a container ends the
boot.

This is the same family as the obstacles already fixed (a merge-inserted body that is correct on 26.2 and reached at
a point this line does not reach it), and the kernel already carries repairs of exactly this shape -- notably
returnFromANestedBootstrapBeforeItsTail, which exists because a nested Bootstrap was reached before its tail.

NOT FIXED. Two candidate repairs exist and they must be told apart before either is written:

  a) make ForgeHooks.gatherItemComponents tolerate ModLoader not being initialised, returning the vanilla component
     view instead of throwing -- correct if items genuinely need components during Bootstrap;
  b) keep Item.components off the Forge hook entirely on this line, if 1.21.1's vanilla component path is complete
     without it and the merge simply wired the hook in where 26.2 expects it.

Choosing between them needs Item.components' merged body read on both lines, and whichever is chosen needs a build
plus a 1.21.1 launch plus a 26.2 launch. That is the next session's first task.

## Obstacle 10 resolved to ONE candidate: the hook is on both lines, only the timing differs

javap -c on Item.components in both staged bases:

    1.21.1   invokestatic  net/minecraftforge/common/ForgeHooks.gatherItemComponents
                             (Lnet/minecraft/world/item/Item;
                              Lnet/minecraft/core/component/DataComponentMap;)Lnet/minecraft/core/component/DataComponentMap;
    26.2     invokevirtual  net/minecraft/core/Holder$Reference.components()Lnet/minecraft/core/component/DataComponentMap;
             invokestatic   net/minecraftforge/common/ForgeHooks.gatherItemComponents
                             (same descriptor)

So candidate (b) from the previous entry is DEAD: the Forge hook is not something the merge wired in on 1.21.1 -- BOTH
lines call it, and 26.2 calls it right after asking Holder.Reference for the vanilla map. This is MinecraftForge's own
item-component patch, present on both lines.

That leaves (a), and it also explains why 26.2 boots: on 26.2 the first ItemStack that asks for components is
constructed AFTER FML's ModLoader exists, so ForgeEventFactory's static initialiser finds loadingModList populated. On
1.21.1 the first such ItemStack comes from Foods.<clinit> during Bootstrap, which is before mods load, so the same
initialiser dereferences a null. Nothing is wrong with the hook; the timing of the first caller differs.

THE REPAIR IS THEREFORE NARROW: make the path tolerate ModLoader not being initialised yet, returning the vanilla
component map unchanged rather than throwing, and say so in the log the first time. That is a repair on
net/minecraftforge/common/ForgeHooks (or the ForgeEventFactory initialiser it reaches) of exactly the kind the kernel
already applies to Forge classes -- guarded so that once ModLoader IS up the hook runs untouched.

WHY IT WAS NOT WRITTEN THIS ROUND: it needs the ForgeHooks body read at both call sites, a guard that cannot hide a
real failure once mods are loaded, a build, a 1.21.1 launch and a 26.2 launch. Deciding it is done; writing and
proving it is not.

## The gatherItemComponents body, so the repair is exact

javap -c on 1.21.1's net.minecraftforge.common.ForgeHooks.gatherItemComponents:

     0: aload_1                                  // original map
     1: aload_0                                  // item
     2: aload_1                                  // original map
     3: invokestatic  ForgeEventFactory.gatherItemComponentsEvent(Item, DataComponentMap)
                                                    -> GatherComponentsEvent$Item
     6: invokevirtual GatherComponentsEvent$Item.getDataComponentMap() -> DataComponentMap
     9: invokestatic  DataComponentMap.composite(DataComponentMap, DataComponentMap)
    12: areturn

Four instructions plus the aloads. The throw is at offset 3: that invokestatic is the first touch of ForgeEventFactory, so
its static initialiser runs right there, and during Bootstrap it reaches a null ModLoader.

THE REPAIR, therefore, is a try/catch around offsets 2..9 whose handler returns aload_1 -- the ORIGINAL map -- and logs
once. Catching Throwable is correct here rather than sloppy: what arrives is ExceptionInInitializerError (or
NoClassDefFoundError on the second attempt), not a checked exception, because it is a class initialiser failing.

Guarded so it cannot hide a real failure: the handler must log, and the log must say the vanilla component map was used
because FML was not initialised -- so a mod developer reading it knows the Forge event never fired for that item rather
than wondering why their component edit vanished. If the alternative of only catching while
LoadingModList.get() is null is available inside the handler, that is strictly better than catching unconditionally.

WRITING THAT HANDLER is not a source edit: it is an exception-handler table entry added to a class the kernel does not
own, and it needs frame computation for the injected handler block. The kernel does this elsewhere, but the call sites
for inserting a handler block are different from the call sites for rewriting instructions, and getting the frames wrong
produces a VerifyError that would replace a diagnosable NPE with a much worse one. That is why it is specified here
rather than attempted at the end of a session.

## A constraint that changes how obstacle 10 must be repaired: no COMPUTE_FRAMES anywhere

The guard I specified last round -- "if LoadingModList.get() is null, return the original map" -- introduces a branch,
and a branch needs a StackMapTable entry at its target. Checked whether the kernel can just compute it:

    ClassTweakerTransformer:161     "No COMPUTE_FRAMES: ... COMPUTE_FRAMES would need to load game classes from
                                     inside a class definition."
    KernelHudBridge:382-383         "Neither method branches, so no StackMapTable is needed and COMPUTE_MAXS
                                     suffices; COMPUTE_FRAMES would have to resolve game types through a loader
                                     this code does not have."
    LootSourceCallbacks:117         new ClassWriter(ClassWriter.COMPUTE_MAXS)

So every writer in the kernel uses COMPUTE_MAXS at most, on purpose: COMPUTE_FRAMES cannot be used while a class is
being defined, which is exactly when these transforms run. Every existing repair is therefore either branchless or
ships a hand-written FrameNode.

CONSEQUENCE, which is why this is written down before any code: the obstacle-10 repair has to be one of

  a) branchless -- and the one branchless shape available is to make the PRECONDITION true rather than test it, i.e.
     initialise FML's LoadingModList before Bootstrap reaches the first ItemStack, so that the existing
     ForgeEventFactory path simply works. That is a field/state repair, not a control-flow one.
  b) branching with a hand-written FrameNode at the merge/target -- which is what the kernel does where it must, and
     which is the part that must not be guessed.

Either way the earlier instruction "wrap offsets 2..9 in a try/catch" is the HARDEST of the three (exception tables
plus frames) and should be the last resort, not the first. Corrected here rather than left standing: a try/catch was
the obvious shape and it is the wrong one for this codebase.

WHERE TO LOOK FIRST FOR (a): who assigns Forge's loadingModList, and whether the kernel can run that assignment before
Bootstrap.bootStrap. If it can, obstacle 10 disappears without touching control flow at all.

## Obstacle 10: fixing the precondition is NOT cheap either, and there is a fourth way

Checked whether option (a) -- make FML's state ready before Bootstrap -- is cheap. It is not:

    net.minecraftforge.fml.ModLoader
        private final LoadingModList loadingModList;        <- final, set in the constructor
        private ModLoader();
    net.minecraftforge.fml.loading.LoadingModList
        private static LoadingModList INSTANCE;
        public static LoadingModList of(List<ModFile>, List<ModInfo>, EarlyLoadingException);

INSTANCE is only ever assigned by LoadingModList.of(...), which needs an actual mod-discovery scan to have produced
mod files, mod infos and any early loading error. So making the precondition true means running FML's discovery before
Bootstrap -- reordering the loader's startup, not a single repair. Option (a) is out.

That leaves (b) impossible (a guard is a branch, and there is no COMPUTE_FRAMES) and (c) the hardest. So:

## THE FOURTH WAY, and it is the cheap one: retarget the invokestatic, keep the branch in Java

The kernel already has the pattern for this -- LifecycleHookInjector retargets an invokestatic's owner and name and
nothing else, which needs no frames because the instruction count is unchanged.

So: add a Forbric-prefixed class in src/runtime/java -- where JAVAC writes the frames -- with a method of EXACTLY the
signature gatherItemComponents currently calls:

    public static GatherComponentsEvent.Item gatherItemComponentsEvent(Item item, DataComponentMap map)

whose body checks whether FML is up, returns a component event built from the unmodified map when it is not (logging
once, saying the Forge event did not fire because FML was not initialised), and otherwise delegates to
ForgeEventFactory.gatherItemComponentsEvent exactly as before.

Then the transformer retargets the single invokestatic at offset 3 of ForgeHooks.gatherItemComponents to that method.
One owner+name swap. No branch in bytecode the kernel writes, no exception table, no StackMapTable, no COMPUTE_FRAMES.
The Java compiler computes the frames because the branch lives in a .java file.

THIS IS THE SHAPE TO WRITE NEXT. It fits every constraint the previous three candidate shapes violated, and it uses
the retarget machinery the kernel already proves works (LifecycleHookInjector).

## Obstacle 11, fully traced: the seeder asks for a method 1.21.1 does not have

    [Forbric/Seed] could not seed NeoForge FMLLoader identity
      java.lang.NoSuchMethodException: net.neoforged.fml.loading.FMLLoader.getCurrentOrNull()
        at PassiveSeeder.seedNeoForgeLoader(PassiveSeeder.java:206)
        at KernelBoot.launch(KernelBoot.java:1191)

so the seeder aborts, FMLLoader.versionInfo stays null, and:

    NeoForgeVersion.<clinit>(NeoForgeVersion.java:26)
      Optional.orElseGet -> FMLLoader.versionInfo().neoForgeVersion()   NPE
        because FMLLoader.versionInfo() is null

javap on 1.21.1's FMLLoader explains it -- the whole seeding surface differs:

    1.21.1                                   26.2 (what PassiveSeeder assumes)
    private static VersionInfo versionInfo    public static FMLLoader getCurrentOrNull()
    public FMLLoader()          <- no-arg    public static FMLLoader getCurrent()
    static void setupLaunchHandler(IEnvironment, VersionInfo)   <- SETS versionInfo
    public static VersionInfo versionInfo()

There is no getCurrentOrNull, no getCurrent, and the constructor takes no arguments. So PassiveSeeder's shape is 26.2's
and nothing about it applies here.

## Two ways forward, and they differ in kind

(a) REFLECT THE FIELD. Set the private static `versionInfo` directly, constructing a VersionInfo to put in it. Needs
    VersionInfo's constructor shape read first, and leaves setupLaunchHandler un-run.

(b) USE THE REAL SETTER. Call `setupLaunchHandler(IEnvironment, VersionInfo)`. That is 1.21.1's own entry point, but it
    needs an IEnvironment -- i.e. more of ModLauncher's surface than the kernel currently stands up on this line.

This is a genuine design fork: (a) is a narrow repair that plants state, (b) uses the line's own contract but drags in
IEnvironment. It is recorded here rather than guessed at, because PassiveSeeder's javadoc says the whole point is
"environment identity only, no lifecycle" -- and (b) starts to look like lifecycle.

## The "real setter" is not available on this line without ModLauncher: measured

The user asked for `setupLaunchHandler(IEnvironment, VersionInfo)` instead of reflecting the field. Reading its body
settles whether that is possible:

    static void setupLaunchHandler(cpw.mods.modlauncher.api.IEnvironment, VersionInfo);
       0: getstatic     IEnvironment$Keys.LAUNCHTARGET
      12: invokeinterface IEnvironment.getProperty(Key) -> Optional
      17: ldc           String "MISSING"
      28: invokeinterface IEnvironment.findLaunchHandler(String) -> Optional
      49: invokevirtual Optional.isEmpty()
      52: ifeq          83            // non-empty -> continue
      61: ldc           String "Missing LaunchHandler {}, cannot continue"
      69: new           RuntimeException

So it reads the launch target out of the environment, looks up a launch handler for it, and THROWS
"Missing LaunchHandler, cannot continue" when there is none. A stub IEnvironment cannot satisfy that: the only way to
get a launch handler is ModLauncher's own service registry, i.e. standing up ModLauncher.

And on 26.2 the method does not exist at all (javap on run/neoforge-runtime: getCurrent / getCurrentOrNull, no
setupLaunchHandler), so it is a 1.21.1-only entry point -- any use of it has to be behind the same absence check that
currently guards the field write.

CONCLUSION: reflecting `private static VersionInfo versionInfo` and `private static Path gamePath` is not a shortcut
around the real setter; it is the only option available without dragging ModLauncher's launch-handler machinery into a
seeder whose own javadoc says "environment identity only, no lifecycle". The field write is the narrower thing.

Recorded here because the user asked for the setter and deserves the reason it is not used, not just the fact.

## Obstacle 13 inventory: the Forge family, read with javap

PassiveSeeder knows only net.neoforged.fml. The merged base also runs net.minecraftforge.fml, and its statics are just
as unseeded. javap on the 1.21.1 forge runtime:

    net.minecraftforge.fml.loading.FMLLoader
        private static AccessTransformerService accessTransformer;
        private static ICoreModProvider            coreModProvider;
        private static LanguageLoadingProvider     languageLoadingProvider;
        private static Dist                        dist;
        private static String                      naming;
        private static LoadingModList              loadingModList;   <- the NPE that stops the boot
        private static RuntimeDistCleaner          runtimeDistCleaner;
        private static Path                        gamePath;
        private static final VersionInfo           versionInfo;      <- FINAL
        private static String                      launchHandlerName;
        private static CommonLaunchHandler         commonLaunchHandler;

So the same four that mattered on the NeoForge side are here: loadingModList, gamePath, versionInfo, dist. Two
differences make this harder than the NeoForge repeat it looked like:

  versionInfo is FINAL on the Forge side, not merely private. Java 21 refuses Field.set on a static final unless the
  field is made modifiable, so the NeoForge trick does not transfer as-is.

  The failure that surfaced first is not the NPE but
      IllegalStateException: Failed to find version for package net.minecraftforge.versions.forge
      This is an invalid environment
  which comes from MinecraftForge's own version provider, not from FMLLoader -- so seeding FMLLoader's fields alone
  will not clear it.

WHAT THE NEXT PASS SHOULD DO FIRST: read net.minecraftforge.versions.forge.ForgeVersion (and its base) to see where
"version for package" is looked up from, because that decides whether the version has to be seeded in FMLLoader at all
or in a Forge-side package registry. Guessing at FMLLoader first would fix the NPE and leave the IllegalStateException
in place, which is the same shape of error as the six obstacles already cleared -- one layer down, not the same layer.

## 26.2 re-verified cleanly after the version gate, and a process-hygiene problem I caused

Fresh log, every leftover JVM killed, latest.log deleted first:

    log start  21:19:12  [Forbric/Versions] this instance provides {neoforge=26.2.0.88}
    Done (0.496s)! For help, type "help"
    [Forbric/Tick] 100 tick(s): mean 49.89ms, at twice the budget or worse 0 (0.0%)
    JarVersion lines: 0

The zero matters as much as the Done line: the gate did NOT fire on 26.2, because that line's merged base carries real
Implementation/Specification-Version attributes, so the gate delegates without logging. Read it both ways, the same way
the getAppearance guard was checked earlier.

THE PROBLEM: four JVMs were still running from launches at 20:40 and 20:45. The bash timeout does not kill the exec'd
java, so the latest.log read afterwards belonged to an ORPHAN run rather than to the launch just started. The tick
counts reported in the previous rounds were real ticks from a real server -- but not necessarily from the launch I
believed produced them, and one later launch may have lost a port race.

That is the same class of mistake as the three undeclared repairs: an observation true of SOMETHING, reported as if it
were true of the thing just done. The fix is procedural: kill every JVM, delete latest.log, launch once, then read.

## Obstacle 15, traced to the instruction: 1.21.1's EventListenerHelper probes a no-arg constructor

Full chain from the clean launch's log:

    ForgeMod.<init>                                    ForgeMod.java:372
      MinecraftForge.initialize                        MinecraftForge.java:56
        TagConventionMigrationHelper.init              TagConventionMigrationHelper.java:30
          EventBus.addListener(ServerStartingEvent.class, ...)   EventBus.java:190
            EventListenerHelper.getListenerList        :33
              getListenerListInternal                  :44
                Cache.computeIfAbsent
                  computeListenerList                  :58
                    Class.getConstructor(Class[])      <- EMPTY array, i.e. the NO-ARG constructor
                      NoSuchMethodException: ServerStartingEvent.<init>()
                        wrapped as RuntimeException "Error computing listener list for ..."  at :63

And the two-line comparison that makes it a defect rather than a mystery:

    1.21.1 forge-runtime-interop: EventListenerHelper.computeListenerList CONTAINS
        48: invokevirtual java/lang/Class.getConstructor([Ljava/lang/Class;)Ljava/lang/reflect/Constructor;
    26.2   same jar, same class: NO such call at all

So this line's interop EventListenerHelper demands a no-arg constructor on the event class, and NOTHING on either line
provides one:

    1.21.1  public ServerStartingEvent(MinecraftServer)              -- no no-arg
    26.2    public ServerStartingEvent(MinecraftServer)              -- no no-arg
            and 26.2's class is a final Record implementing ServerLifecycleEvent

Real Forge 1.21.1 works, so this branch is not normally taken: something about the merged base makes this interop copy
of EventListenerHelper the one that runs, and its probe is fatal here.

WHERE TO LOOK NEXT: read computeListenerList's body around offset 48 -- what array it builds, what it does with the
Constructor it gets, and whether real Forge 1.21.1 has the same probe under a different name or guarded by a
try/catch. That decides whether the repair is (a) make the probe tolerant, or (b) stop the interop copy being chosen
for this bus. Reading the body is the next action; guessing between those two without it would repeat the mistake this
session already made six times.

## Obstacle 15, the mechanism: computeListenerList has two paths and this event takes the fatal one

javap -c on 1.21.1's interop EventListenerHelper.computeListenerList(Class, boolean):

     0- 9  if (eventClass == Event.class) return EVENTS_LIST
    10-21  if (!flag && !Modifier.isAbstract(eventClass.getModifiers())) goto 43
           else fall through to 24
    24-42  SUPERTYPE PATH: superclass = getSuperclass(); parent = getListenerList(superclass);
                             return new ListenerList(parent)
    43-73  NO-ARG PATH:  ctor = eventClass.getConstructor();            <- empty Class[], offset 45
                         ctor.setAccessible(true);
                         return ((Event) ctor.newInstance()).getListenerList();
    74+    catch -> new RuntimeException("Error computing listener list for " + name)

So a CONCRETE event class with flag == false is instantiated through a NO-ARG constructor to obtain its per-instance
ListenerList. ServerStartingEvent is concrete, so it takes that path, and neither line gives it a no-arg constructor:

    1.21.1  public ServerStartingEvent(MinecraftServer)
    26.2    public ServerStartingEvent(MinecraftServer)   (a final Record implementing ServerLifecycleEvent)

THE REPAIR IS NEITHER (a) NOR (b) AS I FRAMED THEM. Making the probe tolerant (a) would leave getListenerList()
unavailable for a class that the bus is trying to give one to, and forcing another EventListenerHelper to be chosen
(b) fights the classloader. The narrow repair is (c): GIVE THE EVENT CLASS THE NO-ARG CONSTRUCTOR THE BUS WANTS, as a
synthetic <init>()V that calls super() and nothing else. Then getListenerList() returns a fresh empty ListenerList for
the probe, which is exactly what the no-arg path is for -- the probe does not keep the instance.

BEFORE WRITING IT, two things must be checked, and neither is a guess:
  * ServerLifecycleEvent (1.21.1) must itself have a no-arg constructor for the synthetic one to chain to. If it only
    has ServerLifecycleEvent(MinecraftServer), the synthetic ctor has to pass a value -- and there is no honest value
    for a server, which would make this repair wrong rather than narrow.
  * Whether the kernel can add a constructor at all: it adds METHODS freely (addBlockAppearanceResolver), but a
    constructor has to chain to a super ctor and the verifier checks that unconditionally.

That check is the next action. Writing the synthetic ctor without it would be the seventh time this session guessed at
a shape instead of reading it.

## Obstacle 15: the prerequisite check fails, and what that means for the repair

The narrow repair I proposed (give the event class the no-arg constructor the bus wants) needs the superclass chain to
have one. It does not:

    net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.Event                 public Event()                       <- yes
    net.minecraftforge.event.server.ServerLifecycleEvent  public ServerLifecycleEvent(MinecraftServer)   <- NO no-arg
    net.minecraftforge.event.server.ServerStartingEvent   public ServerStartingEvent(MinecraftServer)    <- NO no-arg

So a synthetic ServerStartingEvent()V cannot chain to super() -- there is no super() to chain to. Two ways remain, and
they are not equivalent:

  (c1) TWO synthetic ctors: ServerLifecycleEvent()V chaining to Event()V, then ServerStartingEvent()V chaining to it.
       The probe then gets an instance whose `server` field is null, which is harmless ONLY because
       computeListenerList discards the instance after calling getListenerList() -- offsets 62-70 in the interop
       bytecode. Without reading what ServerLifecycleEvent(MinecraftServer) does with its argument we do not know
       whether anything validates it, but this path never runs that constructor at all, so nothing it validates runs.

  (c2) ONE synthetic ctor: ServerStartingEvent()V chaining to super((MinecraftServer) null). Narrower in class count,
       but it RUNS the existing constructor with a null, so anything that constructor does with the server -- a field
       store is fine, a dereference is an NPE -- now happens during a probe that is supposed to be free.

(c1) is the safer of the two, and it is also the one whose failure mode is a verify error (loud, immediate) rather than
a null that surfaces somewhere far away later.

WHY THIS IS NOT BEING WRITTEN NOW: it is two class transforms on Forge event types plus a line gate, and it needs a
build, a 1.21.1 launch, a 26.2 suite, and a 26.2 launch to land -- five verifications. Writing it and leaving it
unverified is the exact failure this session has already committed three times (the undeclared repairs, the two
PatEntity drafts, the orphan-log readings). The correct action is to stop with the mechanism understood and the two
candidate shapes named, which is what this entry is.

STATE AT STOP: 26.2 compiles, 5629/78/0/840, Done (0.496s) + ticks, version gate silent there (0 lines). 1.21.1
compiles, reaches obstacle 15, cleanly and reproducibly, from a fresh log with no orphan JVMs.

## The moduleLayerManager wall, measured to the point where a decision is possible

IModuleLayerManager (modlauncher-10.2.4, javap):

    Layer: BOOT, SERVICE, PLUGIN, GAME
    Optional<ModuleLayer> getLayer(Layer)

The return type is the important part: Optional. "This instance has no such layer" is a REPRESENTABLE answer, so a
module layer manager that answers BOOT with ModuleLayer.boot() and the rest with Optional.empty() is not a lie about
what the layers contain -- it is a statement that the other three do not exist here. That is the one property that
makes option (b) worth investigating rather than dismissing.

WHAT IS STILL UNKNOWN, and decides (b):
  * WHICH layers Forge actually asks for, and what it does with an empty Optional. The first grep matched a different
    call (Module.getLayer(), inside a ServiceLoader loop for FMLLoader's own module) rather than
    IModuleLayerManager.getLayer, so this is not yet answered.
  * Whether GAME must resolve. If Forge uses it to locate game classes, empty breaks it; if it is used only for a
    service scan that Forge can skip, empty is fine.

THE THREE OPTIONS, unchanged and still the user's call:
  (a) stand up ModLauncher -- the startup model the kernel deliberately avoids
  (b) an honest IModuleLayerManager -- BOOT real, others empty; needs the "what does Forge ask for" answer first
  (c) declare the boundary -- Forge mods unusable on 1.21.1, NeoForge and Fabric still work

WHAT IS CLEAR: this is the third time the Forge family has hit ModLauncher's own surface (setupLaunchHandler needed
IEnvironment, moduleLayerManager needs IModuleLayerManager, and the seeder's real setter needed a launch handler). That
is a pattern, not three coincidences: driving the Forge side far enough into FML requires ModLauncher, and the kernel
was built to not have it.

## Option (b) collapses: GAME must resolve, and orElseThrow makes empty fatal

javap on 1.21.1's FMLLoader:

    public static ModuleLayer getGameLayer();
       0: getstatic  moduleLayerManager
       3: getstatic  IModuleLayerManager$Layer.GAME
       6: invokeinterface IModuleLayerManager.getLayer(Layer) -> Optional
      11: invokevirtual java/util/Optional.orElseThrow()
      14: checkcast  ModuleLayer
      17: areturn

So "this instance has no GAME layer" is NOT an acceptable answer: the one consumer of that Optional in FMLLoader calls
orElseThrow on it. An honest-but-empty stub would move the NPE to a NoSuchElementException in the same place.

THE REAL REQUIREMENT, therefore: moduleLayerManager.getLayer(GAME) must return a ModuleLayer that actually contains the
game classes. In Forbric the game is a classpath entry (patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar) plus libraries, not a named
module. Producing a ModuleLayer for it means defining modules for the game jar and its dependency graph and handing
Forge a layer whose contents match what it is looking for.

That is not a stub, and it is not small. It is most of what standing up ModLauncher does for this purpose -- so option
(b) does not sit between (a) and (c) as a cheap middle; it converges on (a) from below.

WHAT THAT LEAVES:
  (a) stand up ModLauncher's module layer machinery so GAME is real
  (c) declare the boundary: on 1.21.1, Forge mods cannot be supported because the Forge family requires ModLauncher's
      module layer manager, and Forbric has no game module layer to give it. NeoForge and Fabric are unaffected.

RECOMMENDATION, on this evidence: (c) now, and treat (a) as a separate project if Forge-on-1.21.1 ever matters. The
boundary is honest, it is cheap, and it does not block anything else -- 1.21.1 would ship with NeoForge and Fabric
support, and a Forge mod would be told why rather than crashing in FML.

This also completes the pattern the last entry named: three separate Forge-family stops all landed on ModLauncher's own
surface, and this one is the surface itself rather than a field on it.

## 1.21.1 reaches Done and then stops immediately -- cause not yet established

Three clean single launches, all the same shape:

    Done (1.0xx s)! For help, type "help"
    [Forbric/EventMux] bridged the first LevelEvent.Unload to MinecraftForge
    [Forbric/Shutdown] stopped 1 config file-watcher(s) at exit so the JVM can end

Done and Unload land in the SAME SECOND. No [Forbric/Tick] line is ever emitted, so nothing is measured about whether
the server could run -- only that it started and then stopped itself.

WHAT WAS RULED OUT, by trying it:

  * stdin EOF. Tried `sleep 300 | bash run/launch-kernel-server.sh nogui` so stdin is a pipe held open for five
    minutes. Same immediate stop.
  * missing jline. 1.21.1's version JSON lists NO jline at all (0 occurrences) and build/x11/mc-1.21.1/libraries had no
    org/jline, so the console handler could not load org.jline.reader.EndOfFileException. Copied jline 3.25.1
    (reader, terminal, terminal-jna) from .dev/minecraft/libraries into build/x11/mc-1.21.1/libraries. Same immediate
    stop. So the missing jline is REAL and worth keeping fixed, but it is not what ends the server.

WHY THIS IS NOT A HARNESS ARTEFACT: 26.2 in the SAME harness reached Done and then ticked for 45,000 ticks. So the
harness can hold a server up, and something on the 1.21.1 line asks it to stop.

WHERE TO LOOK NEXT, and it is a guess until measured: something calls MinecraftServer.stopServer() or closes the
reloadable-resources/console path right after the first LevelEvent.Unload. The Unload itself is suspicious -- a level
should only unload when the server is already stopping, so it is more likely a CONSEQUENCE of the stop than its cause.
The next step is to find what on this line triggers the stop, not to guess at the console.

STATE: still true that 1.21.1 compiles 810 -> 0 and reaches Done (2.126s / 1.101s / 1.037s / 1.030s across runs). NOT
true, and never yet measured: that it runs.

## The immediate stop is explained: a crash in initServer, not the console

Read the log around Done rather than guessing at stdin:

    23:28:04  [Server thread/INFO]  Done (1.037s)! For help, type "help"
    23:28:04  [Server thread/ERROR] Encountered an unexpected exception
              java.lang.NullPointerException: Cannot invoke "Dist.isDedicatedServer()"
                because "net.neoforged.fml.loading.FMLEnvironment.dist" is null
                  at net.neoforged.neoforge.server.ServerLifecycleHooks.handleServerStarting(:102)
                  at net.minecraft.server.dedicated.DedicatedServer.initServer(:230)
                  at net.minecraft.server.MinecraftServer.runServer(:670)

So the server reaches Done and then DIES in initServer on the next call. Nothing about the console, stdin or jline is
involved -- both of those were wrong theories and are now ruled out by evidence rather than argument.

Two cosmetic-only problems in the same window, recorded so they are not confused with causes:
    WARN  Configuration conflict: more than one oshi.architecture.properties on the classpath  (the same jar twice)
    WARN  Failed to get system info for ModLauncher: Launcher.INSTANCE is null   (crash-report path only)

WHAT THE FIX HAS TO DEAL WITH, measured:

    net.neoforged.fml.loading.FMLEnvironment
        public static final Dist dist;            <- FINAL
        public static final boolean production;   <- FINAL
        static void setupInteropEnvironment(cpw.mods.modlauncher.api.IEnvironment);
        static {};                                <- where they are presumably assigned

and setupInteropEnvironment does NOT assign dist itself. Its whole body is one call:

    environment.computePropertyIfAbsent(net.neoforged.neoforgespi.Environment$Keys.DIST.get(), <lambda>)

i.e. it populates a property ON THE ENVIRONMENT. dist itself is assigned in <clinit> from somewhere else. So the real
setter is not a usable hook the way Forge's completeScan was, and the field is final.

THREE WAYS FORWARD, none of them written yet:
  (1) read FMLEnvironment.<clinit> to find what it reads, and provide THAT;
  (2) write the static final with Unsafe/VarHandle -- a deliberate final-field bypass, which needs a justification
      stronger than "it works";
  (3) reconsider: this is the NeoForge side objecting, and the NeoForge family is seeded by seedNeoForgeLoader, so
      there may be an existing seam there that simply has not been used for dist.

(1) or (3) before (2): a final-field bypass is the kind of thing that turns a diagnosable null into a silent wrong
answer somewhere else.

## Mod loading on 1.21.1: the machinery works; a 26.2-targeted mod fails exactly as predicted

Put architectury's FABRIC and NEOFORGE jars (both 21.1.12) into the 1.21.1 server's mods/ and launched. Everything the
loader is supposed to do, it did, and said so:

    [Forbric/DupeId]  mod id 'architectury' claimed by 2 jars -- loading architectury-neoforge-21.1.12.jar (NEOFORGE),
                      aliased into [FABRIC]
    [Forbric/DupeId]  remembered 135 class(es) only architectury's FABRIC build has, so a guest mixin that targets one
                      can be named rather than going silently inert
    [Forbric/Versions] architectury requires neoforge [26.2.0.82,) but this instance provides 21.1.249 -- it is being
                      loaded anyway, and a genuine loader would have refused
    [Forbric/Fabric]  presence alias 'architectury' 21.1.12 -- its Fabric jar lost arbitration, but the winning jar
                      supplies the classes; isModLoaded now answers
    [Forbric/Fabric]  FabricLoader ready -- 4 mod(s), entrypoint keys []
    [Forbric/Boot]    sovereign kernel -- server 1.21.1, 106 owned jar(s), 1 Forge-family mod(s), 0 Fabric jar(s)
    [Forbric/Mixin]   mixin configs: 0 Fabric + 3 Forge-family -- architectury (architectury.mixins.json),
                      architectury (architectury-common.mixins.json), neoforge (neoforge.mixins.json)

So this line demonstrably: discovers mods, arbitrates a duplicate mod id across the two families, keeps the losing
build's exclusive class set so a mixin on it can be NAMED, aliases isModLoaded, checks version ranges, registers the
winning mod's mixin configs, and reports all of it.

WHAT HAPPENED NEXT IS THE PREDICTED FAILURE, not a surprise:

    Done (1.014s)! For help, type "help"
    ... then LevelEvent.Unload + Shutdown in the same second

Without any mod in mods/ the SAME build reached Done and ticked 1300 times. So this mod's presence ended it, and the
kernel had already said why it expected that: architectury 21.1.12 declares a neoforge range this instance does not
satisfy. A 26.2-targeted mod on 1.21.1 is not the test that proves mod loading works -- it is the test that proves the
WARNING works.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT ESTABLISH: that a mod BUILT for 1.21.1 loads and runs. That needs a 1.21.1-targeted jar, and
.dev-test/client/mods has none -- everything there that names a version names 26.2, plus version-agnostic architectury.
Until such a jar is tried, "mods load on 1.21.1" is not a claim this session can make.

mods/ has been emptied again so the next launch is the clean one.

## Mod loading, part 2: a second 26.x mod, and two real features proven by its failure

Tried ferritecore 9.0.0 (fabric + neoforge pair) as a version-agnostic candidate. It is not version-agnostic either:
its neoforge jar declares [26.1.0.1-beta,), which this instance (21.1.249) does not satisfy, and the kernel said so
twice before doing anything with it.

WHAT THE FAILURE PROVED, which is more than a success would have:

  1. MIXIN FIT ANALYSIS, per anchor, with the missing ones named:
       guest mixin ferritecore (ferritecore.fastmap.mixin.json):FastMapStateHolderMixin applies only partially on the
       merged base -- 1/6 anchors resolve, missing: @Shadow field StateHolder.propertyValues, @Shadow method
       StateHolder.isSingletonState()Z, @Overwrite StateHolder.setValueInternal(...), @Overwrite
       StateHolder.initializeNeighbors(...), @Inject target StateHolder.getNullableValue|lambda$getValues$0
       (kept; -Dforbric.mixinFit=strict drops these)
     and auto-suppression of the ones with NO anchor at all:
       auto-suppressing guest mixin ...:StateDefinitionMixin -- UNFIT on the merged base (no anchor resolves)
       ferritecore (ferritecore.fastmap.mixin.json): left out 1 of 2 mixin(s)

  2. A COMPATIBILITY POLICY that refuses to pretend:
       [Forbric/Compatibility] launch stopped: required mod initialization or features are unavailable; continuation
       was not approved (policy STRICT). Evidence: .forbric-kernel/compatibility-report.json.
       -Dforbric.compatibilityPolicy=continue launches anyway, for runs with nobody to ask

     and the evidence file it points at exists, with policy / schemaVersion / confirmedRequired / findings /
     catalogFailures / mods.

So the loader does not silently load a mod whose mixins it knows it cannot place: it names the anchors, suppresses the
hopeless ones, writes a machine-readable report, and stops unless told to continue. On 1.21.1. That is the behaviour a
merged base needs, and it did not exist as a demonstrated fact before this run.

STILL NOT ESTABLISHED: that a mod BUILT for 1.21.1 loads and runs. Every jar in .dev-test/client/mods that is not
version-agnostic names 26.x: architectury 21.1.12 wants [26.2.0.82,), ferritecore 9.0.0 wants [26.1.0.1-beta,). There
is no 1.21.1-targeted jar in this repository to try, so this is a missing input rather than unfinished work.

A next run could also pass -Dforbric.compatibilityPolicy=continue to see whether the mod survives despite the partial
anchors. That is a different question from "does a 1.21.1 mod work" and it has not been asked yet.

mods/ emptied again.

## A 1.21.1-targeted mod loads and the server keeps running

The repository had no jar built for 1.21.1, so one was made rather than waiting for one. It is deliberately minimal --
a single fabric.mod.json declaring nothing but a minecraft dependency:

    {
      "schemaVersion": 1, "id": "forbriclineprobe", "version": "1.0.0",
      "name": "Forbric Line Probe", "license": "Apache-2.0",
      "depends": { "minecraft": "~1.21.1" }
    }

649-byte jar, no classes, no entrypoints. Put it in the 1.21.1 server's mods/ and launched:

    [Forbric/Order]  1 Fabric mod(s) initialise in Fabric Loader's order, by mod id, as native Fabric orders them
    [Forbric/Fabric] FabricLoader ready -- 4 mod(s), entrypoint keys []
    [Forbric/Fabric] discovered 1 Fabric mod(s) in 1 jar(s) (incl. nested)
    [Forbric/Boot]   sovereign kernel -- server 1.21.1, 106 owned jar(s), 0 Forge-family mod(s), 1 Fabric jar(s),
                     100 MC library jar(s)
    [Forbric/Tick]   1300 tick(s): mean 49.99ms, max 55.70ms, at twice the budget or worse 0 (0.0%)

So on 1.21.1: the jar was discovered, accepted, counted as a Fabric mod, and the server reached Done and ticked 1300
times with it present. NOTE the absence of a [Forbric/Versions] warning -- the two 26.x mods both produced one, and this
one does not, because its declared minecraft range is satisfied. That is the version check answering correctly in BOTH
directions, which is stronger evidence than the warning alone.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT PROVE, and it matters: the probe has NO classes and NO entrypoint. It proves discovery, version
acceptance, and that the server still runs with a 1.21.1 mod present. It does NOT prove that a mod's CODE executes --
nothing in that jar ever runs. A mod with a real entrypoint is the next rung and is still untested.

STATE: 26.2 Done + 45,000 ticks; 1.21.1 Done + 1300 ticks (no mods) and Done + 1300 ticks (with the 1.21.1 probe).

## A 1.21.1 mod's ENTRYPOINT executes -- the last rung of mod loading, measured

The previous probe had no code, so it could only show discovery. This one has an entrypoint, and it ran.

SOURCE (all of it -- there is no more):

    package probe;
    import net.fabricmc.api.ModInitializer;
    public final class Probe implements ModInitializer {
        @Override public void onInitialize() {
            System.out.println("[PROBE] onInitialize RAN on this line");
        }
    }

    {
      "schemaVersion": 1, "id": "forbriclineprobe2", "version": "1.0.0",
      "name": "Forbric Entrypoint Probe", "license": "Apache-2.0",
      "depends": { "minecraft": "~1.21.1" },
      "entrypoints": { "main": [ "probe.Probe" ] }
    }

BUILT WITH THE KERNEL JAR AS THE COMPILE CLASSPATH, because net/fabricmc/api/ModInitializer.class is inside
build/libs/forbric-kernel-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT-1.21.1.jar -- no separate fabric-loader jar was needed or found:

    javac -cp forbric-kernel-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT-1.21.1.jar -d classes src/probe/Probe.java
    jar cf forbric-probe2-1.0.0.jar fabric.mod.json -C classes probe

MEASURED, one clean launch:

    [Forbric/Fabric] FabricLoader ready -- 4 mod(s), entrypoint keys [main]      <- the entrypoint KEY was read
    [Forbric/Boot]   sovereign kernel -- server 1.21.1, ... 1 Fabric jar(s)
    [23:55:27] [main/INFO]: [STDOUT]: [PROBE] onInitialize RAN on this line       <- it EXECUTED
    Done (1.045s)! For help, type "help"
    [Forbric/Tick] 700 tick(s): mean 49.99ms, at twice the budget or worse 0 (0.0%)

Note the difference from the class-less probe, which logged "entrypoint keys []". The key appearing, and then the line
appearing, are two independent observations of the same fact and both are needed: the key alone would only prove the
manifest was parsed.

A MISTAKE I MADE AND CAUGHT, worth recording because it is the opposite of the usual one: the first grep for the probe
output reported nothing, and I nearly wrote that execution was unproven. The grep had been `-Pattern "PROBE"` with
-First 5, and the earlier case-insensitive matches inside "probed" in the MergedBaseCompat lines pushed the real line out
of the window. An exact -SimpleMatch found it: count 1. Reporting a success as unproven is the same class of error as
reporting a failure as success, just in the other direction.

WHAT THIS ESTABLISHES: on 1.21.1 the loader discovers a Fabric mod, reads its entrypoints, counts it, invokes
onInitialize(), and the server runs on past Done with the mod loaded.
WHAT IT DOES NOT ESTABLISH: that a real mod (one that touches registries, mixins or events) works -- this probe does
nothing but print. And nothing here says anything about the client; only the dedicated server has been started.

## The first client launch: file completion passes, then the kernel refuses to boot (correctly)

Installed into E:\project\Forbric_tweaked test\versions\1.21.1-forbric and launched through PCL CE. File completion now
passes -- that took three fixes, all of them mine:

  1. I put a FAKE "url":"" + "size":1 on the two kernel libraries. A launcher treats a present url as "must download",
     and an empty url cannot succeed.
  2. I mapped the template's forbric-loader row onto forbricruntime, which collided with the existing forbricruntime
     row and left two entries for one artifact, one of them pointing at a file that does not exist.
  3. ROOT CAUSE: a launcher derives BOTH the expected filename and the download URL from the NAME coordinates, not
     from downloads.artifact.path. net.forbric:forbric-kernel:0.1.0-SNAPSHOT means forbric-kernel-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar,
     so a jar carrying the -1.21.1 CLASSIFIER could never satisfy it and got 404'd against every mirror. Fixed by
     putting the line in the VERSION (0.1.0-SNAPSHOT-1.21.1) instead of a classifier, and by ALSO placing copies at
     the old coordinate paths so a cached manifest is satisfied too.

Two of my readings were wrong before I got there, and both cost the user a launch:
  * "no new log means it never started" -- false. PCL APPENDS to the same session log, so the file grows without a new
    file appearing. The 200 B/s decaying to 0 the user saw was three stalled 404 retries inside that appended log.
  * "changing the json is enough" -- false while PCL holds a parsed version in memory. The same session id proved it
    was never restarted.

WHAT THE CRASH ACTUALLY SAYS, and it is the kernel behaving correctly rather than breaking:

    [Forbric/Lifecycle] net.minecraft.client.main.Main.main contained NO known genuine-loader trigger -- the merged
      base's entry shape changed; refusing to boot on an un-hooked lifecycle
    [Forbric/Anchor] forbric:lifecycle-hook-injector was handed net.minecraft.client.main.Main and made no edit --
      its anchor is gone
    [Forbric/Boot] IllegalStateException: kernel refusing to boot: the genuine client-loading lifecycle trigger was
      NOT redirected from the merged base (its shape changed)

MEASURED, and this is the whole finding:

  26.2 (what the injector knows):  MMain.main calls ClientModLoader.begin()V -- NO ARGS, at bc 814
  1.21.1:                         Main.main (797 javap lines) contains NO ClientModLoader call at all, and no
                                  Hooks.* call either
  1.21.1 ClientModLoader:         begin(Minecraft, PackRepository, ReloadableResourceManager) -- THREE ARGS
  1.21.1's real site:             net.minecraft.client.Minecraft.<init>, bytecode 945,
                                  invokestatic ClientModLoader.begin(...)

So the fix is not "add another row to CLIENT_TRIGGERS" -- the row alone would not help, because the injector is handed
net.minecraft.client.main.Main and on this line the call is not in that class. Two things have to change together:

  (a) CLIENT_TRIGGERS needs a 1.21.1 row: owner net/neoforged/neoforge/client/loading/ClientModLoader, name begin,
      desc (Lnet/minecraft/client/Minecraft;Lnet/minecraft/server/packs/repository/PackRepository;Lnet/minecraft/server/
      packs/resources/ReloadableResourceManager;)V, with the three argument slots popped.
  (b) the injector must also be handed net.minecraft.client.Minecraft on this line, which is a change to its target set,
      not to its trigger table.

(b) is the part that makes this more than a table edit, and it is why this stopped here rather than being pushed in
half-verified.

STATE: dedicated server Done + ticks on both lines; client reaches the kernel's own lifecycle check and is refused by
design, on a real install, through a real launcher.

## The shader lookup: my namespaces repair was aimed at the wrong method

Read after the repair failed to fire, which turned out not to matter -- because it was the wrong repair.

VanillaPackResources.getResource(PackType, ResourceLocation) does NOT use the namespace up front:

    0-4:   location.getPath() ; FileUtil.decomposePath(path)      -- namespace not read here
          mapOrElse(lambda$getResource$4, <error>)

and lambda$getResource$4 is the resolver:

    0-4:   String namespace = location.getNamespace()
    6-24:  for (Path p : this.pathsForType.get(packType))
    48-61: Path resolved = FileUtil.resolvePath(p.resolve(namespace), pathParts)
    63+:   if (Files.isRegularFile(resolved)) return IoSupplier.create(resolved)

So the namespace IS used -- per root path, as one path segment. The `namespaces` Set on the class is only what
getNamespaces() REPORTS, i.e. what a caller may list. Making that set name four namespaces changes listing, not
resolution. That is why the repair was pointless even though it was written correctly.

WHAT ACTUALLY DECIDES THIS: whether one of `pathsForType.get(ASSETS)` roots, with "neoforge" appended, resolves to a
real file. Measured against the instance:

    E:\project\Forbric_tweaked test\assets\  contains: indexes, objects, skins
    assets\neoforge\shaders\core\rendertype_entity_unlit_translucent.json   -> does NOT exist
    assets\minecraft\shaders\core\*.json                                     -> 0 files

There is no extracted namespace tree at all, on either namespace. So the pack must be serving from the merged base JAR
through a zip FileSystem -- root/assets/minecraft/... and root/assets/neoforge/... inside the archive. Whether that is
what the kernel builds, and whether the neoforge subtree is reachable through whatever roots it registers, is NOT
checked. That is the next thing to read: whoever constructs VanillaPackResources (or its builder) in this kernel, and
what root paths it passes for PackType.CLIENT_RESOURCES.

The repair stays committed but is documented here as aimed at the wrong method, so nobody re-derives the same dead end.

## The fix for the shader problem exists in this repo, is documented as fixing exactly this, and is never called

Narrowed in four steps, each one a measurement:

  1. WHERE THE FILE IS. rendertype_entity_unlit_translucent exists ONLY under assets/neoforge/ and assets/forge/ --
     six entries, three each. There is no assets/minecraft copy.

  2. SO THE BASE JAR IS BEING SERVED. assets/minecraft/shaders/core/*.json resolve fine -- the client reaches model
     stitching and vanilla shaders work. One jar, one pack, one section resolves and the other does not.

  3. HOW RESOLUTION WORKS. VanillaPackResources.getResource -> lambda$getResource$4 iterates
     pathsForType.get(packType) and does FileUtil.resolvePath(p.resolve(namespace), pathParts). So the namespace IS
     used, one path segment per root. The `namespaces` Set is only what getNamespaces() REPORTS. My earlier repair
     touched that Set and was therefore aimed at the wrong method -- it would not have helped even if it had fired.

  4. WHAT DOES NOT HAPPEN. This kernel already has the answer written down. KernelClientPacks' own class doc says it:

       NeoForge's own ResourcePackLoader.findResourcePacks (its mod_resources source) is ORPHANED on the merged base --
       nothing calls it ... So every ecosystem asset is unreachable. That is invisible until something actually needs
       one: NeoForge's NeoForgeRenderPipelines registers pipelines whose shaders live at assets/neoforge/shaders/*

     and it provides KernelClientPacks.addTo(Object packRepository, ClassLoader, List<Path> jars,
     Collection<Path> carriers). But:

       - no line matching [Forbric/ClientPacks] or [Forbric/Packs] appears in a 15,153-line log;
       - grep finds NO caller of KernelClientPacks anywhere -- not in src/main, not in src/runtime, not in the 1.21.1
         line overlay.

     So the mechanism that would make assets/neoforge reachable is present, public, and never invoked on this line.

That is the finding. Not "the pack is missing some namespace", and not "the shader is absent": the wiring that packs
ecosystem jars into the client repository is not being run.

NEXT, precisely: find what was supposed to call addTo (on 26.2 something else may cover this -- the mod_resources source
may be reachable there), and either call it on the client pack-repository path or explain why the 1.21.1 line reaches a
repository that never gets ecosystem packs. That is a call-graph question, not a bytecode-shape question.

## The exact missing call, and the exact edit it needs

Chain, all measured:

    ClientPackHookInjector redirects ClientModLoader.setupModResourcePacks(PackRepository)V
    -> KernelLifecycle.onClientResourcePacks(Object) -> KernelClientPacks.addTo(...)

    1.21.1's ClientModLoader has NO setupModResourcePacks. It has begin(Minecraft, PackRepository,
    ReloadableResourceManager). ClientPackHookInjector's own comment already says this: "it takes the repository in
    begin(...) instead -- so that entry currently matches nothing".

    LifecycleHookInjector's 1.21.1 client row DOES redirect begin, but with popSlots = 3, i.e. it pops the three
    arguments and calls the no-arg hook. The repository is thrown away one frame before onClientResourcePacks would
    have used it.

So the two injectors want the same instruction and only one wins, and the winner discards what the loser needed.

THE EDIT, in LifecycleHookInjector.emit:

    if (t.popSlots() == 0) {
        call.owner = KERNEL_HOOK_OWNER;
        call.name  = t.hookName();
        // descriptors are NOT touched here today
    }

A popSlots == 0 trigger keeps the caller's descriptor, so a hook reached this way must have exactly the game method's
descriptor -- which a boot-side class cannot name. That is why onClientResourcePacks(Object) uses popSlots > 0 and an
Object descriptor. So the 1.21.1 row needs BOTH a new hook and a descriptor the injector is willing to rewrite:

    Trigger(CLIENT_MOD_LOADER, "begin", CLIENT_BEGIN_3ARG, "onClientBeginReached", /* popSlots */ 0)
  + injector sets call.desc = "(Ljava/lang/Object;Ljava/lang/Object;Ljava/lang/Object;)V" for rows flagged that way
  + KernelLifecycle.onClientBeginReached(Object mc, Object packRepository, Object resourceManager) which calls
    onClientModLoading() and then onClientResourcePacks(packRepository)

NOT DONE, and deliberately not half-done: it changes the emit path that every trigger in this injector goes through, it
needs a flag on Trigger to opt in, and LifecycleHookInjectorTest / ClientPackHookInjectorTest both assert the current
shape of both halves. A wrong change here breaks the server boot, which is currently green on both lines.

This is the smallest remaining step toward the shader problem and it is written down so the next attempt starts from the
call site rather than from the shader.

## attribute lookup: which registry, reached by which route

Chased the swim_speed failure one level further, and ruled out the alias mechanism by reading bytecode.

RULED OUT: `NamespacedDefaultedWrapper` does not override `getHolder` at all -- javap lists no such method on it -- so
`DeferredHolder.bind`'s `registry.getHolder(key)` runs `MappedRegistry`'s inherited implementation over a registry whose
`keySet()` demonstrably contains the id. The "wrapper delegates to a ForgeRegistry" hypothesis is simply not there.

WHAT IS ACTUALLY CONTRADICTORY, both halves measured:

  * `KernelRegistryContent.logRegisteredContent` counts by iterating `BuiltInRegistries`' FIELDS of type `Registry` and
    their `keySet()`. It printed `registered content: neoforge: 22 entr(ies) {attribute=3, ...}` -- so
    `BuiltInRegistries.ATTRIBUTE` HAS three neoforge attributes.
  * `DeferredHolder.getRegistry()` is `BuiltInRegistries.REGISTRY.get(key.registry())` -- the ROOT registry, by
    ResourceLocation -- then `.getHolder(key)`. That returned empty, five times.

So the object at `BuiltInRegistries.ATTRIBUTE` and the object returned by
`BuiltInRegistries.REGISTRY.get(ResourceLocation.parse("minecraft:attribute"))` are not the same registry. On a merged
base with two registry systems alive that is the shape to expect, and it is the first explanation that fits both facts
at once.

THE INSTRUMENT FOR EXACTLY THIS IS ALREADY INSTALLED: `KernelLifecycle.reportNeoForgeAttributes` logs the attribute
registry's actual class as reached through REGISTRY, and whether the three neoforge attributes resolve by direct `get` on
it. It runs from `onClientBeginReached`, i.e. before any world is opened.

NOT FIXED, deliberately. The two candidate repairs are opposite in kind -- make the root registry hand back the same
object as the field, or make the field's registry the one `DeferredHolder` consults -- and choosing between them by
preference is how the last dozen rounds went wrong.

## 26.2 does not regress under the DeferredHolder repair

Full suite after adding `resolveDeferredHoldersThroughTheWritableRoot` to the shared compat transformer:

    5629 tests completed, 78 failed, 840 skipped

which is the contract's failure count, so the repair is inert on the line it was not written for -- its guard is the
class name `net/neoforged/neoforge/registries/DeferredHolder`, it touches only the `GETSTATIC
BuiltInRegistries.REGISTRY` inside `getRegistry`, and the runtime helper falls back to `REGISTRY` when
`WRITABLE_REGISTRY` does not exist.

The open question from the previous entry is partially settled: the 21:07 log carries BOTH transform-time lines and NO
`NoClassDefFoundError` naming `ForbricNullSafe` or `ForbricLoadingOverlay`. So those two classes were resolvable at
runtime even though the instance's `forbricruntime/0.1.0-SNAPSHOT` jar (dated 10-31) does not contain them. Where they
came from is still unidentified. What IS settled is that they were present, so those two fixes were real rather than
phantom.

## swim_speed, final mechanism: NamespacedWrapper.getHolder reads a map that was never backfilled

The diagnostic answered it in one launch. Measured, from the self-verified run:

    [Forbric/RegistryRoot] DeferredHolder resolves through WRITABLE_REGISTRY ...
    [Forbric/AttrCheck] attribute registry is net.minecraftforge.registries.NamespacedWrapper
    [Forbric/AttrCheck] neoforge:swim_speed      direct get -> PercentageAttribute
    [Forbric/AttrCheck] neoforge:nametag_distance direct get -> RangedAttribute
    [Forbric/AttrCheck] neoforge:creative_flight  direct get -> BooleanAttribute

So the attributes ARE reachable from the registry DeferredHolder resolves to, by ResourceLocation. The failure is narrower
than "wrong registry", and WRITABLE_REGISTRY was not the problem.

javap on NamespacedWrapper.getHolder(ResourceKey) settles it -- it is four instructions:

    aload_0; getfield holdersByName; aload_1; invokevirtual ResourceKey.location();
    invokeinterface Map.get; checkcast Holder$Reference; invokestatic Optional.ofNullable; areturn

i.e. `Optional.ofNullable(holdersByName.get(key.location()))`. That map is the Forge-side one, populated for Forge
registrations; the registration window that put the three NeoForge attributes into the registry did not put them there.
`get(ResourceLocation)` reaches them because it goes through the delegated registry, which is why the two disagree.

So the repair is a BACKFILL, not a redirect: after the registration window, copy the plain registry's holders into the
wrapper's holdersByName for every entry it is missing. That is the same shape as the other seeding fixes in this kernel
(ModList's three null lists, LoadingModList's brokenFiles) and it needs no bytecode branch, so no StackMapTable.

NOT WRITTEN YET. Recording it here with the exact instruction sequence above so the next attempt starts from the map
rather than from the crash.

## byLocation IS a java.util.Map -- which sharpens the contradiction instead of resolving it

javap on MappedRegistry:

    private final ObjectList<Holder$Reference<T>> byId;                             fastutil, not java.util.Map
    private final Reference2IntMap<T> toId;                                         fastutil
    private final java.util.Map<ResourceLocation, Holder$Reference<T>> byLocation;   IS a java.util.Map
    private final java.util.Map<ResourceKey<T>, Holder$Reference<T>> byKey;          IS a java.util.Map
    private final java.util.Map<T, Holder$Reference<T>> byValue;                     IS a java.util.Map
    private final java.util.Map<ResourceKey<T>, RegistrationInfo> registrationInfos;

and NamespacedWrapper.register(ResourceKey, T, RegistrationInfo) references holdersByName repeatedly, so `register` IS the
writer and the wrapper's constructor initialises that map.

So "the entries are in a fastutil map my scan skipped" is WRONG for byLocation/byKey/byValue -- all three are
java.util.Map, and ForbricRegistryHolders.copyFrom walks every Map field up the superclass chain. That is why SOUND_EVENT
gained 1611 and BLOCK 1060. If the NeoForge attributes were in ATTRIBUTE's byLocation or byKey, the same scan would have
copied them; ATTRIBUTE gained 31 and then only 3.

So the three entries added to ATTRIBUTE's holdersByName were NOT the NeoForge attributes, and the NeoForge attributes are
found by get(ResourceLocation) through a path that is not any of the six fields above.

UNRESOLVED, precisely stated: four measured attempts, one falsified hypothesis (two registries -- identity hash identical),
and now one narrowed question -- what does NamespacedWrapper.get(ResourceLocation) consult, given that byLocation / byKey /
byValue do not hold those three ids and holdersByName did not either before the backfill. That is one javap of get away,
and it should have been the first thing read rather than the eleventh.

## SOLVED, at the fifteenth round: get() reads the ForgeRegistry delegate, getHolder() reads holdersByName

The one javap that should have been first:

    public T get(ResourceLocation id);
        0: aload_0
        1: getfield  delegate:Lnet/minecraftforge/registries/ForgeRegistry;
        4: aload_1
        5: invokevirtual ForgeRegistry.getRaw(ResourceLocation)Object
        8: areturn

    public Optional<T> getOptional(ResourceLocation id);   -- same delegate.getRaw

    public Optional<Holder$Reference<T>> getHolder(ResourceKey<T> key);
        0: aload_0; 1: getfield holdersByName; ... invokeinterface Map.get; Optional.ofNullable

So the two lookups read TWO DIFFERENT STORES on the same object:

  * get(ResourceLocation)  -> delegate (a ForgeRegistry). The NeoForge attributes ARE there, which is exactly what AttrCheck
                             printed: PercentageAttribute, RangedAttribute, BooleanAttribute.
  * getHolder(ResourceKey) -> holdersByName, a separate wrapper map that the ForgeRegistry-backed path never fills.

That reconciles every measurement that had been contradicting every other: one registry object (identity hash equal), the
attributes present (get finds them), and the holder missing (getHolder reads a different map).

AND IT EXPLAINS WHY THE BACKFILL COPIED THE WRONG THINGS. ForbricRegistryHolders.copyFrom copies from fields whose value is
a `net.minecraft.core.Registry`. `delegate` is a `net.minecraftforge.registries.ForgeRegistry`, which is NOT a
net.minecraft Registry -- so my source scan skipped it, and the +31/+3 it added came from MappedRegistry's byLocation/byKey/
byValue, which do not hold the NeoForge entries either.

THE FIX, now with a target rather than a guess: backfill holdersByName FROM THE delegate ForgeRegistry, not from the
MappedRegistry fields. ForgeRegistry exposes its contents by id, so each Holder.Reference the delegate can produce for the
three NeoForge ids gets put into holdersByName under the same ResourceLocation.

That is the fifteenth round's answer to a question that one `javap -c` of `get` would have settled at the first.

## The delegate call is in the right method, and still prints nothing

Read by line number this time instead of by text -- which is what the previous three rounds should have done:

    2895:  private static void backfillRegistryHolders() {
    2898:      Object copied = holders.getMethod("backfill").invoke(null);
    2899:      Object fromDelegate = holders.getMethod("backfillFromTheForgeDelegate").invoke(null);
    2900:      ForbricLog.info("... delegate pass copied %s holder(s) from the ForgeRegistry", fromDelegate);
    2904:  } catch (Throwable t) {
    2905:      ForbricLog.warn("[Forbric/RegistryHolders] backfill did not run", t);

and `backfillRegistryHolders()` is called at 2916, immediately before `freeze(cl)`.

Artifact verified by unzipping the INSTALLED jar: `probe: True  delegateMethod: True` (22:54:28). Yet the run shows

    "field=" lines .......................... 31   from backfill(), same helper, one line earlier
    "delegate probe" lines ................... 0   unconditional, top of the delegate pass's loop body
    "backfill did not run" warn .............. 0   the helper's catch
    "unbound value ... neoforge:swim_speed" .. 4
    "freezeData() THREW" ..................... 2

`backfill()` runs, so the helper runs. The next call in the same try must run or throw, and neither its info line nor the
helper's catch appears. That is not a state explainable with the evidence in hand, and inventing a fifth explanation for
it is what produced the previous four wrong ones.

ONE UNEXPLAINED FACT MAY BE THE SAME AS ANOTHER. At 21:07 the log showed `ForbricNullSafe` and `ForbricLoadingOverlay`
working while the installed `forbricruntime/0.1.0-SNAPSHOT` jar provably did not contain them; that was recorded and never
resolved. If the game is loading classes from somewhere other than the jars this loop copies into, BOTH mysteries have one
answer -- and every "it is in the jar" verification in this session verified the wrong file.

That is the next thing to establish: not what the code does, but which file the process actually reads.

## CORRECTION: DeferredRegister.register(IEventBus) DOES exist

An earlier round concluded it did not, and that conclusion was wrong -- it came from a `Select-String -Pattern "public " | Select-Object -First 14` that truncated before reaching it. javap -p in full:

    public void register(net.neoforged.bus.api.IEventBus);
        1: getfield      registeredEventBus:Z
       19: putfield      registeredEventBus:Z
       29: invokeinterface net/neoforged/bus/api/IEventBus.addListener
       41: invokeinterface net/neoforged/bus/api/IEventBus.addListener

So the API is there, it takes an `IEventBus`, and `registeredEventBus` makes it idempotent -- calling it twice cannot add
the listeners twice.

That reopens the fix that was written off one round ago and makes it the shortest path for priority 1: reflect over
`NeoForgeMod`'s `DeferredRegister` fields and call `register(buses.get(0))` on each, before freeze. NewRegistryEvent now
reaches that bus (0 -> 1 measured), so the registry objects the listeners resolve exist by then.

The lesson is narrow and worth keeping: a truncated listing is not evidence of absence. When a javap answer is "this method
does not exist", the query that produced it must be complete before a plan is built on it -- this one cost a round and a
wrong architectural claim in a commit message.

Measured on the 1.21.1 client, latest build:

    [NeoRegisters] bus=EventBus, NeoForgeMod has 76 declared field(s)

    NeoForgeMod declared fields . 76    (present and readable)
    bus ......................... EventBus, non-null

`DeferredRegister.register(bus)` throws `InvocationTargetException` whose `getCause()` is null even after unwrapping, which
is itself suspicious -- an InvocationTargetException with no cause means the reflection call was constructed rather than
thrown by the target, so the unwrap edit may not have reached the running class. Verify the installed kernel jar contains
`Throwable cause =` in KernelLifecycle before trusting this line; that check is the first thing the next attempt should do,
because a stale jar has silently voided a verification in this session at least twice.

WHAT IS SOLID AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-DERIVED:
  * freezeData failed because KernelForgeAttributes.validate eagerly initialised DefaultAttributes before NeoForge's
    attributes existed. Holding validation across the client-entrypoint freeze fixed it (2 -> 0). Keep that.
  * NamespacedWrapper.get(ResourceLocation) reads the ForgeRegistry delegate; getHolder(ResourceKey) reads
    holdersByName. Two stores, one object -- identity hashes match. Every "backfill from the wrong map" theory is dead.
  * There is exactly one event bus on this base and it is reachable as NeoForge.EVENT_BUS.
  * The runtime jar is bundled inside the kernel jar and extracted to .forbric-kernel/lib/<sha>/, so ONLY copying the
    kernel jar is a real install. Verify by unzipping the installed artifact, never the build output.
  * Class.getMethod finds only public methods. Two rounds of "no output" were a package-private target.
  * SilentFailureCensusTest reports a total and then a truncated alphabetical file list; it cannot name the new catch.
    Locate them by scanning changed files for catch blocks whose body has no ForbricLog.

A METHOD FAILURE WORTH RECORDING, repeated five times this session: treating "I did not observe it" as "it is not there".
The instances were Class.getMethod/public, the bundled runtime jar, a Select-String truncated at 14 lines, ForbricLog.debug
being invisible at default level, and an `if (bus != null)` that skipped its whole body without a word. Every one cost
rounds. The rule that would have prevented all five: any branch that makes work silently not happen must say so.

## Round 49: the contradiction is inside one execution, and that is as far as this session got

`closeClientEntrypointWindow` has exactly ONE call site (KernelLifecycle:2731, guarded by `if (reopened)`), and
`onClientEntrypoints` has exactly one. So the window closes once, and within that single execution:

    line 333  ATTRIBUTES registeredEventBus=false  seenRegisterEvent=false
    line 359  attach failed on ATTRIBUTES: IllegalStateException: Cannot register DeferredRegister to more than one
              event bus.

Ten flags lines and eight attach lines separate them. Nothing in between is anything but my own two loops over
`neoMod.getDeclaredFields()`, both reading the same static field of the same object. Between reading `false` and calling
`register`, `registeredEventBus` became true.

That is the narrowest unresolved interval reached in 49 rounds, and it is not resolved. Two candidates the next attempt
should distinguish first, in this order:

  1. `neoMod.getDeclaredFields()` is not what I think. Print each field's NAME and TYPE index in BOTH loops and diff the
     two lists. If the attach loop sees a different first field, the mismatch is in the iteration, not in NeoForge.
  2. Something else already attached them between the two loops. `seenRegisterEvent` was false, so nothing had FIRED the
     event -- but attaching is not firing, and another pass could attach without firing. Grep the kernel for any other
     `DeferredRegister` + `register(` use; the kernel has other registration machinery that this pass did not account for.

WHAT IS SOLID, unchanged and not to be re-derived:
  * `freezeData()` no longer throws because `KernelForgeAttributes.validate` was eagerly initialising DefaultAttributes
    before NeoForge's attributes existed; holding validation across the client-entrypoint freeze fixed it (2 -> 0).
  * `get(ResourceLocation)` reads the ForgeRegistry delegate, `getHolder(ResourceKey)` reads `holdersByName` -- two
    stores, one object, identity hashes equal.
  * One event bus on this base, reachable as `NeoForge.EVENT_BUS`.
  * The runtime jar is bundled inside the kernel jar and extracted to `.forbric-kernel/lib/<sha>/`; only copying the
    kernel jar is a real install, and it must be verified by unzipping the INSTALLED artifact.
  * `Class.getMethod` finds only public methods.
  * `DeferredRegister.register(IEventBus)` throws IllegalStateException when `registeredEventBus` is already true
