KernelForgeBlockColors.postBlockTintColors(BlockColors)V` before it — stack-neutral, so NeoForge's own call still gets
its argument — and the new hook posts `ForgeHooksClient.onBlockColorsInit(colors)` over the same instance. Verified by
the next boot: `all 3 CLIENT_INIT bridge(s) installed`, and the "MISSING" block is gone.

### What the boot still names (the next queue, in order of what a mod would notice)

`restoreForgeGeometryReload` (Forge OBJ/custom models never reload), `forbric-neo-screen-events` (NeoForge mods are
never told a screen changed — Controlling, JEI, Balm), `forbric-forge-particle-groups`, `forbric-hud-element-bridge`
(guest HUD elements never draw), `GUI_OVERLAY_LAYERS` (Forge's layered HUD tree is never built), `forbric-client-pack-hook`,
`forbric:method-body-neuter`, `forbric-forge-options#keptUnknownKeys`, `forbric:vanilla-early-returns` at `Camera`,
`forbric-fabric-fluid-models`, and four compat repairs (`hideTheLegacyLootModifierIndexFromTheDirectoryScan`,
`translateAGuestsPrivateSkipMarker`, `vetoUnjudgeableOverlayConditions`, `keepTheSaveOffTheTeardownsFailurePath`).

Note on the ledger count: the run before this one read `71 of 192 ... 107 never loaded`, this one `70 ... 108`. The
difference is a target class that the client happened to load in one boot and not the other — it moved between
"landed" and "never loaded", not into the miss list, and no `did NOT` section was printed in either run.

Commits: `00b28ae` (breathing shape + two hedges), then the block-tint bridge.

## Round 92 - the geometry reload and the screen events: two anchors aimed at a base that is not this one

Both misses came from the same habit these rounds keep finding: an anchor written against one line's shape, then
reported as a lost feature on a line whose shape differs while the feature is either already present or lands from a
different place. Measured before touching either.

### `restoreForgeGeometryReload` - the reload signature moved, and the Forge table is read by nobody

`ModelManager.reload` on 1.21.1 (javap, header at line 79 of the dump) is the old six-parameter form:

```
public final CompletableFuture<Void> reload(PreparationBarrier, ResourceManager, ProfilerFiller,
                                            ProfilerFiller, Executor, Executor)
   0: aload_3
   1: invokeinterface ProfilerFiller.startTick:()V
   6: invokestatic net/neoforged/neoforge/client/model/geometry/GeometryLoaderManager.init:()V
   9: ...
```

The repair demanded 26.2's `(SharedState, Executor, PreparationBarrier, Executor)CompletableFuture` and then asserted
that the first real instruction was `ALOAD 1` followed by `SharedState.resourceManager()` - on this shape slot 1 is a
`PreparationBarrier`, so both the lookup and the assertion missed. It now tries the new descriptor first and falls back
to `LEGACY_RELOAD_DESC`, skipping only that entry assertion when the fallback matched. 26.2 still takes the first branch.

What the repair can and cannot buy on this line, measured over all 51262 entries of
`patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar`:

- `net/minecraftforge/client/model/geometry/GeometryLoaderManager` and its NeoForge twin are two independent classes,
  each with its own `private static ImmutableMap LOADERS` + `LOADER_LIST` and its own `get`/`getLoaderList`/`init`.
- A string scan finds the Forge class referenced by exactly **two** entries: itself and
  `net/minecraftforge/client/model/ExtendedBlockModelDeserializer`. `net/minecraftforge/client/model/ModelLoaderRegistry`
  has **zero** entries. `BlockModel` references the **NeoForge** deserializer, and `ModelManager` references the
  **NeoForge** loader manager.
- So model deserialisation is NeoForge's end to end and nothing reads the Forge geometry table. The repair now makes a
  Forge mod's `RegisterGeometryLoaders` listener actually run (its registration code executes and its map is populated),
  but a loader registered there will not be consulted when a block model is baked. Bridging that needs a Proxy adapter
  from Forge's `IGeometryLoader`/`IUnbakedGeometry` onto NeoForge's namesakes; the interfaces do line up member for
  member - `read(JsonObject, JsonDeserializationContext)` is identical, `bake(...)` differs only in the context type,
  and the two `IGeometryBakingContext` agree on every abstract method (Forge adds two default `getRenderType*`) - so
  the bridge is feasible and is a known limit, not a dead end.

### `forbric-neo-screen-events` - the anchor had the direction backwards

The injector's claim was that the merged `Gui.setScreen` posts only MinecraftForge's event and never NeoForge's. On
1.21.1 `Gui` has **no** `setScreen` at all; the screen changer is `Minecraft.setScreen(Screen)V`, and it already posts
both NeoForge events itself:

```
142: aload_0 / 143: getfield screen / 146: astore_2          // previous screen -> slot 2
147: aload_1 / 148: ifnull 185
151: new ScreenEvent$Opening / 160: astore_3
161: getstatic NeoForge.EVENT_BUS / 165: invokeinterface post
173: isCanceled / 176: ifeq 180 / 179: return                 // a cancelled Opening keeps the old screen
180: getNewScreen / 184: astore_1
185: aload_2 / 186: ifnull 215 / 189: aload_1 / 191: if_acmpeq 215
194: getstatic EVENT_BUS / 197: new ScreenEvent$Closing / 205: post / 210: pop
211: aload_2 / 212: Screen.removed()
215: aload_0 / 217: putfield screen
```

The mirror image is what is actually broken: a whole-jar scan finds `ForgeEventFactoryClient` together with
`onScreenOpening` in **one** class - the factory itself - so MinecraftForge's `ScreenEvent.Opening`/`Closing` are never
posted on this base, and a Forge mod that refuses or replaces a screen (Controlling, JEI, Balm) is never asked. The
factory's two methods are present and behave as on an old base: `onScreenOpening(Screen, Screen)Screen` posts Opening
and answers `aconst_null` when cancelled, otherwise `getNewScreen`; `onScreenClose(Screen)V` posts Closing.

The obvious repair - insert a call next to NeoForge's in `Minecraft.setScreen` - would need a branch (`if Forge
cancelled, return`), and this project deliberately never computes stack map frames: `CommonNetworkInteropInjector.java:367`
records that `COMPUTE_FRAMES`' `getCommonSuperClass` resolves through the wrong class loader, so every injector uses
`ClassWriter(0)`/`COMPUTE_MAXS` and adds no new jump targets. Instead `KernelScreenEvents.installForgeScreenEvents()`
registers a HIGHEST-priority listener on NeoForge's own two events and forwards, so MinecraftForge sees exactly the
moments its two calls were written for, ahead of every other listener, and a cancelled Opening cancels NeoForge's -
which vanilla's `if (isCanceled) return` already honours. Reflection keeps Forge's event package off 26.2's classpath:
absent factory, no listener, no warning.

Verified in the launch log at 13:54:

```
[13:54:51] [Forbric/MergedBaseCompat] ModelManager initializes Forge geometry loaders on every resource reload
[13:54:52] [Forbric/Screens] MinecraftForge's ScreenEvent.Opening and Closing now ride NeoForge's on this base,
           ahead of every other listener
[13:54:48] [Forbric/Anchor] 71 of 192 declared repair(s) landed; 107 target(s) were never loaded on this side,
           and 14 hedge(s) correctly found nothing to do
```

No `did NOT` section at all; `VerifyError` 0, `Missing textures in model` 0, blocks atlas still 1024x512x4 from 1698
sprites. `NeoScreenEventsInjector`'s GUI anchor is now `HEDGE` with the measurement written into its text, since on
this base there is no seam there to fill.

### Addendum - the kernel's own pack.mcmeta named a format this line cannot read

`[Forbric/ClientPacks]` served the three ecosystem packs, but the kernel's own one fell back: vanilla's
`Pack.readPackMetadata` threw `JsonParseException: No key pack_format in MapLike[{"description":"Forbric kernel
assets","min_format":88,"max_format":88}]`, the log carried `Couldn't load pack metadata` as an ERROR, and the pack
was then served through NeoForge's reader — which skips the hooks mods put on vanilla's reader (fusion mounts its
overlays there). The file is shared across lines and had been written for 26.2, whose codec takes a
`min_format`/`max_format` range; 1.21.1's `MetadataSectionType` codec still wants the single `pack_format` integer.

Adding `"pack_format": 88` alongside the range fixes both sides, and was checked rather than assumed: 26.2's own
strict codec still accepts it (`KernelRuntimePackMetadataTest`, 1 test, 0 failed) because the extra key is ignored
where the range is read. Measured on the next launch — `No key pack_format` 1 -> 0, `Couldn't load pack metadata`
1 -> 0, `Missing metadata in pack forbric/forbric-kernel-runtime` 1 -> 0, and the pack now reads through vanilla's
reader: "2 of the 2 routed to vanilla's Pack.readPackMetadata" (was 1 of 2).

### Addendum - two anchors that were reporting a feature this line already has

- **`forbric-client-pack-hook`** claimed the kernel would never receive the live `PackRepository`. It does: the same
  log carries `[Forbric/Begin] the client entry reached the kernel: ... repository=net.minecraft.server.packs.repository.PackRepository`
  and then `served 3 ecosystem asset pack(s)`. 1.21.1's `ClientModLoader` has no `setupModResourcePacks` at all; its
  `begin(Minecraft, PackRepository, ReloadableResourceManager)` carries the repository and `LifecycleHookInjector`
  redirects that to `onClientBeginReached`, which calls `onClientResourcePacks`. 26.2 has no repository in `begin()`
  and does need `setupModResourcePacks`, so the anchor is now `HEDGE` with that split written into its text; the 26.2
  guarantee stays with `ClientPackHookInjectorTest`, which asserts the staged 26.2 carrier still declares it.
- **`forbric:method-body-neuter`** named `finish()V` and `completeModLoading()Z` as neuters that "would not be
  applied". Measured: 1.21.1's `ClientModLoader` declares neither — it has `finishModLoading(Executor, Executor)V`
  and `completeModLoading(Runnable)Runnable` — and the client boots to the main menu with those running, so nothing
  the kernel meant to hide is running. `MethodBodyNeuter.Target` gained an `optional` flag; an owner whose targets
  are all optional now declares a `HEDGE` ("this line does not declare ..., so there is nothing to neuter") instead
  of claiming a lost feature every boot.

Launch after both: ledger `71 of 192 ... 107 ... 14`, and the "anchor is gone" list fell from 13 names to 10.

### Addendum - Forge has no particle-group API on 1.21.1 either

`forbric-forge-particle-groups` claimed "a MinecraftForge mod registering a particle group throws". Measured both
sides before touching it: MinecraftForge's own `ParticleEngine`, read out of the merged base at
`META-INF/forbric/native-reference/FORGE/net/minecraft/client/particle/ParticleEngine.class.bin`, declares **no**
`registerParticleGroup`, **no** static `factories` map and **no** `particleRenderOrder` — only vanilla's
`trackedParticleCounts`, `updateCount` and `hasSpaceInParticleLimit`. And the merged base contains no
`RegisterParticleGroupsEvent` of either family. So on this line a Forge mod cannot register a particle group because
Forge itself stopped offering the seam; nothing throws from the platform, and the repair has nothing to bridge. Its
anchor is now `HEDGE` carrying that measurement, the repair keeps declining cleanly, and the "anchor is gone" list fell
to 9 names.

## Round 92 - MinecraftForge's HUD overlay stack is built here, because nothing else builds it

Measured on `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar` (51262 entries):

- `net.neoforged.neoforge.client.gui.GuiLayerManager` declares only `add(ResourceLocation, LayeredDraw$Layer)`,
  `add(GuiLayerManager, BooleanSupplier)`, `render`, `renderInner`, `initModdedLayers`, `getLayerCount`. There is no
  `GuiLayer` type in the whole `net/neoforged/neoforge/client/gui/` package.
- `GuiLayerManager` is referenced by `Gui`, `ClientHooks`, `RegisterGuiLayersEvent` and its own `NamedLayer`, so the
  NeoForge half of the HUD is wired. `ForgeLayeredDraw` is referenced only by `AddGuiOverlayLayersEvent`,
  `ForgeEventFactoryClient`, `ForgeLayeredDraw$LayerOffset` and itself: **nothing in the merged base ever constructs
  one**, which is why every MinecraftForge HUD overlay draws nothing on this line. There is no `ForgeGui` and no
  `ForgeLayer` either, so the builder MinecraftForge itself used on older lines does not exist here.
- `ForgeLayeredDraw` has `(ResourceLocation)` and `(ResourceLocation, List)` constructors plus
  `add/putAbove/putBelow/move/addAbove/addBelow/addWithCondition/addConditionTo/resolveLayers/locateStack/getChild/
  getLayer/getName`. It has **no `extractRenderState`** (that member is 26.x); `render` comes from `LayeredDraw`.

Two defects were fixed:

1. `HudElementBridgeInjector` called `appendForgeOverlayInstall(node)` and then returned the untouched `classBytes`
   whenever `edited == 0` — throwing away the overlay hook it had just appended. That single early return is why the
   `initModdedLayers` tail call never existed on a base whose `GuiLayerManager` takes `LayeredDraw.Layer` rather than
   `GuiLayer`. It is now `if (edited == 0 && overlays == 0)`.
2. The 1.21.1 overlay copy of `KernelForgeOverlayLayers.install` refused outright (`"1.21.1 has no Hud"`). It now
   builds the tree: `new ForgeLayeredDraw(ForgeLayeredDraw.VANILLA_ROOT)`, seed every name from `vanillaLayerNames()`
   as a no-op so a mod's `addAbove(CROSSHAIR, ...)` has an anchor, `ForgeEventFactoryClient.onComputeLayerOrder(tree)`
   to post `AddGuiOverlayLayersEvent`, `tree.resolveLayers()` (whose head the kernel neuters, replacing the seeded
   no-ops), then `GuiLayerManager.add(LAYER, tree::render)`.

Honest limit: the stack-targeted overloads (`addBelow(HOTBAR_AND_DECOS, id, ..., layer)`) resolve through
`subLayerStacks`, and a flat tree has none, so those layers still drop. Rebuilding the real stack shape needs the
builder class this line does not ship.

Verified in the launch log:

```
[Forbric/HudBridge] entering net.minecraftforge.client.gui.overlay.ForgeLayeredDraw.resolveLayers before it posts
  its registration event ...
[Forbric/HudBridge] MinecraftForge's overlay stack is on NeoForge's layer manager, built here from 17 seeded vanilla
  position(s) - silenced 17 vanilla leaf/leaves across 1 stack(s), re-seeded 0 name(s) at the root so root-relative
  anchors still resolve - and NO mod layer is in the tree, so nothing new will draw
[Forbric/EventMux] all 1 CLIENT_HUD bridge(s) installed - both Forge families receive these events 1:1
```

`anchor is gone` 9 -> 8, `VerifyError` 0, no FATAL, blocks atlas still `1024x512x4`.

## Round 93 - two compat anchors proved to describe 26.x shape, not this base

Both were `fixed(...)` against a class that exists here, so the ledger reported them as lost features on every
launch. Measured, then demoted to `scanned(...)` with the measurement in the comment:

- `translateAGuestsPrivateSkipMarker` -> `SimpleJsonResourceReloadListener`. `javap -c` of
  `scanDirectory(ResourceManager, String, Gson, Map)` shows one `Map.put` at offset 100 fed straight from
  `GsonHelper.fromJson`, and the class contains **zero** calls to `DataResult.ifSuccess`. This line's reader is the
  plain vanilla one: a guest mixin's skip marker has no cast to reach here. The reader that does carry a condition
  layer (`ConditionalOps`) is the registry loader, and `letFabricResourceConditionsDecide` serves that seam.
- `keepTheSaveOffTheTeardownsFailurePath` -> `IntegratedServer`. `stopServer()` on 1.21.1 is
  `aload_0; invokespecial MinecraftServer.stopServer()V; return`, and `teardownPublishedState` exists nowhere in the
  class. The save-loss ordering this guards is 26.x shape; the save itself is vanilla's (`PlayerList.saveAll` runs
  before anything is torn down).

`anchor is gone` 8 -> 6. `VerifyError` 0, no FATAL, log 1400 lines.

## Round 94 - the Camera and Options anchors were claims about 26.x, not about this base

- `forbric:vanilla-early-returns @ Camera`. The shipped 1.21.1 table (`src/main/mc-line/1.21.1-tables/.../vanilla-early-returns.txt`,
  2698 rows) contains **no** `net/minecraft/client/Camera#...` row, so the transform returns at `rows == null` for
  `Camera` and never folds its guard into a tail. The anchor now asks `table().containsKey(CAMERA)` and says so instead
  of claiming issue #31 on a line whose table cannot produce it.
- `forbric-forge-options#keptUnknownKeys @ Options`. Neither staged base declares `unknownKeys`: javap of
  `patched-mc-neoforge-1.21.1.jar` and `patched-mc-forge-1.21.1.jar` `net.minecraft.client.Options` shows
  `load()`, `load(boolean)`, `save()`, `processOptions(Options$FieldAccess)` and `processOptionsForge(Options$FieldAccess)`
  and no such field, and the merged base is identical on that surface. The unknown-key retention this repairs is 26.x
  shape; both upstream bases behave the same way here, so the merge removed nothing.

`anchor is gone` 6 -> 5 -> 4 across the two launches. Ledger: `71 of 188 declared repair(s) landed; 103 target(s) were
never loaded on this side, and 14 hedge(s) correctly found nothing to do`. `VerifyError` 0, no FATAL.

## Round 95 - the loot-modifier index, and the last four anchors measured shut

- **Real fix, `hideTheLegacyLootModifierIndexFromTheDirectoryScan`.** On 1.21.1 NeoForge's
  `LootModifierManager` *does* declare its own `prepare(ResourceManager, ProfilerFiller)`
  (`aload_0; aload_1; aload_2; invokespecial SimpleJsonResourceReloadListener.prepare`), so the repair's
  "synthesize one" branch declined and `loot_modifiers/global_loot_modifiers.json` was still scanned by the directory
  walk. It now falls through to `wrapMinecraftForgePrepare`, which wraps the `aload_1` feeding that `super.prepare`
  with `KernelLootModifiers.withoutTheLegacyIndex`. Synthesizing instead would have produced a duplicate method and a
  `VerifyError`.
- Three anchors measured shut, each with the reason in the code:
  - `vetoUnjudgeableOverlayConditions`: 1.21.1's `OverlayEntry` is a record of `(InclusiveRange<Integer> format,
    String overlay)`; there is no condition component, so a condition key is dropped by the codec before any decision
    point exists. Vetoing needs the CODEC wrapped, not this record patched.
  - `fabric-fluid-models`: `ClientHooks` on this base declares no fluid method at all and the log carries zero
    "Missing FluidModel for fluid" lines — the check this hook explains arrives with 1.21.4's `FluidStateModelSet`.
  - `letTheAtlasLowerItsMipLevelLikeVanilla`: every atlas uploads at its full stitched size (blocks 1024x512x4 from
    1698 sprites), and no launch has ever printed "Unable to fit", "limits mip level" or "dropping miplevel".

Result: `[Forbric/Anchor] 71 of 185 declared repair(s) landed; 100 target(s) were never loaded on this side, and 14
hedge(s) correctly found nothing to do` — **zero anchors missed**, zero `anchor is gone` lines, `VerifyError` 0,
no FATAL, blocks atlas `1024x512x4`.

## Round 96 - the three startup failures, and the three ways a lookup can lie

Three lifecycle errors had been in every launch log for dozens of rounds:

    [Forbric/Lifecycle] NewRegistryEvent.fill failed - mod-declared custom registries may be missing
      IllegalStateException: Attempted duplicate registration of registry neoforge:entity_data_serializers
    [Forbric/Forge] the traditional-Forge baseline mod (ForgeMod) did not come up
      NoSuchMethodException: net.minecraftforge.data.event.GatherDataEvent.<init>()
    [Forbric/Forge] NewRegistryEvent failed - Forge custom registries (fluid_type etc.) will not exist
      NoSuchFieldException: BUS

### 1. The no-arg constructor Forge probes for

`ForgeMod`'s constructor calls `EventListenerHelper.computeListenerList`, which asks each event class for
`Class.getConstructor()` - a no-argument constructor it only ever holds, never invokes, to read the generic
bounds off it. The merged `GatherDataEvent` has exactly one constructor,
`(ModContainer, DataGenerator, DataGeneratorConfig, ExistingFileHelper)`, so the probe throws and Forge's
baseline mod never constructs at all.

The repair that already synthesised no-arg constructors for `ServerStartingEvent` and
`ClientPlayerNetworkEvent$LoggingIn` was gated on the package `net/minecraftforge/event/`. Widening that gate
to `net/minecraftforge/data/event/` fixed `GatherDataEvent` and moved the failure to the next subscriber:
`net.minecraftforge.registries.RegisterEvent.<init>()`. Enumerating packages only postpones the problem, so the
gate is now structural - any `net/minecraftforge/**.&#42;Event` whose superclass is a Forge eventbus type gets the
synthesised `ALOAD 0; INVOKESPECIAL super.<init>()V; RETURN`. 91 classes took it on this line, and
`constructed traditional-Forge baseline mod ForgeMod` now appears where `did not come up` used to.

### 2. Three BUS lookups that could not work, and ModLoader discarding silently

`KernelForgeBaseline.fireNewRegistryEvent` and `ForgeDatapackDeclarations.declare` both did
`eventClass.getField("BUS").get(null)`. Measured: neither `net.minecraftforge.registries.NewRegistryEvent` nor
`net.minecraftforge.registries.DataPackRegistryEvent` declares a `BUS` field on this base, so both threw
`NoSuchFieldException` before any listener ran.

The obvious replacement, `ModLoader.get().postEvent(event)`, is worse than it looks:

    javap -c net.minecraftforge.fml.ModLoader#postEvent
      getstatic loadingStateValid; ifne 25
      ... LOGGER.error("Cowardly refusing to send event {} to a broken mod state")
      return

On this line `loadingStateValid` is false (the mod list is synthesised by the kernel), so every event posted
that way is dropped with one log line and no exception - a failure that looks like success.

What does work is posting straight onto each mod's own bus. `KernelForgeContainers` (the 1.21.1 overlay) builds
`IEventBus bus = modBus()` per container and stores it in `Handle.busGroup()`, so the new
`boot/ForgeEventPost` posts onto every handle that `IEventBus.isInstance`, and only falls back to `ModLoader`
and then to the `BUS` field. Its first version tried `ForeignType.EVENT.binary(FORGE)` for the event base class,
which resolves to 26.2's `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.internal.Event` - a class that does not exist on 1.21.1,
turning the real 0-listener outcome into `ClassNotFoundException` reported as "listener failed". The event base
is now discovered from the event object itself.

The third site, `LateForgeRegistryDeclarations.snapshot`, has no correct answer on this line at all: EventBus 6
has no `BusGroup.create`, so the late-declaration replay cannot be built. It now returns `null` (and `fire`
returns early on both `null` and a missing `BusGroup`), because returning an empty list would make the
before/after diff treat every existing listener as newly added and re-register the baseline registries.

### 3. The duplicate, and three lookups that each reported "clean"

With the baseline mod constructing, 90 calls to `RegisterEvent` started reaching Forge's generated dispatchers
and failing:

    IllegalAccessError: failed to access class net.minecraftforge.registries.DeferredRegister$EventDispatcher
      from class net.minecraftforge.registries.__EventDispatcher_handleEvent_RegisterEvent

The eventbus compiles its handler class in its own `ASMClassLoader`; the dispatcher lives in the game loader, so
the same package name is a different runtime package. `makeTheGeneratedDispatcherTargetsPublic` makes those
generated `$EventDispatcher` classes public. Both `IllegalAccessError` and `threw handling RegisterEvent` went
to 0.

The duplicate registration survived all of that and was the last thing to fall. `fill()` unfreezes the root,
registers each collected registry and throws on the first id the root already holds. The kernel's guard tries
to drop those first, and each version of it reported a clean list while the duplicate remained:

  * by key TEXT: `11 registry(ies) collected, 11 distinct key(s)` - two distinct ResourceKeys naming one id;
  * by id OBJECT: also clean, because the merged base can hand the same registry an id of each spelling
    (`ResourceLocation` here, `Identifier` there) and each class's `equals` checks its own type while both print
    `ns:path`;
  * through `((java.util.Map<?, ?>) BuiltInRegistries.REGISTRY).containsKey(id)`: every entry unreadable -
    `ClassCastException: class net.minecraft.core.MappedRegistry cannot be cast to class java.util.Map`. The
    registry is not a Map, and because the surrounding catch treats an unreadable entry as "leave it to fill()",
    the failure was invisible except as the duplicate.

The fix is two-part: deduplicate on the id's TEXT (the only form that sees both spellings and both keys), and
ask the root through a lookup it actually declares - walking its methods for `containsKey` / `getOptional` /
`getValue` / `get` whose parameter accepts this id type, rather than naming a type this shared file cannot.
Result on the next launch: `11 registry(ies) were already in the root ... left out of this fill`,
`Attempted duplicate` 1 -> 0, `NewRegistryEvent.fill failed` 1 -> 0.

### Lesson

All three failures were lookups whose failure mode was a quiet "no": a probe that only asks for the shape it
was compiled against, a post that is discarded without an exception, and a containsKey that threw and was
caught as "unknown". When a guard reports that there is nothing to do, the next question is not "what else
should I check" but "how would this guard tell me it could not look at all".

## Round 97 - the listener wrappers the eventbus asks ModLauncher to write

After the three startup failures were fixed, the log still carried twelve of these:

    [Render thread/ERROR]: Error registering event handler:
      class net.minecraftforge.event.network.ConnectionStartEvent
      public void net.minecraftforge.common.ForgeInternalHandler.onConnectionStart(...)
    java.lang.ClassNotFoundException:
      net.minecraftforge.common.__ForgeInternalHandler_onConnectionStart_ConnectionStartEvent
      (game-side, but not found in any kernel-owned jar)

Twelve is the number of `@SubscribeEvent` methods on Forge's own `ForgeInternalHandler`, and the same would have
happened to every Forge mod's listener object. The eventbus never calls a `@SubscribeEvent` method directly: it
asks its `ClassLoaderFactory` for a wrapper class named
`<owner package>.__<owner simple name>_<method>_<event simple name>`, and on a real Forge that class is written
by ModLauncher during transformation. `javap` of `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.ModLauncherFactory` shows the
contract in two halves:

    protected Class<?> createWrapper(Method m) {
        enqueueWrapper(m);                       // remembers m in a static PENDING cache under the unique name
        return Class.forName(getUniqueName(m), true, getClassLoader());   // expects the transformer to fill it in
    }
    public static boolean hasPendingWrapperClass(String name)
    public static void processWrapperClass(String name, ClassNode node)  // <- transformNode fills the node in

So the missing half is exactly one call. `defineEventBusWrapper` in `ForbricClassLoader` asks
`hasPendingWrapperClass`, allocates the `ClassNode` type that `processWrapperClass` declares, has the eventbus
fill it, writes it with `ClassWriter(COMPUTE_MAXS)` and defines it in the game loader.

Two details cost a build each:

  * the eventbus class must be looked up through the **thread context loader** - that is the loader its own
    `Class.forName` for the wrapper uses, and asking `ForbricClassLoader` first found no
    `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.ModLauncherFactory` at all;
  * `ClassNode.accept` is declared against `ClassVisitor`, not `ClassWriter`, so `getMethod("accept", writerType)`
    throws `NoSuchMethodException` - resolve `ClassVisitor` from the node's own loader and pass that.

Measured after the fix: `Error registering event handler` 12 -> 0, two wrappers generated and logged by name,
`Attempted duplicate` 0, `VerifyError` 0, `Missing textures in model` 0, log 1379 lines. Forge's
`ForgeInternalHandler` now has working listeners for reload, server tick, chunk unload, player login, commands,
tags updated and the network/task events - the machinery a Forge mod's own `@SubscribeEvent` objects need.

The diagnostic that made this fixable is worth keeping: the first attempt returned `null` silently and the log
looked unchanged, so `defineEventBusWrapper` now reports the first three distinct reasons it could not generate
a wrapper (`reportWrapperMiss`), capped so a bad line cannot flood the log.

## Round 98 - three lifecycle errors, the listener wrappers, and a spawner inline that read its own result

**The three startup failures.** `NewRegistryEvent.fill` was dying on `Attentioned duplicate registration of
registry neoforge:entity_data_serializers`; `ForgeMod` never constructed (`NoSuchMethodException:
net.minecraftforge.data.event.GatherDataEvent.<init>()`); and two `NoSuchFieldException: BUS` sites aborted the
Forge custom-registry pass and the datapack declarations.

  * The no-arg constructor probe is `EventListenerHelper.computeListenerList`'s `Class.getConstructor()`, and it
    probes EVERY event ForgeMod subscribes. Fixing `data/event/` only moved the failure to
    `net.minecraftforge.registries.RegisterEvent`; fixing "classes ending in Event" moved it again to
    `TickEvent$ServerTickEvent$Pre`, whose name ends in `$Pre` and whose superclass is its parent event. The
    predicate is now structural: a `net/minecraftforge/` class whose name carries `Event` (as a suffix or as a
    `Event$` segment) chaining to the bus base, `ServerLifecycleEvent`, or its own parent event. 91 constructors
    added, `constructed traditional-Forge baseline mod ForgeMod` for the first time.
  * `getField("BUS")` cannot work on 1.21.1: neither `net.minecraftforge.registries.NewRegistryEvent` nor
    `DataPackRegistryEvent` declares it. `ModLoader.get().postEvent` is no better — `javap` of
    `ModLoader.postEvent` is `getstatic loadingStateValid; ifne 25`, else it logs "Cowardly refusing to send event
    {} to a broken mod state" and returns, and `loadingStateValid` is false here. `ForgeEventPost.post` now finds
    the posting form this line actually has: an `IEventBus` handle from `KernelForgeModContext` (1.21.1's
    `KernelForgeContainers` stores one per mod), then `ModLoader`, then the event's own `BUS` field.
  * The duplicate registry was two different `ResourceKey`s pointing at one id, so key equality did not see it;
    `dropRegistriesTheRootAlreadyHas` dedupes by id text and asks the root through its own methods. The same round
    fixed the generated `__EventDispatcher_*` classes' access: 90 `IllegalAccessError`s, because the bus defines
    them in a loader of its own while `DeferredRegister$EventDispatcher` was package-private.

**The listener wrappers.** Twelve `Error registering event handler` lines were the eventbus asking ModLauncher to
write `<owner package>.__<owner>_<method>_<event>` wrappers during transformation. `ModLauncherFactory` exposes
the other half of that contract (`hasPendingWrapperClass`, `processWrapperClass(String, ClassNode)`), so
`ForbricClassLoader.defineEventBusWrapper` fills the node in and defines it. Two details each cost a build: the
eventbus class is visible through the THREAD CONTEXT loader, and `ClassNode.accept` is declared against
`ClassVisitor`, not `ClassWriter`.

**The spawner inline.** `SpawnPositionCallsInjector` inserted its replacement AFTER the hook call, so its
`ASTORE`s popped the hook's own boolean result and its `ALOAD`s read slots nothing had written. The verifier
named it exactly ("Argument 1: expected Mob, but found ServerLevel") and ASM's frame merge then died with
`ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 0 out of bounds for length 0` on NaturalSpawner and SpawnUtil — which is
why two of the three classes silently kept NeoForge's hook. Two fixes: insert BEFORE the call, and give the
kernel call the fourth argument it always takes (`KernelSpawnPosition.decide`'s `BaseSpawner`), which the plain
position hooks do not have. `decidePosition` and a retarget-in-place fallback remain for classes whose frames
genuinely cannot be recomputed; the log says which path each class took and what the fallback costs.

**Measured after all of it (16:26 launch, 1432 lines):** `anchor is gone` 0, `Attempted duplicate` 0,
`VerifyError` 0, `Missing textures in model` 0, `Error registering event handler` 0, FATAL 0, ledger
`71 of 184 declared repair(s) landed; 99 target(s) were never loaded on this side, and 14 hedge(s) correctly
found nothing to do`. `ReloadableServerResources` was measured to already post MinecraftForge's reload event
itself, so that repair is now a scan rather than a fixed anchor (and it says so once).

**26.2:** the regressions this round introduced were repaired in their tests and mechanisms - `KernelForgeClientInit`'s
lazy `LinkageError` guard, the `PartEntityViews` helper in the part-tracking fixture, the M9 contract test moved to
the scanned-atlas truth, the registration-isolation test moved to `ForgeEventPost`, the consumers lambda fallback
restricted to a renamed NUMBER (`lambda$new$N`, digits only), the silent-catch ceiling re-argued at 672 of 1284.

## Round 99 - the first launch with mods, and the layer that is still missing

Eight real mods installed into `versions/1.21.1-forbric/mods` (JEI for all three ecosystems, fabric-api 0.116.17,
Jade, BetterF3, curios, AppleSkin). The launch reached the title screen - `Setting user`, `Sound engine started`,
`created: 1024x512x4 minecraft:textures/atlas/blocks.png-atlas` - but only after two switches, and for a reason worth
writing down.

**The compatibility dialog blocks unattended launches, by design.** The first modded launch stopped at line 1126 with
no crash and no further output; `jcmd <pid> Thread.print` showed `main` inside
`java.awt.EventQueue.invokeAndWait` <- `DependencyDialogMain.confirmationMain(:225)` <- `DependencyDialogMain.main(:174)`,
a forked `java -cp ... DependencyDialogMain <tsv> --compatibility` waiting for a click. That is the fail-closed
confirmation working exactly as documented (`DependencyDialog` line 53: a closed window or
`-Dforbric.dependencyDialog=off` is a launch that was not approved). Two switches make a launch that nobody is
watching possible, and both matter:

    -Dforbric.compatibilityPolicy=continue   # CompatibilityDecision.Policy.CONTINUE: accept the confirmed losses
    -Dforbric.dependencyDialog=off           # no window at all, so no child JVM to wait on

passed through `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`, because `launch-from-pcl.bat` line 8 carries a fixed `-D` set.

**What the findings actually said.** The report (`%TEMP%\forbric-compatibility*.tsv`, sections
`--compatibility-v1--` / `--suspected--`) named five fabric-api mixins that cannot apply and one curios mixin that
resolves 4 of 5 anchors, missing `@Inject target Inventory.hasAnyMatching(Ljava/util/function/Predicate;)Z`.
Measured: `Inventory` on the merged base has `contains(ItemStack)`, `contains(TagKey)`, `contains(Predicate)` and no
`hasAnyMatching` - and so do BOTH family bases (`run\neoforge-patched\patched-mc-neoforge-1.21.1.jar`,
`run\forge-patched\patched-mc-forge-1.21.1.jar`), so the merge did not drop it: that method is 1.21.2+, and the
curios build behind that jar name targets a newer line. The dialog's `suspected` verdict was right; nothing to repair.

**The real gap: nothing remaps a guest jar's names.** In the same launch, 268 `[Mixin/mixin] Error loading class:
net/minecraft/class_2591` (and class_3218, class_1966, class_6568, class_4766, class_7138, class_2169, class_2248 ...)
plus every fabric-api entrypoint failing with `could not construct entrypoint 'net.fabricmc.fabric.impl.networking.
CommonPacketsImpl::init'`. Both are one fact: a Fabric jar is written in intermediary (`class_1799`), the merged base
is written in Mojang's names, and the kernel loads the jar unchanged. The fabric-api modules are only remapped at
BUILD time, and the build says so in as many words (`build.gradle` line 774: "these jars are compile-time only --
nothing in build/transfer-api-mc is ever loaded").

The machinery for a runtime remap exists and is unwired:
* `net.forbric.kernel.mapping.ForgeModRemapper` (tiny-remapper, SRG -> official) - referenced by its own test and by
  nothing else.
* `net.forbric.kernel.fabric.KernelMappingResolver` - a real resolver with a 5 MB pre-joined table available at
  `E:\project\namespace-probe\mappings-1.21.1.tsv`, read from `-Dforbric.mappings`. It is exposed to mods through
  `FabricLoader.getMappingResolver()`, and NOTHING in the load path asks it anything: with the property set, a
  3,214-line launch contained no `[Forbric/Mappings]` line at all, because `table()` is lazy and no lookup happened.
* Consequence measured the same way: `BlockTransferBridge.install` dies on `NoClassDefFoundError:
  net/minecraft/class_1936` raised while resolving a fabric-api signature, which is why
  `[Forbric/Transfer] initialization failed; foreign storage will not be exposed`.

**Two repairs landed, both small and both verified in that launch.**
* `PairedTransactions` had no `neoFinal(Object)` in the 1.21.1 overlay, so the boot side's contract audit said
  `game-side kernel classes: 125/126 linked`. The method cannot be reached on this line (`neoforge.transfer` does not
  exist here; `TransferTransactionHooks` redirects NeoForge's `callOnRootCommit`, and that anchor is absent), so it is
  present, documented, and says so once if it is ever called. Now `126/126 linked`.
* `KernelBoot.launch` now forces the mapping table to load before mods are prepared, so an absent or unreadable table
  is named at the launch rather than inside fifty mixin errors. With the table present the launch says
  `[Forbric/Mappings] loaded 8220 class, 39655 method and 37497 field mappings ... the resolver now maps intermediary
  to official instead of returning its input`.

**The next round's shape.** Remap guest jars at load, not at build: rewrite each Fabric mod jar (and fabric-api) from
intermediary to official through the same table, cache the result under `.forbric-kernel/remapped/`, and put the
remapped jar on the classpath in place of the original; the Forge side needs the same treatment through
`ForgeModRemapper` (SRG -> official). Only then do the 268 mixin targets, the fabric entrypoints, and the transfer
bridge have names the game actually has. A new anchor also appeared with mods installed and is not yet chased:
`[Forbric/Anchor] forbric-fabric-sound-contracts ... its anchor is gone`.

### Addendum - where the intermediary names actually are

Measured so the next step does not guess: `fabric-api-0.116.17+1.21.1.jar` carries **49 nested module jars** under
`META-INF/jars/` and **zero classes of its own**; the top level holds only `fabric.mod.json` and the nested set. So a
remap aimed at the bundle would do nothing at all. Inside one module
(`META-INF/jars/fabric-lifecycle-events-v1-0.116.17.jar`, 78 classes), **65 classes carry intermediary names in their
constant pools** and 2 json files do (its two mixin configs). That settles the shape of the work: expand the nested
modules, remap each module's bytecode and its mixin configs' string selectors, and hand the remapped modules to the
classpath in place of the bundle. `Jade-1.21.1-NeoForge-15.10.6.jar`, for contrast, packs 301 classes and **none**
of them mentions an intermediary name - the Forge family on this line is Mojmap and stays as-is.

## Round 100 - the remap layer that carries a Fabric guest onto a Mojmap base

The first launch with eight real mods (JEI in three flavours, fabric-api 0.116.17, Jade, BetterF3, curios,
AppleSkin) put the whole Fabric side of the ecosystem in front of the merged base, and 268 `Error loading class:
net/minecraft/class_2591` lines named the reason plainly: the guests are compiled against intermediary, the base
speaks Mojang names, and nothing in the load path translated between them.

What now runs (`net/forbric/kernel/remap/GuestJarRemap.java`), and what each step bought, measured on the same
eight jars:

* **class and text names** - every `.class` through `ClassMapper` + a visitor that rewrites `ldc` strings and
  annotation values (`@Mixin(targets = "net.minecraft.class_2591")` is that shape), every `.json` through a
  token regex. `Error loading class` 268 -> 0; every fabric entrypoint constructs.
* **signatures** - rewriting a class invalidates the jar's digests, so `META-INF/*.SF|RSA|DSA|EC` are dropped and
  the manifest's per-entry digests and `Signature-Version` are cleared. Before this, Bootstrap died in
  `ManifestEntryVerifier` for the first mixin the adapter read.
* **refmap selectors in three passes** - `"Lowner;method_1234(..)V"`, `"Lowner;field_5678:D"` and the bare
  `"field_19315:Ljava/util/Set;"` fabric-api writes without an owner; members resolve through the table's
  `owner#name` rows, and the bare shape falls back to a name-only map with ambiguous names refused. Selectors
  resolved 1074 -> 1306, `InvalidAccessorException` 48 -> 6.
* **candidate copies** - `DuplicateModArbiter` unpacks a multi-loader jar's submodules into
  `.forbric-kernel/candidates`, and those paths sit ahead of the fabric jars on the classpath, so Mixin kept
  reading a 492-byte pristine copy while the remapped one was 612. `remapUnder` covers that directory too.
* **inherited members** - a call site names the static receiver type, so `DefaultedRegistry.holderByNameCodec`
  (filed under `Registry`) missed the qualified lookup; the unqualified fallback resolves it, with lambda names
  excluded because nine intermediary ids share `lambda`.

Two base-side repairs came out of the same launch:

* `mirrorTheDefaultedRegistriesTheMergeWidened` - the merge widened the `BuiltInRegistries` declarations to
  `Registry` while the `<clinit>` still stores `DefaultedRegistry` values, so fabric's field reference died
  with `NoSuchFieldError` in `ItemVariant.<clinit>`. Every widened slot now also exists under the narrower
  descriptor, filled through `KernelRegistryViews.asDefaulted` - a reflective `Proxy`, because the runtime value
  is Forge's `NamespacedWrapper` and a plain cast threw `ClassCastException`. Teaching `NamespacedWrapper` to
  implement `DefaultedRegistry` was tried first and reverted: `GameData.getWrapper` compares
  `instanceof DefaultedRegistry` on both sides and threw `Invalid wrapper minecraft:sound_event was defaulted
  when not expected` for every ordinary registry.
* `aliasTheVanillaLambdaTheGuestCallsByItsOfficialName` - fabric-api-base calls
  `ResourceLocation.lambda(String, String)`; this line's only `lambda` takes
  `(String, ResourceLocationException)`. The alias hands the call to `fromNamespaceAndPath`, which has exactly
  that shape.

Still open, and measured rather than guessed: the mapping table collapses every synthetic lambda of one enclosing
method onto a single official name - nine intermediary ids all read `lambda`, and
`ByteBufCodecs.lambda(ResourceKey)` is the next one in line. A descriptor-aware
selection at remap time (pick the vanilla method whose descriptor matches when the mapped name is a lambda and the
class has no such method) is the fix; the two aliases already landed are point fixes for the first two it hit.
With those two aliases the Bootstrap failure moved from `ResourceLocation.lambda` to
`ByteBufCodecs.lambda` and the client still stops before `Setting user`.
## Round 101 - the lambda table is lossy, and the index that answers by descriptor

The 26.2 census caught the alias repair's anchor: `aliasTheVanillaLambdaTheGuestCallsByItsOfficialName` claimed
`net.minecraft.resources.ResourceLocation` at REQUIRED, and 26.x names that class `Identifier`, so the claim's target
is absent on that line by design. `AnchorLedger.Report.absent()` lists HEDGE anchors too, so hedging the severity did
not help either: the claim now reports `scanned(...)`, which registers no anchor at all, and the repair logs its own
line where it applies. Census subset: 14 tests, 0 failed.

The Bootstrap failure after that is the same shape as `ResourceLocation.lambda$read$0`: the table maps several
intermediary ids of one enclosing method onto one official lambda name, so a name-only answer is right for at most one
of them. `GuestJarRemap` now carries a `MethodIndex` over the base jars and answers by descriptor instead:

- `MethodIndex.methods(owner, descriptor)` reads `<owner>.class` out of each base jar, records every method as
  `BaseMethod(name, access)`, and caches per owner. Reading uses `SKIP_CODE|SKIP_DEBUG|SKIP_FRAMES` — only the
  declaration surface matters.
- `ResolverRemapper.lambdaShape` maps the owner and the descriptor first (ASM hands both over in their *source*
  spelling, which is the trap that made the first attempt a no-op), then: one callable non-lambda candidate wins;
  several callable candidates lose the `try*` variants and, if exactly one "reader" is left, that one wins.
- The base jars for the index are `minecraftLibraries` + the JVM classpath + the merged jar under
  `<library root>/net/forbric/patched-mc-merged`. That root is **not** always `gameDir.getParent()`: on a
  version-isolated instance the game directory is `<instance>/versions/<name>` and the libraries sit one level higher
  (measured: `<instance>/versions/libraries` does not exist). Both roots are now tried.
- Measured effect on the offline probe (one module, the merged base, no launcher): `lambda$read$0(String, String)`
  becomes `fromNamespaceAndPath(String, String)` once the private `createUntrusted` sibling is filtered out by access
  and the public `tryBuild` sibling loses the `try*` tie-break. In the full launch the counts moved from
  `380 replaced / 324 ambiguous` to `139 replaced / 565 ambiguous` — the access filter is stricter, so fewer
  candidates qualify at all, and the lambda that crashed ResourceLocation is gone.

What is still open: `ByteBufCodecs` has three public static candidates for `(ResourceKey)StreamCodec`
(`registry`, `holderRegistry`, `holderSet`) and the table cannot say which one `fromCodecWithRegistries` meant, so the
launch now stops one step later at `ByteBufCodecs.lambda$fromCodecWithRegistries$9(ResourceKey)`. A descriptor alone
cannot separate them. The principled fix is a mapping row keyed by (intermediary name, descriptor) rather than by
name, which the tiny file can supply and the generated TSV currently cannot; that is the next measurement.

## Round 102 - a lambda table that is lossy, and the two passes that read the base instead

The launch now reaches `Setting user = 1` with eight real mods installed (JEI in three ecosystems,
`fabric-api-0.116.17+1.21.1`, Jade, BetterF3, curios, AppleSkin), and `Error loading class` is 0. Three
files carry this round: `GuestJarRemap` (the guest-jar rewrite), `AccessWideners` (new), and
`KernelRegistryViews` (the reflection view over a Forge wrapper).

**The lambda table is lossy, twice over.** `mappings-1.21.1.tsv` holds `class_5321 method_29177
lambda$create$2`, but `ResourceKey.location()` is what the base declares; and nine different ids all map
to `lambda$read$0`. A name-keyed table cannot answer either, so `MethodIndex` reads the merged base's own
class files (`ClassReader.SKIP_CODE|SKIP_DEBUG|SKIP_FRAMES`) and answers by *descriptor*:
`lambdaShape` first asks the base what of that exact shape exists, filters to public non-lambda members,
and accepts a single answer. Measured: 47 names replaced, 14 left alone as ambiguous, 11 named purely by
shape where the table had no row. One detail cost a build: `BaseMethod.callable()` demanded
`public && static`, so `Registry.key()` (access 0x401, no static) was rejected and `method_30517`
survived into `ListenableRegistry`; `callable()` is now public-only and `staticMethod()` keeps the lambda
case. A `@Shadow` member is the same problem in a different place: it carries no selector string, so a
refmap can never fix it. `renameShadows` reads `@Mixin` targets (`@Retention(CLASS)`, therefore in the
*invisible* annotation list — the first attempt read only visible ones and renamed nothing) and renames
each `@Shadow` method whose base declares exactly one method of that shape: `method_29113(T)Optional` →
`getResourceKey`, `method_30517()` → `key`, `lambda$referenceHolderWithLifecycle$6` → `get`.

**A refmap key already says what the value could not.** When a value's member half is an intermediary
name the base does not declare, `useTheKeyWhenTheValueDoesNotResolve` takes the key's member name
(`get`, `getHolder`) and accepts it only if the base declares that name with the value's descriptor.
`SimpleRegistryMixin` went from 6/15 resolved anchors to 10/15 with this and the shadow pass.

**Access wideners, not shadows.** `IllegalAccessError: PackRepository tried to access private field
FolderRepositorySource.packSource` cannot be fixed by a refmap: fabric mods declare an `accessWidener`
in `fabric.mod.json`. `AccessWideners` installs those rules and `ForbricMergedBaseCompatTransformer`
applies them to every class it transforms; 168 members open. The lookup must use the *source* owner from
the rule, not its mapped spelling, or no row is found and the name stays intermediary.

**Two smaller repairs.** `KernelRegistryViews.View.methodOn` searched only the proxy interface's own
methods and missed the super-interfaces (`Registry.holderByNameCodec` is a `default` method on a parent),
then invoked the target on the view instead of the proxy — `object is not an instance of declaring
class`. And `StreamCodec.cast(StreamCodec, Function, StreamCodec, Function, BiFunction)` does not exist:
the base calls that shape `composite`, so `answerTheStreamCodecCastTheGuestCalls` synthesizes the alias.

What is still open: 25 `Failed to apply`, and the client now dies at
`ClassCastException: Pack cannot be cast to FabricResourcePackProfile` inside fabric-resource-loader,
before any registry-sync work happens. The registry-sync mixin reports 10 of 15 anchors, still missing
`@Shadow lambda$freeze$5()V` (ambiguous: many no-arg void methods on `MappedRegistry`) and `@Inject`
targets whose selector strings carry *yarn* class names (`net/minecraft/registry/RegistryKey`) that the
TSV never sees. The refmap `data` section holds yarn-key → intermediary-value pairs, which is the
dictionary that pass would need.

### Round 102, continued - the shadow that already looked official

`ResourcePackProfileMixin` then failed with `ClassCastException: Pack cannot be cast to
FabricResourcePackProfile`, which is the same failure one layer down: its `@Shadow` was
`getChatLink()Lnet/minecraft/server/packs/PackLocationInfo;`, but `Pack` declares `location()` of that
shape and `getChatLink(boolean)Component` of a different one. The table had mapped `class_3288.method_56933`
onto `getChatLink`, so the name *looked* official and the first renamer skipped it — it only considered
intermediary-shaped names. `renameShadows` now verifies every `@Shadow`/`@Overwrite` member by name **and**
shape against the base, and renames whenever the base declares exactly one method of that shape; the class
now carries `location()`, and the mixin applies. The client moved past the cast to
`NullPointerException: this.parentsPredicate is null` in `FabricResourcePackProfile.fabric_isHidden()`,
i.e. the interface is now on the target but the mixin's own constructor body was not merged — the next
thing to measure.

## Round 103 - the table was folding case, and a proxy that only answered one interface

Two failures, both of which had been read as "the guest jar is wrong" for several runs, turned out to be
ours.

The namespace table. `emit-table.ps1` built its joins with PowerShell `@{}` literals, which compare keys
case-insensitively: `lt|f|e` (the obfuscated field `e` of `BuiltInRegistries`) and `lt|f|E` (the field
`E`) were the same key, so `field_41175` came out as `LOOT_FUNCTION_TYPE` instead of `BLOCK`. fabric's
`BlockInitTracker.postFreeze` was then remapped to iterate the loot registry and cast entries to `Block`,
which is the `ClassCastException: LootItemFunctionType cannot be cast to Block` that ended the 22:36 run.
The four maps are now `[hashtable]::new([System.StringComparer]::Ordinal)`, and members join on the
descriptor rather than on the obf member alone, because `client-1.21.1.txt` will place another class's
member row inside a class block (the ByteBufCodecs block carried
`net.minecraft.nbt.NbtAccounter lambda$fromCodec$4() -> c`). Members are first-wins for the same reason.
After regenerating: `C=8,220 M=39,636 F=37,497`, `field_41175 -> BLOCK`, `method_58000 -> list`,
`method_58001 -> holderSet`, `method_58002 -> fromCodecWithRegistriesTrusted`. The registry-sync mixin
still fails on `lambda$freeze$5()V`, which no family declares — that one is not a table error.

The proxy. `KernelRegistryViews` mirrored each widened `BuiltInRegistries` slot with a `Proxy` that
declared exactly one interface (`DefaultedRegistry`, then `ListenableRegistry`). The first consumer that
knows the value as a registry cast it: `RegisterEvent.register` did `(WritableRegistry) registry` and got
`$Proxy34`. Declaring every interface of the wrapped value fixed the cast, but then the default
`Registry.register` re-entered the handler through the proxy and recursed until `register` threw with an
empty message. The handler now resolves declared methods through the class chain only and invokes an
interface default on the real registry, and it rethrows the target's own throwable instead of letting
`InvocationTargetException` turn into `UndeclaredThrowableException` with a null message.

Measured on the 8-mod launch at 23:11:51 (JEI for all three families, fabric-api 0.116.17, Jade,
BetterF3, curios, AppleSkin): 3,886 lines, `Setting user` 1, `cannot be cast to` 0, `Create
level` 0, no new crash report, and the remaining 6 event exceptions are semantic
(`Adding duplicate key 'minecraft:loot_function_type / curios:set_curio_attributes'`), i.e. a
DeferredRegister running twice rather than a proxy being rejected.

26.2 census subset stays 14 / 0.
## Round 104 - a modded client that opens a world

The round before this one ended with the title screen drawing while the window still reported every
frame white. It was not white: a stale capture. `mcp__windows-mcp__Screenshot` with a stale region
returned the DWM surface from before the game presented, and clicking the middle of that "white"
window landed in the world list, which is what finally proved the screen was live. The lesson kept
for later rounds is to locate the window from a full screenshot, then crop.

Everything after that came from one crash report per fix.

**The table filed methods as fields.** `emit-table.ps1` decided a row's kind from the `line:column:`
prefix that `client-1.21.1.txt` prints for some members only, so every method without that prefix went
in as a FIELD keyed `owner|f|returnType|obf`. It then missed the descriptor join and fell through to
the name-only fallback, where one obfuscation letter carries every overload of the class, so the first
row to claim the letter won. `ProfilerFiller.push(String)` became `tee` and resource packs stopped
loading: `NoSuchMethodError: 'void net.minecraft.util.profiling.ProfilerFiller.tee(String)'`. The kind
now comes from whether the row carries an argument list, and methods no longer take the name-only
fallback at all - `GuestJarRemap` answers them from the base by descriptor. Re-emitting the table gave
`C=8,220 M=40,968 F=37,497` in 5,098,762 bytes, 1,332 methods recovered, and the whole
`class_3695` block read `push`/`pop`/`tee`/`popPush`/`endTick` again.

**An interface implementation keeps its intermediary name.** Opening a world died with
`AbstractMethodError: ModResourcePackCreator does not define … 'abstract void loadPacks(Consumer)'`.
The class declared `method_14453`; the table maps that to `loadPacks`, but `ClassRemapper` only looks
a member up under the class that DECLARES it, and a guest class is not in the table. `renameOverrides`
now reads each guest class, takes the superclass and interfaces, and renames a method whose name still
looks intermediary when the base declares exactly one method of that shape under another name. Two
follow-on errors came from the same pass and are worth keeping:

- it read with `ClassReader.SKIP_FRAMES` and wrote with `ClassWriter(0)`, which dropped every
  StackMapTable: `VerifyError: Expecting a stackmap frame at branch target 22` in the very constructor
  it had renamed. Both `renameShadows` and `renameOverrides` now accept with flags 0; renaming does not
  move an offset, so the frames stay valid.
- a reference to an inherited member is written with the SUBCLASS as owner
  (`invokeinterface ModResourcePack.method_56926`), so a per-class rename never reached it. The jar is
  remapped in two passes now: the first records `owner|name|desc -> name` in `OVERRIDES`, the second
  rewrites every reference. When no override was recorded, a reference whose name is still
  `method_NNNN` is answered by the table's name-only lookup, which refuses when two methods share the
  name - `method_56926` has exactly one row, `PackResources.location`.

**Registries were posted twice.** `Adding duplicate key` came from the registry views: the merge
mirrors each widened slot as a same-named field whose value is a proxy, and once that proxy answered
every interface of what it wraps, the field scan counted the same registry twice and posted
`RegisterEvent` twice. `KernelRegistryViews.unwrap` walks a view back to the value it wraps, identity
sets dedupe both scans, and the Forge path went from `x150` to `x81` targets with the duplicate gone.

**Guest mixins whose anchors do not exist.** Where a mixin applies only partially, Mixin still emits
the handler and the interface method it was supposed to implement stays unimplemented, so the first
call throws `UnsupportedOperationException: Implemented via mixin`. Three of them blocked a world:
`fabric-resource-conditions` (an `@Inject` whose handler did not verify), `fabric-loot-api` and
`fabric-data-attachment-api` (builder/attachment interfaces), `fabric-lifecycle-events` (`ServerLevel`
never got its `LoadedChunksCache`). The diagnosis used `-Dforbric.suppressMixins=<config>:<Mixin>` and
then `<config>:*`, which needed `ForbricMixinService` to expand the wildcard from the config JSON.

**Two last links.** `ForgeHooks.readAdditionalLevelSaveData` asks `ModList` for each mod in `level.dat`;
the kernel owns mod loading and deliberately leaves `ModList` unindexed, so that lookup NPE'd before a
world opened - the method is neutered with that reason. And `SpawnPositionCallsInjector` emitted
`Mob.checkSpawnRules(LevelAccessor, EntitySpawnReason)` from a hardcoded descriptor; 1.21.1 names that
parameter `MobSpawnType`, so the injected call site could not link and the first chunk tick threw
`NoClassDefFoundError: EntitySpawnReason`. The descriptor is now read from the hook it inlines.

Measured at 00:48:34 with the same 8 mods: `Setting user: rex888888888`, `Sound engine started` 1,
`EventMux … forwarded 200 frames`, 3,205 log lines, `InvalidMixinException` 101, and a screenshot of
the world: terrain, water, mobs, hotbar, HUD, the vanilla tutorial toast, and a pause menu whose Mods
button opens the kernel's unified list. `jei` appears 168 times in the log, `jade` 35, `curios` 23,
`appleskin` 5, `betterf3` 7.

What is NOT proven by that screenshot: that each mod's own behaviour works. The unified Mods list reads
`Mods (0) - 0 Fabric 0 NeoForge 0 MinecraftForge - 160 not tied to one mod`, so the per-mod rows that
would let a player recognise a mod are still missing, and JEI's recipe screen, Jade's tooltip and
BetterF3's debug overlay have not been opened yet.
## Round 105 - the launch stops depending on the dev flags

Everything in Round 104 was measured with `-Dforbric.mappings` pointing at a probe directory and
`-Dforbric.suppressMixins` naming seven configs. A player starts the game from their launcher, which
passes neither, so the install had to carry both answers itself.

**The table ships beside the instance.** `KernelBoot` now looks for
`mappings-<version>.tsv` in `.forbric-kernel` under the game directory and one and two levels above it
(the launcher layout keeps that directory beside `versions/`), sets the property itself when it finds
one, and loads the resolver after that - the announcement moved below `--gameDir` parsing for exactly
this reason, because loading the table before the directory is known would have cached "no table" and
answered identity for the rest of the session. Measured on a launch with neither property:
`[Forbric/Mappings] using the table shipped beside the instance: …\.forbric-kernel\mappings-1.21.1.tsv`
and `loaded 8220 class, 40968 method and 37497 field mappings`, with `Error loading class` 0 across
3,214 lines.

**The suppressed configs are policy now, not flags.** `ForbricMixinService.DISABLED_GUEST_CONFIGS`
holds the seven configs with the crash report that forced each one, and `suppressedMixinsFor` turns a
matching config off whole. `suppressionSource` reports them as `1.21.1 line: <reason>`, so the load
report still says why a mod's mixins did not run rather than dropping them in silence.

Measured at 00:57:38 with only `-Dforbric.compatibilityPolicy=continue -Dforbric.dependencyDialog=off`
(the two prompts a player answers by clicking): the title screen, then Singleplayer, then the world -
terrain, water, mobs, hotbar, HUD and the tutorial toast all present, and 111 `suppressed mixin` lines
in the log, which is the policy doing what the env var used to.

Still open: the unified Mods list says `Mods (0) - 0 Fabric 0 NeoForge 0 MinecraftForge - 160 not tied
to one mod`, so no mod is named anywhere a player can see, and no mod's own screen (JEI recipes, Jade
tooltips, BetterF3's debug overlay) has been opened yet.
## Round 106 - the Mods list was published before the mods were discovered, and "did not finish loading" counted running mods

**The empty catalogue.** `ModCatalog.publish` had exactly one production caller,
`KernelModCatalog.publish(presence, modsDir)` from `PassiveSeeder`, and that call runs before Fabric
discovery: the log showed `[Forbric/Seed] MinecraftForge LoadingModList seeded empty (zero mods)` at
boot and the NeoForge list only at 01:00:05, so the list the screen read was the empty one. Fixed in
`KernelModLoader` after both `publishNeoModList`/`publishForgeModList` calls and before the construct
phase: forge-family mods plus `ModPresence.fabricMods()`, de-duplicated by id, published together.
Measured: `[Forbric/Catalog] 56 installed mod(s) for the unified Mods screen: 50 Fabric, 5 NeoForge,
1 MinecraftForge`, the title screen's footer became `forbric-v0.1.0-SNAPSHOT (56 mods)`, and the Mods
screen lists AppleSkin, BetterF3, Curios API, Fabric API and its submodules with authors and
descriptions.

**"31 did not finish loading" was 31 running mods.** `ModCatalog.failures()` returns every entry whose
status is not OK - FAILED *and* DEGRADED - and both the screen summary and the log line printed the
whole list under the sentence "did not finish loading". The report body for the same entries already
said "part of this mod did not run", and `aModThatOnlyDegradedIsNotCalledBroken` has pinned that rule
for the file since it was written. Measured on the 8-mod launch: 31 entries, 2 of them entrypoint
failures, every one of the 31 rows reading "part of this mod did not run". The screen now prints
`N did not finish loading` for FAILED and `M running with part left out` for DEGRADED, and the log
splits into the same two sentences (`[Forbric/Load] 31 mod(s) are running with part of themselves left
out: fabric-biome-api-v1, …`), which is what the launch after the fix logged. One assertion in
`KernelLoadReportTest` pinned the old collapse and was corrected, plus a negative assertion that the
sentence is not said about a DEGRADED mod; 41 tests, 0 failed.

Still open: the count is honest now, which does not make the 31 whole - each is a guest mixin that did
not fit the merged base, and `InvalidMixinException` remains at 101. No mod's own screen (JEI recipes,
Jade tooltips, BetterF3's debug overlay, Curios' slots, AppleSkin's hunger bar) has been opened yet.

## Round 107 - the cache key was the bug, and every shadow answer needs the base

Three fixes, one theme: the remapper's own answers were being thrown away.

**The cache was keyed by the guest jar alone.** `remapWithYarn` hashed the input bytes and served
whatever sat under that name, so a jar rewritten by yesterday's remapper came back byte for byte
today. Measured while chasing `@Shadow field field_1887`: the runtime copy of
`.forbric-kernel/remapped/8b87d1d8070d9735-fabric-resource-loader-v0-0.116.17.jar` still declared
`field_1887`, while the same jar rewritten by hand through `RemapProbe` declared `resourcePacks`.
The file's mtime gave it away - it was from a run an hour before the fix. The key now folds in
`CACHE_VERSION` (a constant to bump when the rewriting changes) and the mapping table's size and
mtime, so a regenerated table invalidates too.

**The arbiter's extracted copies were rewritten without the base index.** `remapUnder` had no
`minecraftJars` parameter, so the `candidates/` copies - the ones that precede the Fabric list on
the classpath and that Mixin actually reads - were rewritten with a null `MethodIndex`: every
question of the form "which name does the base really declare for this shape" was answered from the
table alone or not at all. `remapUnder(jars, under, cacheDir, minecraftJars)` now takes the base
jars and `KernelBoot` passes the same list it already gives `remapAll`.

**A shadow whose shape is ambiguous is answered by the table, not left alone.** `pickBaseFieldName`
and `pickBaseName` only adopted a candidate when the base declared exactly one of that shape;
`net.minecraft.client.Options` has many `List` fields, so `field_1887` and `field_1839` stayed
intermediary. `fromTheTable` now asks the table with the owner (unmapping the official name back to
intermediary through `KernelMappingResolver.unmapClassName`), falls back to the owner-less lookup,
and adopts the answer only when the base does declare that name with that shape - the table answers,
the base verifies. Measured on the module before the run: `GameOptionsMixin` came out as
`public java.util.List<String> resourcePacks` and `public KeyMapping[] keyMappings`.

Together: `InvalidMixinException` 101 -> 25, "was not located in the target class" 72 -> 18,
"running with part of themselves left out" 2 -> 1, log 3,217 -> 2,575 lines, `Error loading class`
0, 56 mods in the Mods list, main menu up.

What the remaining 25 are, measured rather than assumed: shapes the merged base genuinely does not
have. `fabric-rendering-v1`'s `BlockColorsMixin` shadows an `IdMapper<BlockColor> field_1995` while
1.21.1's `BlockColors` holds a `Map<Block, BlockColor> blockColors`; `fabric-registry-sync-v0`'s
`ParticleManagerMixin` shadows `Int2ObjectMap<ParticleProvider<?>> field_3835` while 1.21.1's
`ParticleEngine.providers` is a `Map<ResourceLocation, ParticleProvider<?>>`. Renaming cannot fix a
descriptor that disagrees; those stay and are reported.

One finding, not fixed: the shipped `native-only-methods.txt` is derived from the 26.2 base - its
header says `platform neoforge minecraft=26.2 neoforge=26.2.0.88` - and the 1.21.1 jar carries it,
so `NativeAbsentTargets` judges a 1.21.1 mod's version range against 26.2 and logs `curios requires
minecraft >=1.21 <1.22, which ... 26.2 does not satisfy`. The census producer for that table skips on
this line (it needs the three platform jars), so `-Pforbric.censusWrite lineCensus` regenerates the
other tables - `8 tests, 5 skipped, 0 failed`, `vanilla-early-returns.txt` re-derived for 1.21.1 -
but not this one. The per-class digest check keeps its answers safe; the version gate is the part
that misleads.

`lineCensus` on 1.21.1: 8 tests, 5 skipped, 0 failed. 26.2 census subset: 14 tests, 0 failed.
GuestJarRemap + Mods screen + load report tests on 1.21.1: 23 tests, 0 failed.
## Round 108 - the table's versions belong to the base it was derived on

`NativeAbsentTargets.dropsNatively` asked two questions of a guest injector: does the mod's platform lack
every target (a native drop Mixin swallows), and is the mod native to the game the table describes (its
manifest's mandatory ranges must admit that game's version). The second was asked first, and it was asked
of a table that records 26.2: on the 1.21.1 client it read

    [Forbric/Mixin] curios requires minecraft >=1.21 <1.22, which NeoForge's patched 26.2
    (minecraft 26.2, neoforge 26.2.0.88) does not satisfy, so its own loader would not run it on the
    game native-only-methods.txt describes

about a mod that runs on that client, whose mixins were applying. The base check that would have refused
the whole table (`speaksFor`: the serving jar's members digest against the `base` line) came last, so the
version claim was made before anything established the table had any authority here.

Base first, versions second. `versionsApply` (package-private, and the only caller) is `speaksFor`, and
`dropsNatively` returns before the requirement step when it is false: the table has no authority over
another build, so the versions on its `platform` lines decide nothing about that build's mods, and no line
is written about them. The cost is one members digest for a jar whose misses reach this far — already
cached per jar in `ForbricMixinService.BASE_DIGESTS`, about a quarter of a second for the merged base once.

Measured on the eight-mod 1.21.1 client: `does not satisfy` 1 -> 0, and nothing else moved —
`InvalidMixinException` 25, unlocated shadows 18, `Error loading class` 0, log 2,575 lines, main menu up.

Pinned by `theVersionsAreOnlyReadForTheBaseTheTableRecords`: the recorded base is spoken for, another base
is not, and a context with no digest is not either. `NativeAbsentTargetsTest` 22 tests, 0 failed; 26.2
census subset 14 tests, 0 failed.

Still open, and named so it is not mistaken for progress: the shipped `native-only-methods.txt` remains
26.2's table, so on this line the rows are never applied at all - every mixin miss on 1.21.1 counts as the
merge's, which is what the log has been saying all along. Giving that table a 1.21.1 derivation needs the
three platform jars for the line (the census skips here for exactly that reason), so it stays a debt.
## Round 109 - what the data-attachment policy costs, measured and not shipped

`fabric-data-attachment-api-v1` is the one mod the load report still calls failed, and the reason is our
own policy: `DISABLED_GUEST_CONFIGS` turns its whole config off, so the accessor
`CustomPayloadS2CPacketAccessor` is "missing from fabric-data-attachment-api-v1.mixins.json" and
`AttachmentChange.<clinit>` dies with `IllegalClassLoadError`, taking the entrypoint with it.

Removing the two entries and booting measured this: `IllegalClassLoadError` 0, no entrypoint failure,
`Implemented via mixin` 0, `InvalidMixinException` 25, unlocated shadows 18, log 2,497 lines (down from
2,575), 29 mods degraded, main menu up. The kernel's own fit analysis leaves out only the two UNFIT mixins
(`BannerBlockEntityMixin`, `ChunkDataSenderMixin`), so 13 of the 15 would apply, accessors included.

The half that decides it - `ChunkAccess.fabric_shouldTryToSync` and `fabric_readAttachmentsFromNbt` on a
real chunk - cannot be reached without loading a world, and this session could not drive the client:
`mcp__windows-mcp__Click` and a direct `PostMessage(WM_LBUTTONDOWN)` to the window handle both left the
log untouched, and the window's GL surface presented white or black throughout. No click reached the
game, so no world was loaded, so nothing about chunk load was measured.

Reverted rather than shipped: the entry is back, the rebuilt jar is byte-identical to `86adc36`'s, and the
boot-only measurement above is recorded as a candidate (13 of 15 mixins instead of 0) rather than a fix.
The measurement that would settle it is one world load with the entries removed, and it needs a client
that accepts input.
## Round 110 - the overlay never came down because a Yarn-shaped mixin rebuilt a full id, and the world loads now

For three rounds the client sometimes reached the main menu and sometimes sat forever behind the Mojang
splash, with no crash report and a render loop still pumping frames. The difference was not input and not
a freeze: `jcmd <pid> Thread.print` put the render thread in `glfwWaitEventsTimeout` under
`RenderSystem.limitDisplayFPS`, and the tail of `latest.log` held the real exception:

```
net.minecraft.ResourceLocationException: Non [a-z0-9/._-] character in path of location:
    neoforge:neoforge:shaders/core/rendertype_entity_unlit_translucent.vsh
    at ResourceLocation.assertValidPath(ResourceLocation.java:284)
    at ResourceLocation.createUntrusted(ResourceLocation.java:48)
    at ResourceLocation.fromNamespaceAndPath(ResourceLocation.java:52)
    at ShaderInstance.getOrCreate(ShaderInstance.java:186)
```

The namespace had been prefixed twice. Dumping the transformed class
(`-Dforbric.dumpClass=ShaderInstance`) and reading it with `javap -p -c` showed why: fabric-rendering-v1
injects `localvar$zgp000$fabric-rendering-v1$modifyStageId(String, ResourceProvider, Program$Type, String)`
into `getOrCreate`, and that visitor hands back a *full* id when the stage already contains a colon:

```
if (aload_3.contains(":")) return FabricShaderProgram.rewriteAsId(aload_0, aload_3).toString();
else return aload_0;
```

Its companion `wrapOperation$zgp000$fabric-rendering-v1$allowNoneMinecraftId(String, Operation)` takes one
string, which is Yarn's `Identifier.of(String)`. The merged base is Mojmap with the NeoForge patch, so the
call after that visitor is the two-argument `fromNamespaceAndPath(namespace, path)` -- and the namespace is
applied to a string that already carries one. The shader fails to build inside `RegisterShadersEvent`, the
first resource reload never completes, and the loading overlay stays on screen forever. A base run whose
`RegisterShadersEvent` completed before the failure looks fine, which is why the same jar reached the menu
one launch and not the next.

The repair is one call: `KernelShaders.stageId(String namespace, String stage)` parses the stage when it is
already an id and only prefixes it when it is a bare name. It lives in `src/runtime/java`, not in the 1.21.1
overlay -- `normalizeRuntimeJava` refuses an overlay file whose base counterpart does not exist, which is a
good rule: an overlay replaces, it does not add. `readTheStageIdTheGuestRebuiltAsTheIdItAlreadyIs` rewrites
the single `ResourceLocation.fromNamespaceAndPath` call site inside `ShaderInstance.getOrCreate` to call it,
and reports `scanned()` rather than `fixed()` because a base with no such guest never takes that path.

Measured with 8 mods (`03:06:17`, `launch-quickplay.bat`, 3,951 lines, no crash report):

- `Non [a-z0-9` 0 -- the shader exception is gone.
- `Preparing spawn area: 0%`, `Time elapsed`, `Loaded recipes`, and a live server thread:
  `[Forbric/Tick] 3700 tick(s): mean 49.99ms (a server keeping up holds 50), max 237.11ms,
  at twice the budget or worse 4 (0.1%)`. The world is loaded and running.
- The loot bridge found its 1.21.1 seam at last:
  `[Forbric/LootBridge] routed the loot-table load site in LootDataType through the kernel -- this line
  loads loot tables there and has no TagLoader.loadTagsForRegistry anywhere in the base`. REPLACE and
  MODIFY listeners now fire; ALL_LOADED still has nothing to fire it, and the anchor keeps saying so
  instead of claiming three events where one is possible.
- Ledger unchanged at `103 of 197 declared repair(s) landed, and 2 did NOT`; the two remaining entries are
  the ALL_LOADED half above and `forbric-fabric-sound-contracts` on `SoundInstance`.

Reaching the world without a mouse also needed a channel: `--quickPlaySingleplayer "test01 (1)"`, inserted
before `--libraryPath` in a copy of the launcher script, because a flag after that argument is swallowed.
`[Forbric/Args] 28 forwarded game argument(s), of which 14 are flags` proved the argument survives
`KernelBoot`'s forwarding. Pixels on this machine cannot be trusted for verification -- the desktop capture
returns stale frames for this window and the GL surface often reads as white -- so the proof above is the
log: a spawn-area line and a tick line are things only a loaded world emits.

Not measured yet: what the thirteen data-attachment mixins would add on top of this (they were reverted
last round for lack of exactly this world load), JEI's recipe screen, curios' slots, AppleSkin's hunger bar.

## Round 111 - a mixin's own selectors are a statement the kernel has to read

Three separate holes were measured on the 1.21.1 line in one sitting, and each of them had the same shape: a guest
says what it means in Yarn, the merged base answers in official names, and the step between them was missing.

1. **`@Inject(method = "updateEntries")`.** fabric-item-group-api-v1's `ItemGroupMixin` resolves through its own
   refmap at runtime, but that refmap records intermediary on *both* sides of its dictionary
   (`"Lnet/minecraft/class_1761;method_47306(…)V": "Lnet/minecraft/class_1761;method_47306(…)V"`), so a hit is not an
   answer. The kernel's anchor analysis read the selector literally, called the target missing, and relaxed the
   injection away; opening the creative inventory then died with `IllegalStateException: Item group has no page`
   because the injection that would have set the page had been switched off by us. `GuestJarRemap` now reads each
   mixin's `mappings` block, maps the member through the table, rewrites the annotation string, and drops the owner
   when it names the mixin's own target class (Mixin refuses a qualified selector in `@Inject`: "specifies a target
   class 'net/minecraft/class_481', which is not supported"). Verified offline: `ItemGroupMixin` now carries
   `method=["Lnet/minecraft/world/item/CreativeModeTab;buildContents(Lnet/minecraft/world/item/CreativeModeTab$ItemDisplayParameters;)V"]`
   and the creative screen's mixin carries `method=["init()V"]`.
2. **The member may live on a superclass.** `method_25426` is recorded under `class_437` (Screen) alone, so an
   owner-aware lookup answers "unchanged" for a selector written against `class_481`. The owner-less table answers,
   and the result is only written when the base really declares that name with that descriptor.
3. **`@At(target = …)` is a different contract.** Rewriting those looked right and measured wrong: 52 hard
   `InvalidInjectionException` failures appeared ("specifies a target class … which is not supported"), and the
   half-applied hooks they left behind aborted world opening with
   `NullPointerException: TheEndBiomeSource.fabric_modifyBiomeSet … overrides null`. That pass is now behind
   `-Dforbric.rewriteAtTargets=true` and off by default.

Everything here is bounded by one rule: **an answer the base does not confirm is not written**. `baseDeclares(owner,
name, descriptor)` gates every rewritten selector; the table's keys carry no descriptor, so an overloaded name can
otherwise map to a name the base does not have with that shape.

Two consequences recorded in the same run:

- `KernelRegistryViews.listenable` now asks the registry whether it *answers* (`fabric_getAddObjectEvent` returns an
  event) instead of trusting that it implements the interface. A partially applied `SimpleRegistryMixin` (12 of 15
