  anchors) leaves the interface present and the field null, and `Bootstrap` died with
  `NullPointerException: Event.register(Object) … RegistryEntryAddedCallback.event(...) is null`. The type lookup also
  had to move to the kernel's own loader: the game loader cannot see fabric-api, so `type(LISTENABLE, …)` returned
  null and the registry was handed back untouched.
- fabric-biome-api-v1's whole mixin config is off on this line, with the measurement written next to it: the biome
  sources kept a hook whose backing field is never assigned, and opening a world died in
  `TheEndBiomeSource.fabric_modifyBiomeSet` on a null `overrides`. With the config off the world opens again
  (`Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick]` 1900 ticks at mean 49.98ms).

Measured state after the run: world opens and the integrated server ticks, but the **client is still disconnected**
during the configuration handshake — `NoSuchFieldError: ServerConfigurationPacketListenerImpl.field_45012` from
fabric-networking-api-v1's server configuration addon (`[Forbric/Anchor] forbric-common-network-interop#fabricAddonHandle
was handed … ServerConfigurationNetworkAddon and made …`). That is the next hole: a `@Shadow` field of a guest class
that the remapper did not rename.

## Round 112 - a mixin that reads an inherited field declares nothing, and fabric names its registries the other way round

Two holes, both found from the same run.

**1. An inherited field read through the mixin's own name.** The client died with
`NoSuchFieldError: Class net.minecraft.server.network.ServerConfigurationPacketListenerImpl does not have member
field net.minecraft.server.MinecraftServer field_45012`. The mapping table says
`F net/minecraft/class_8609 field_45012 server`, and `class_8609` is `ServerCommonPacketListenerImpl` - the field is
declared on the parent, and the mixin carries no `@Shadow` declaration at all, so the descriptor-indexed shadow pass
had nothing to rename. A byte scan of the rewritten guest jars found exactly three classes referencing the name
(fabric-resource-loader-v0's `ServerConfigurationNetworkHandlerMixin`, fabric-networking-api-v1's
`ServerConfigurationNetworkHandlerMixin` and `ServerPlayNetworkHandlerMixin`), each as
`getfield field_45012:Lnet/minecraft/server/MinecraftServer;` on itself.

`GuestJarRemap.reachableName(index, superName, name, descriptor, method)` now answers those: the name must look like an
intermediary member, the owner-less table must give exactly one answer, and the base must confirm that answer is
reachable from the mixin's superclass (`declares(superName, descriptor, mapped)` for methods,
`fieldsOfShape(superName, descriptor).contains(mapped)` for fields). Anything less stays visible as a failure instead of
being guessed.

The first attempt still died, on the sibling hole `field_45013` (`ServerGamePacketListenerImpl`, `connection`), because
`renameShadows` returned early when no `@Shadow` member had been renamed: the access loop that calls `reachableName`
never ran. That early return is gone; the method now tracks whether anything actually changed and only then rewrites the
class. With both fixed, the client passes configuration, the server places the player, and the world runs.

**2. fabric's payload registries are named protocol-first on this line.** After the field fix the client got through
configuration and was disconnected while encoding `minecraft:register`: `ClassCastException: RegistrationPayload cannot
be cast to MinecraftRegisterPayload`. `PayloadInterop.fabricRegistryField` asked for `CLIENTBOUND_CONFIGURATION` /
`SERVERBOUND_CONFIGURATION` / `CLIENTBOUND_PLAY` / `SERVERBOUND_PLAY`; fabric 1.21.1's `PayloadTypeRegistryImpl`
declares `CONFIGURATION_C2S`, `CONFIGURATION_S2C`, `PLAY_C2S`, `PLAY_S2C` (protocol first, flow second), so every lookup
returned null, the fabric codec was never found, and the proxy fell back to NeoForge's codec for a fabric payload.
`fabricRegistryFields(protocol, flow)` now returns both spellings and the two callers take the first non-null field.

**Measured, 04:45 run, 8 mods installed, quick-play into `test01 (1)`:**

| reading | before | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `field_45012` / `field_45013` | 1 + 1 fatal | 0 |
| `lost connection` | 1 per run | 0 |
| `Failed to encode custom payload` | 1 | 0 |
| integrated server | died at `placeNewPlayer` | 1300+ ticks, mean 49.95 ms |
| creative inventory (E) | `IllegalStateException: Item group has no page` | opens, 41 pages, tooltip resolves |
| log | - | 2,736 lines, process still alive |

Screenshots taken with the game's own F2 (the desktop capture path returns a white or stale frame for this window):
`2026-11-09_04.49.57.png` is the world at night with the hotbar and the tutorial toast, and `2026-11-09_04.50.16.png` is
the creative inventory with 41 pages - the fabric item-group page field is now written, which is what the selector
rewrite in Round 111 was for. `2026-11-09_04.50.55.png` shows the pause menu with the kernel's Mods redirection.

Still not proven: JEI's recipe screen, curios slots, AppleSkin's hunger overlays and BetterF3's debug screen have not
been opened. `InvalidMixinException` is 35 and `InvalidInjectionException` 5 on this run, and `29 mod(s) are running with
part of themselves left out`.

Note on the census subset: on this line `MergedBaseRepairClaimsStagedTest` and `RepairDriftCensusStagedTest` fail with
eight `Absent` claims for classes that only exist in the 26.2 tree (`client.renderer.block.dispatch.BlockStateModel`,
`client.gui.render.GuiRenderer`, ...). That is not from this round - the same two tests fail on the committed tree with
these changes stashed, because both tests hardcode the 26.2 staged jars.

## Round 113 - an @Inject selector names a method of the target, so the owner always goes

Measured first, on `08c8a17`: `InvalidInjectionException` 5 and `did not attach` 141 on one boot, and the mixin named
in every one of those five lines is fabric-rendering-v1's `WorldRendererMixin`, applied to
`net.minecraft.client.renderer.LevelRenderer`:

    @Inject annotation on onReload specifies a target class 'net/minecraft/client/render/WorldRenderer', which is not supported

`javap -v` on the rewritten jar shows the value it is complaining about is constant #323,
`Lnet/minecraft/client/render/WorldRenderer;reload()V` - official names, but with a class qualifier. The earlier
`unqualified` only stripped the qualifier when it matched one of the mixin's `@Mixin` targets, so a selector whose
refmap answer named a *different* class kept the qualifier and Mixin rejected the whole mixin: all nine of its
injections went with it.

Three sources answer a selector and the strip now happens after all of them, in `resolveSelector`:

1. the refmap, which is the guest's own statement of what its authored name means;
2. the table read straight off the selector, for a mixin whose refmap has no key for it (this is the `#323` case:
   nothing to add, so the string as written has to be stripped);
3. the string as written.

`unqualified` then drops `Lowner;` from anything shaped like `name(descriptor)return`.

Result on the same eight-mod client: `InvalidInjectionException` **5 -> 0**, `did not attach` **141 -> 131**, and
`WorldRendererMixin` now applies at 16/25 anchors instead of being rejected outright. `InvalidMixinException` stays at
35 - those are the `@Shadow` fields whose *shape* differs (fabric wants `IdMapper<BlockColor> field_1995` where the
1.21.1 base has `Map<Block, BlockColor> blockColors`), which renaming cannot fix. The world still loads:
`screenshots/2026-11-09_05.27.33.png`.

Two things this round did **not** fix, both measured rather than guessed:

- The F3 debug screen never appears, with or without BetterF3. BetterF3 was renamed out of `mods/` and the client
  restarted (`betterf3` 0 mentions); F3 still drew nothing (`screenshots/2026-11-09_05.14.40.png`). The vanilla path
  was read to the instruction: `KeyboardHandler.keyPress` offset 664 branches on the screen, 680 compares the key
  against 292, and 702-709 call `DebugScreenOverlay.toggleOverlay()`. F11 on the same key channel switches the window
  to full screen and F2 writes a screenshot from it, so the keys do arrive - only the overlay's rendering is missing.
- Curios' slots do not appear. `MixinInventory` is applied 4/5 and the missing anchor is reported as
  `Inventory.hasAnyMatching(Ljava/util/function/Predicate;)Z`, but `javap` on the mod's jar shows the handler body
  calling `Container.hasAnyMatching` - the warning is our own MixinFit reading a handler's call as an anchor. The mod's
  client UI leaves no log line either way, so this is still unexplained. JEI's recipe screen, AppleSkin's hunger
  overlays and the creative inventory are proven: `2026-11-09_04.54.39.png`, `04.55.39.png`, `04.50.16.png`.
## Round 114 - the sound contract guard tested something a transform can never see

The ledger read `103 of 197 declared repair(s) landed, and 2 did NOT` on this line, and one of the two was
`forbric-fabric-sound-contracts on net.minecraft.client.resources.sounds.SoundInstance: native default streaming must
dispatch Fabric sound overrides`, with the per-class line:

    [Forbric/Anchor] forbric-fabric-sound-contracts was handed net.minecraft.client.resources.sounds.SoundInstance and
    made no edit - its anchor is gone. native default streaming must dispatch Fabric sound overrides

`javap` on the base shows why the repair is needed at all: `SoundInstance.getStream(SoundBufferLibrary, Sound,
boolean)` is a *default* method that only does `library.getStream(sound.getPath(), looping)`, so a mod overriding
Fabric's `FabricSoundInstance.getAudioStream(SoundBufferLibrary, Identifier, boolean)` is never asked. Fabric API
closes the gap with two mixins: `SoundInstanceMixin` (a bare interface that adds `FabricSoundInstance`) and
`SoundSystemMixin` (which calls it). On this base the second one resolves no anchor and is suppressed - the log says
so: `auto-suppressing guest mixin fabric-sound-api-v1 … SoundSystemMixin - UNFIT on the merged base (no anchor
resolves (@Inject target SoundEngine.play …))`. So the interface gets added and the promise behind it is never kept.

The transformer declined because of its own guard: `if (!node.interfaces.contains(API)) return bytes;`. Transforms run
before any mixin, so `SoundInstance` never lists `FabricSoundInstance` at that moment; the guard was false on every
line, forever. It was replaced with a body that is correct either way: the default method now calls
`KernelSounds.stream(self, library, sound, looping)`, which asks the instance reflectively (`getAudioStream`, cached
after the first lookup) and falls back to `library.getStream(sound.getPath(), looping)` when no override exists. Both
spellings of the id live where the project keeps them: `src/runtime/java` uses `Identifier` (26.2), the
`mc-line/1.21.1` overlay uses `ResourceLocation`.

Measured on the eight-mod client after the change: the ledger reads `104 of 197 declared repair(s) landed, and 1 did
NOT`; the remaining one is `forbric:loot-table-event-bridge on net.minecraft.server.ReloadableServerRegistries`, whose
ALL_LOADED half has no seam on this line (the loot call itself is routed in `LootDataType`, see Round 110). The class
is live in the JVM - `jcmd <pid> VM.class_hierarchy net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelSounds` answers
`|--net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelSounds`, which only happens once `getStream` has been called. The run itself:
2,669 log lines, `Preparing spawn area` 1, `lost connection` 0, `InvalidInjectionException` 0,
`InvalidMixinException` 35, `Sound engine started` 1, no `NoSuchMethodError` and no exception mentioning a sound.
## Round 115 - F3 does not open the debug screen, and the earlier measurements were on the death screen

What was measured, in order, because the first three answers were wrong:

1. `mcp__windows-mcp__Screenshot` region captures returned stale frames; the full-desktop
   capture showed DSH's own colour-picker overlay sitting on top of the game and eating the
   keyboard. Minimising DSH (`ShowWindow(hwnd, 6)`) removes it. That is why several earlier
   rounds concluded "the key never arrives".
2. The key DOES arrive: F11 switches the window to fullscreen and F2 writes a screenshot in
   the same channel (`screenshots/2026-11-09_05.05.05.png`, 1920x1080  world frame).
   Focus must be re-asserted immediately before every key: the pwsh console grabs the
   foreground between statements, and the pause that follows makes F3 a no-op by design.
3. Some of those "F3 does nothing" runs were the **death screen** ("You Died!
   rex888888888 was slain by Husk") - the world was left in survival at night, the player
   died while the script fought for focus, and a screen is open, which is exactly the branch
   of `keyPress` that skips the toggle. `saves/test01 (1)/playerdata/*.dat` was removed to
   get a live player back.

The real code path, from `javap -p -c` on `KeyboardHandler` dumped out of the running client
with `-Dforbric.dumpClass=net.minecraft.client.KeyboardHandler`:

    675: iload 12;  ifeq 949          // screen == null || (PauseScreen && !showsPauseMenu)
    680: iload_3;   sipush 292; if_icmpne 949   // F3
    687: getfield handledDebugKey; ifeq 702
    694..699: handledDebugKey = false; goto 949 // a debug combo already used this press
    702: getDebugOverlay(); <injected handlers>; toggleOverlay()

With BetterF3 installed the dump shows BetterF3's own handler wrapped around that call:

    733: invokespecial handler$zkb000$betterf3$animationAndAlwaysEnableProfiler(JIIII, CallbackInfo)
    738: isCancelled(); ifeq 745
    744: return
    745: invokevirtual DebugScreenOverlay.toggleOverlay()

and `me/cominixo/betterf3/mixin/KeyboardMixin.animationAndAlwaysEnableProfiler` ends at
offset 190 with an **unconditional `CallbackInfo.cancel()`**, after calling
`DebugScreenOverlay.toggleOverlay()` itself on the branches it keeps:
`GeneralOptions.disableMod` (offset 180) / `!enableAnimations` (60) /
`enableAnimations && !showDebugScreen()` (37..54). The branch that keeps nothing is
`enableAnimations && showDebugScreen()` - it sets `Utils.closingAnimation = true` and cancels.

So BetterF3 alone cannot explain it: with `BetterF3-11.0.3-Forge-1.21.1.jar.disabled`
(the log then has 0 `betterf3` mentions, `Preparing spawn area` 1) pressing F3 in a live
world still shows no overlay, and F2 in the same channel still writes a screenshot
(`screenshots/2026-11-09_06.02.28.png`; the player was at half a heart in the dark, so the
scene is nearly black).

What is therefore NOT yet established: whether `toggleOverlay()` runs and the overlay fails
to render, or whether the branch is never taken at all. The next measurement has to be on
the overlay itself - dump `DebugScreenOverlay` the same way and compare `render`, and if the
bytecode is intact, log the state of `renderDebug` from a runtime helper instead of guessing
from pixels. BetterF3 was restored to the instance after the control run.

Test channel notes for the next round: re-assert foreground right before each key; never
trust a screenshot taken after a click, because the click itself can hand focus away and
pause the game; `mcp__windows-mcp__Click` defaults to the virtual desktop, so add the
window origin from `ClientToScreen`.

## Round 116 - the false positives behind "F3 is broken", and the first clean measurement

The focus channel is finally understood, and it invalidates every earlier F3 conclusion:

* `ShowWindow(game, 6)` / `(9)` - the only way that reliably gave the game the keyboard - **also
  makes singleplayer pause**, and the pause screen is exactly the branch of `KeyboardHandler.keyPress`
  that skips the debug toggle. F2 keeps working while paused because the screenshot keybind is
  checked at offset 297, *before* the `screen == null` gate at 594/675. That asymmetry is why the
  earlier rounds saw "F2 works, F3 does nothing" and blamed the base.
* Worse, the test world was left in survival at night: three "measurements" were taken on the
  **death screen** (`You Died! rex888888888 was slain by Husk / ... by Skeleton`). A `DeathScreen`
  instance was visible in `jcmd GC.class_histogram`, which is how it was finally caught.
* The right way to focus without pausing is `AttachThreadInput(foregroundThread, gameThread, true)`
  + `SetForegroundWindow(game)` + detach: no minimise, no focus loss, no pause.

With that, the test world was moved to `difficulty peaceful` and `gamemode creative` (the
`/gamemode creative` had to be re-issued after a death, because death respawns into the level's
default gamemode). The world is now stably usable for UI measurements.

The clean measurement: foreground held by the game, `jcmd GC.class_histogram` shows **no Screen
instance**, the frame is the plain world, and F3 still produces no overlay
(`screenshots/2026-11-09_06.11.34.png`, `..._06.11.59.png` after three presses). F2 in the same
burst writes a screenshot. So the toggle genuinely does not happen, with a live player, in game,
with BetterF3's own handler in the path and without it.

Bytecode facts already established this round, both from dumped runtime classes:

* `DebugScreenOverlay.toggleOverlay()` is intact: `renderDebug = !renderDebug`.
* `DebugScreenOverlay.showDebugScreen()` is `renderDebug && !options.hideGui`, with BetterF3's
  `shouldRenderDebug` RETURN injector in front.
* `KeyboardHandler.keyPress` needs, in this order: `screen == null` (or a PauseScreen whose menu
  is hidden), `key == 292`, `!handledDebugKey`; then `getDebugOverlay().toggleOverlay()`.
* BetterF3 wraps that call with its own handler and unconditionally `CallbackInfo.cancel()`s at
  its offset 190, after calling `toggleOverlay()` itself on three of its four branches. So with
  BetterF3 installed the vanilla call never runs and everything depends on BetterF3's branches -
  but the same no-overlay result appears with the jar renamed away, so this is not the whole story.

Next measurement, named so it is not guessed again: add a `-Dforbric.probeKeys` transformer that
logs, at `KeyboardHandler.keyPress` entry, the key, the action, `minecraft.screen` and
`handledDebugKey`. That distinguishes the two candidates that are still possible - the branch is
never reached, or it is reached with `handledDebugKey` stuck true - without another pixel argument.

## Round 117 - a key probe that does not fire, and what that rules out

The probe promised at the end of Round 116 was written and installed:

* `src/main/java/net/forbric/kernel/transform/KeyPressProbeTransformer.java`, registered at
  `TransformPhase.COREMOD` only under `-Dforbric.probeKeys=true`, adds three loads and one
  `INVOKESTATIC net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelKeyProbe.record(Object,int,int)` at the head of
  `KeyboardHandler.keyPress`.
* `src/runtime/java/net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelKeyProbe.java` logs, once per distinct key and
  action, that the method was entered and then reads `minecraft.screen` and `handledDebugKey` back
  from the handler instance by reflection.

What the measurements say:

* The transform does run: `[Forbric/Probe] keyPress is being watched` appears at startup.
* The transformed class really carries the call: `-Dforbric.dumpClass=net.minecraft.client.KeyboardHandler`
  writes a class whose `keyPress` starts with
  `invokestatic #830 // Method net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelKeyProbe.record:(Ljava/lang/Object;II)V`.
* The helper really is resolved in the running JVM: `jcmd <pid> VM.class_hierarchy
  net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelKeyProbe` answers with the class, and the runtime jar that holds
  it is the newest one under `.forbric-kernel/lib/`.
* And yet pressing F3, E, W or F1 produces **no probe line at all**, while F2 still writes
  screenshots into `screenshots/`.

So the diagnostic itself is now the suspect, not the key path: either the call returns before its
first `ForbricLog.info`, or the log call does not reach `latest.log` from that class. That is the
next thing to measure (a `System.err.println` instead of `ForbricLog`, which cannot depend on any
logging configuration), and until it is measured, neither "the event never arrives" nor "the branch
is skipped" may be written down as the cause of F3.

Nothing about the client changed this round: the probe is off unless the property is passed, and the
ledger still reads `104 of 197 declared repair(s) landed, and 1 did NOT`.

## Round 118 - F3 works; Snipaste was holding the key

The probe added in Round 117 finally answered the question, and the answer was
not a code defect.

The probe now writes to the file named by `-Dforbric.probeFile` instead of going
through `ForbricLog`, because runtime-side `ForbricLog` cannot reach log4j and
falls back to `System.err`, and the stock launcher does not capture a game's
stderr into `logs/latest.log`. With the file sink the probe printed:

    keyPress entered key=291 action=1        (F2, screenshot taken)
    keyPress entered key=69  action=1        (E, inventory opened)
    keyPress entered key=256 action=1        (Esc, pause screen)
    keyPress state   key=292 action=1 screen=net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.PauseScreen

So `key=292` (F3) does reach `KeyboardHandler.keyPress` - but only once an
overlay tool named Snipaste was stopped. That tool registers F3 as a global
hotkey (paste image from clipboard), so the key never reached the window in any
earlier run; the same tool's floating "Paster" window also sat on top of the
game window and swallowed clicks, which is why clicks and keys kept
"disappearing" during testing. After `Stop-Process` on it, F3 arrived at once.

The second half of the explanation is the screen gate measured in Round 116:
with a screen open, `keyPress` skips the overlay toggle by design. Both halves
are visible in the same probe line above - the key arrived, the screen was a
`PauseScreen`, and the overlay was therefore not toggled.

With no screen open and no overlay tool running, F3 produces the debug HUD:
`screenshots/2026-11-09_06.41.33.png` shows the block info card ("Sand /
minecraft") and the debug crosshair, both of which are drawn only while the
debug overlay is on.

Nothing in the kernel was wrong here. The probe stays in the tree behind
`-Dforbric.probeKeys` (off by default) because it is cheap and it turns "the key
does nothing" into a state reading.
## Round 119 - the curios button exists, and the screen event path is intact

The curios button never appears in the survival or creative inventory, so this
round measured the path that is supposed to put it there.

The merged base posts both screen-init events from
`Screen.init(Minecraft, int, int)`: `ScreenEvent$Init$Pre` before `init()` and
`ScreenEvent$Init$Post` after it, each constructed with the screen, its
`children` list wrapped in `Collections.unmodifiableList`, and two
`Consumer<GuiEventListener>` created by `invokedynamic` over the screen. So the
NeoForge patch survived the merge - it is not a missing hook.

`addListener` does what the name says: in
`neoforge-runtime-1.21.1.jar`, `ScreenEvent$Init.addListener(GuiEventListener)`
is `getfield add; aload_1; invokeinterface Consumer.accept`, and the consumer is
the one handed in by `Screen.init`. A mod that calls `event.addListener(...)`
therefore lands in the screen's own widget list.

Curios gets that far: `jcmd GC.class_histogram` shows one `CuriosButton`
instance after the inventory is opened, and the log carries no missing texture,
no exception from `GuiEventHandler`, and no `curios:textures` lookup at all.

What is not yet known is whether the widget reaches `children` and what
coordinates it renders at. `GuiEventHandler.onInventoryGuiInit` computes
`getGuiLeft() + a - 2` and `getGuiTop() + b + (creative ? 67 : 81)` with `a`/`b`
from `CuriosScreen.getButtonOffset(creative)`, and that method adds
`ButtonCorner` offsets to the two config values; the instance config is
`buttonCorner = "TOP_LEFT"`, both offsets 0. The offsets baked into the enum are
`(26, -75, 73, -62)`, `(61, -75, 95, -62)`, `(26, -20, 73, -29)`,
`(61, -20, 95, -29)`, which puts the survival button at about
`guiLeft + 24, guiTop + 6` - on top of the panel's top edge, not outside it.
Nothing was seen there in the screenshots, so the next measurement has to read
the screen's widget list rather than its pixels.
## Round 120 - the curios button was there, and the config that cannot be saved

The curios button was reported as missing three rounds in a row. It was never missing. A probe that reads the
widget list back from the live screen answers what pixels could not:

```
[Forbric/Probe] screen children of net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.inventory.CreativeModeInventoryScreen = 2:
  EditBox(198,58,80x9) INVISIBLE CuriosButton(187,57,8x8) | guiLeft=116 guiTop=52 | renderables=1: CuriosButton
[Forbric/Probe] screen children of net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.inventory.InventoryScreen = 3:
  ImageButton(229,98,20x18) RecipeBookComponent CuriosButton(149,43,10x10) | guiLeft=125 guiTop=37
  | renderables=2: ImageButton CuriosButton
```

Two facts come out of that line. The button is in `children` **and** in `renderables`, so it is drawn; and with the
layout settled its coordinates are `guiLeft + 26 - 2 = 149`, `guiTop - 75 + 81 = 43` - the top-left corner inside the
inventory panel, a 10x10 icon that reads as a plain white square at 2x scale. Every "curios shows nothing" measurement
before this one was a misread of that square: the earlier probe dump was taken from an `InventoryScreen` whose
`guiLeft`/`guiTop` were still 0 (the dump ran on the first `Screen.init`, before the recipe-book layout is applied),
so the button sat at (24,6) and looked like part of the helmet slot.

The event path is intact and was checked one link at a time: `Screen.init` posts `ScreenEvent.Init.Pre` before
`init()` and `Init.Post` after it, both with `children` and the lambdas `Screen::addEventWidget` / `Screen::removeWidget`;
`addEventWidget` adds to `renderables` when the widget is `Renderable`, to `narratables` when it is `NarratableEntry`,
and to `children` in every case. `addListener` from a mod therefore does produce a drawn widget. No kernel change is
needed here.

What clicking the button did produce is a second, real bug. The click coincided with the integrated server stopping,
and the stop path threw:

```
java.lang.ClassCastException: class com.electronwill.nightconfig.core.SimpleCommentedConfig cannot be cast to
  class com.electronwill.nightconfig.core.file.CommentedFileConfig
  at net.minecraftforge.fml.config.ModConfig.save(ModConfig.java:80)
  at net.minecraftforge.fml.config.ConfigTracker.closeConfig(ConfigTracker.java:94)
  at net.minecraftforge.fml.config.ConfigTracker.unloadConfigs(ConfigTracker.java:63)
  at net.minecraftforge.server.ServerLifecycleHooks.handleServerStopped(ServerLifecycleHooks.java:111)
```

Forge's `ModConfig` keeps a `CommentedFileConfig` so that `save()` can write it back. The kernel builds its config
objects in memory (a plain `SimpleCommentedConfig`), so every `save()` on shutdown throws, and whatever a mod wrote into
its config during the session is lost. The same log also carries, repeated, `Unable to find registry with key
forge:condition_codecs for mod "forge"` and `forge:ingredient_serializers` - Forge's own registries are not reachable
under the `forge` namespace the mods ask for.

Measurements this round:

| what | before | now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| curios button in the screen widget list | unknown (pixel guesses) | present, at guiLeft+24, guiTop+6, in renderables |
| curios button drawn | "not shown" | drawn (the white 10x10 square at the panel corner) |
| Forge config save on world stop | not measured | throws ClassCastException, silently loses the config |
| forge:condition_codecs / forge:ingredient_serializers lookups | not measured | resolved nowhere |
## Round 121 - the config that could not be saved, and the holders behind it

`ConfigTracker.unloadConfigs(SERVER, path)` calls `closeConfig`, which does
`if (getConfigData() == null) return;` and otherwise lets `ModConfig.save()` run. `javap` on the 1.21.1 line
(`forge-runtime-1.21.1.jar`) shows `save()` as `aload_0; getfield configData; checkcast CommentedFileConfig;
invokeinterface save; return` - an unconditional cast. A config that was tracked but never opened still holds the
in-memory `SimpleCommentedConfig` that `ModConfig`'s constructor installs, so the cast threw
`ClassCastException` out of Forge's `ServerStoppedEvent` hook, where `KernelGameServerLifecycle.subscribe` caught it
and reported only "handleServerStopped forward failed; Forge-family mods won't observe this server-lifecycle
event".

The fix opens them first. `KernelForgeConfigLoad.openServerConfigs(Path)` (1.21.1 overlay) walks
`ConfigTracker.INSTANCE.configSets().get(SERVER)`, skips anything already backed by a `CommentedFileConfig`, and
calls Forge's own private two-argument `openConfig(config, dir)` for the rest - the same transaction that installs
the reader-backed config and its file watcher. `KernelGameServerLifecycle.installStopped` calls it before
`ServerLifecycleHooks.handleServerStopped`, reflectively on both ends: the helper lives in the 1.21.1 overlay, and
the directory comes from Forge's private `ServerLifecycleHooks.getServerConfigPath`.

Measured (2026-11-13 18:55:12, quickPlay world, window closed with WM_CLOSE so the whole shutdown runs):

- `[Forbric/Lifecycle] opened 1 MinecraftForge SERVER config(s) that were never opened before unloading them, so
  Forge's own save has a file to write: [forge (com.electronwill.nightconfig.core.SimpleCommentedConfig)]`
- `SimpleCommentedConfig cannot be cast` 0, `handleServerStopped forward failed` 0, `Stopping server` 1.

So the culprit was `forge`'s OWN server config, not a mod's: it is tracked and never opened on this base, because
the class that would open it (`ServerLifecycleHooks.handleServerAboutToStart`'s Forge half) is not what runs here.

The CCE had been hiding the next step. With it gone, Forge's `handleServerStopped` reaches
`GameData.revertToFrozen` and `ObjectHolderRegistry.applyObjectHolders` throws
`RuntimeException: Failed to apply some object holders`, with 26 suppressed
`IllegalStateException: Unable to find registry with key forge:biome_modifier_serializers` /
`forge:structure_modifier_serializers` / `forge:condition_codecs` / `forge:ingredient_serializers` for mod "forge".
Those are Forge-registry names the merged base registers under NeoForge's keys, so Forge's own `@ObjectHolder`
validation cannot resolve them. That is the next hole, and it is a registry-alias problem, not a config one.
## Round 122 - the registry the merge renamed, and the hook that cannot reach it

With the config cast gone (Round 121), Forge's `handleServerStopped` now reaches `GameData.revertToFrozen` and
`ObjectHolderRegistry.applyObjectHolders` throws `Failed to apply some object holders` with 26 suppressed
`IllegalStateException: Unable to find registry with key forge:biome_modifier_serializers` /
`forge:structure_modifier_serializers` / `forge:condition_codecs` / `forge:ingredient_serializers` for mod "forge".

The names are Forge's own: `javap` on `net/minecraftforge/registries/ForgeRegistries$Keys` lists exactly
`forge:biome_modifier_serializers`, `forge:structure_modifier_serializers`, `forge:condition_codecs`,
`forge:ingredient_serializers`. The same objects exist in this base under NeoForge's names - the merged jar's own
`reports/registry_order.json` lists `neoforge:biome_modifier_serializers`, `neoforge:condition_codecs`,
`neoforge:structure_modifier_serializers`, and `net/neoforged/neoforge/registries/NeoForgeRegistries` declares the
keys. The base is built from NeoForge's patched sources, so the registry manager half registers the NeoForge names
and Forge's lookups miss.

`RegistryManager.registries` is a Guava `BiMap` (`javap -p`: `com.google.common.collect.BiMap<ResourceLocation,
ForgeRegistry<?>> registries`), so the same `ForgeRegistry` cannot be stored under a second key - values must stay
unique. `getRegistry(ResourceKey)` forwards to `getRegistry(ResourceLocation)`, whose body is
`registries.get(name)` and nothing else. That leaves exactly one seam in the class: the lookup itself.

`ForgeRegistryNameFallbackTransformer` replaces that body with a call to `KernelForgeRegistryNames.lookup(manager,
name)`, which answers the asked name first and, only for a `forge:` name, retries under `neoforge:`. The helper is
reflection-only (no Minecraft or Forge types) so it compiles into both lines, and the transformer casts the result
back to `ForgeRegistry` so the erased helper descriptor does not break verification.

Measured on 2026-11-13 (quickPlay world, window closed with WM_CLOSE so the whole shutdown runs): the transformer
was registered - the ledger's declared count went 197 -> 198 - and its target never arrived. No
`[Forbric/Anchor] forbric-forge-registry-names was handed ...` line, no `[Forbric/Forge] Forge registry ...` line,
and the shutdown still printed the same 26 `Unable to find registry with key forge:...` entries. The
`sound-registry` repair, whose target is `net.minecraftforge.registries.ForgeRegistry`, never reports either.
**net.minecraftforge.\* classes are not loaded through this transformer chain**, so no bytecode repair can land on
them.

The anchor therefore declares itself `scanned` instead of `required`: claiming a repair the pipeline cannot reach
would make the ledger lie, which is the failure this project has paid for repeatedly. The body stays in place for
the day the class does load through. The fallback has to be installed from the runtime side instead - the
`registries` field is not final (`javap`), so the next attempt replaces it with a `BiMap` delegate whose `get`
retries the `neoforge:` name, which keeps `inverse()`, `forcePut` and the freeze/snapshot paths working because it
delegates to the real `HashBiMap` instead of reimplementing the map.
## Round 123 - the shutdown names registries this base never created, so stop asking

Forge's stop hook was dying twice over the same four names. The first half (Round 121) was the SERVER config Forge
never opened; this half is `ObjectHolderRegistry`.

Measured, at shutdown, after Round 122's bytecode route turned out unreachable:

- `RegistryManager.ACTIVE.registries` holds **30 entries, every one `minecraft:`** (block, fluid, item, mob_effect,
  sound_event, potion, entity_type, …, worldgen/biome). The merged base registers the registries Forge still names
  under its NeoForge-shaped space; the Forge names have no answer under either namespace.
- A `forge:`→`neoforge:` rename therefore cannot succeed, and the wrapper proves it: the delegate lookup for
  `neoforge:condition_codecs` misses too, in the same 30-entry map. Six names, twenty-six handlers, zero answers.
- `ObjectHolderRegistry.validate` runs `validators` (one `Runnable` per `RegistryObject`), each of which reads its
  registry and throws `IllegalStateException("Unable to find registry with key ... for mod ...")`. `apply` catches
  them into a list and `throwIfNeeded` rethrows one `RuntimeException: Failed to apply some object holders` — which
  is what ended `ServerLifecycleHooks.handleServerStopped`, so no MinecraftForge mod saw the world stop.
- `net.minecraftforge.*` never reaches this kernel's transformer chain (Round 122), so the validation itself cannot
  be edited; only the data it walks can.

Fix, `forbric-kernel/src/runtime/java/net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelForgeObjectHolders.java`: before Forge's stop
hook, drop the `namedHandlers` entries and the `validators` whose registry is in neither `RegistryManager.ACTIVE` nor
`BuiltInRegistries.REGISTRY`, and say so once — `looked at 12 @ObjectHolder registry name(s), 6 have no registry on
this base`, `dropped 26 object-holder validator(s) for registries this base never created`. Nothing that could have
succeeded is removed: a validator whose registry is absent has exactly one outcome, the exception.

Verified on the 1.21.1 line (20:03:54 start, WM_CLOSE at 20:07:24, log 2,794 lines):

- `Unable to find registry` 26 → **0**, `Failed to apply some object holders` 1 → **0**
- `Stopping server` 1, `SimpleCommentedConfig cannot be cast` 0, `handleServerStopped forward failed` 0
- world unaffected: `Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick] 3100 tick(s): mean 49.99ms (max 252.78ms)`,
  `Missing textures in model` 0, `InvalidInjectionException` 0, `InvalidMixinException` 35 (unchanged)

Also corrected: the alias logger said "answered with that one" before the alias lookup had been tried, which made six
failed lookups read as six successes in the previous round. It now only says that when the answer was found, and says
`a renamed registry lookup missed both ways: ... the map holds <all 30 keys>` when it was not.

26.2 census subset (`MergedBaseRepairClaimsStagedTest`, `RepairDriftCensusStagedTest`, `TransformerAnchorCensusTest`,
`InjectorExecutionCensusTest`): 14 tests, 0 skipped, 0 failed.

Not verified this round: whether any mod's `@ObjectHolder` fields would now stay unset where a Forge registry was
expected — none of the eight installed mods use them, and the six names have no registry on this base to hold
anything.
## Round 124 - the side Forge asks about is the Netty thread, not the server thread

Curios' panel could not be opened: clicking its button in the inventory ended the session with
`rex888888888 lost connection: Illegal packet received, terminating connection` (measured 2026-11-13, 20:13:30, with
`screenshots/2026-11-13_20.13.38.png` showing the resulting Connection Lost screen). The button itself was fine — a
probe had already listed it in the screen's own widget table at `(149,43,10x10)` with `guiLeft=125 guiTop=37`.

Where the disconnect comes from, from bytecode rather than from the message: `javap -c` over
`net/minecraftforge/common/ForgeHooks` shows `onCustomPayload(CustomPayloadEvent)` computing the side a payload arrived
on as `connection.getReceiving() == PacketFlow.CLIENTBOUND ? CLIENT : SERVER`, comparing it with
`EffectiveSide.get()`, and calling `connection.disconnect(Component.literal("Illegal packet received, terminating
connection"))` when they differ. `EffectiveSide.get()` is `Thread.getThreadGroup() instanceof
net.minecraftforge.fml.util.thread.SidedThreadGroup ? ((...).getSide()) : LogicalSide.CLIENT` — it recognises only its
own group class, so any other group answers CLIENT. `ServerLifecycleHooks` and `ModConfig` do not need to be involved.

First attempt: the server thread. `MinecraftServer.spin` builds it with
`new Thread(net/neoforged/fml/util/thread/SidedThreadGroups.SERVER, runnable, "Server thread")`, so on this base it never
sits in a group Forge recognises. `Thread.group` has no setter, and on JDK 21 there is no such field at all — the first
write failed with `java.lang.NoSuchFieldException: group` at `KernelServerThreads.replaceGroup(:114)`, because the slot
moved into `Thread$FieldHolder.group` (JEP 425). `sun.misc.Unsafe` writes either shape; the log then said
`[Forbric/Threads] the server thread now belongs to MinecraftForge's SERVER thread group`, and the payload still
disconnected the player.

That measurement is what made the real cause findable. A probe inserted at the head of
`ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.handleCustomPayload` printed

    [Forbric/Net] handleCustomPayload payload=ServerboundCustomPayloadPacket thread='Netty Server IO #1'
      group=net.neoforged.fml.util.thread.SidedThreadGroup receiving=SERVERBOUND effectiveSide=CLIENT

The check does not run on the server thread at all; it runs on a Netty IO thread, and `javap -c
ServerConnectionListener` shows why: both event-loop factories (`lambda$static$0` for Nio, `lambda$static$1` for Epoll)
call `ThreadFactoryBuilder.setThreadFactory(getstatic net/neoforged/fml/util/thread/SidedThreadGroups.SERVER)`.

So the fix targets the factory, not the thread. MinecraftForge's `SidedThreadGroup` is itself a `ThreadFactory`
(`javap` on `forge-runtime-1.21.1.jar`: `extends java.lang.ThreadGroup implements java.util.concurrent.ThreadFactory`),
so `ServerThreadGroupTransformer` replaces those two `getstatic` reads with
`KernelServerThreads.sharedServerThreadFactory()`, which returns Forge's group when it exists and the NeoForge group
otherwise. The name format and the daemon flag stay the class's own.

Measured after the change (20:33:13 launch, probe still installed):

- `[Forbric/Threads] the server's Netty IO threads are now created in MinecraftForge's SERVER thread group (...)`
- `[Forbric/Net] handleCustomPayload ... thread='Netty Server IO #1'
  group=net.minecraftforge.fml.util.thread.SidedThreadGroup receiving=SERVERBOUND effectiveSide=SERVER`
- no `Illegal packet received`, no `lost connection`
- the probe's synthetic click on the button (the screen's own `mouseClicked`, so no window focus is involved) moved the
  open screen to `top.theillusivec4.curios.client.gui.CuriosScreen`, and `jcmd GC.class_histogram` on the live process
  shows one `CuriosScreen` and two `CuriosContainer` instances.

Not verified this round: the panel's contents. `screenshots/2026-11-13_20.37.31.png` shows the inventory with its
crafting grid and the usual hotbar, and no curio slot strip beside it, so "curios works" would be an overstatement; what
is measured is that its screen is created and stays open. The `MixinInventory` 4/5 anchor report (`Inventory.hasAnyMatching`)
and the unreadable `curios.refmap.json` are unchanged.

26.2 census subset (`MergedBaseRepairClaimsStagedTest`, `RepairDriftCensusStagedTest`, `TransformerAnchorCensusTest`,
`InjectorExecutionCensusTest`): 14 tests, 0 skipped, 0 failed.
## Round 126 - the curios panel opens with nothing in it, and the payload did arrive

The curios button works now (Round 124), and the panel it opens is a real `CuriosScreen`. What it holds is not what
curios holds: the container it builds has the same 46 slots as vanilla's, with no curio slot among them.

The first reading of this looked like "the client receives no custom payloads at all" — a probe at
`ClientPacketListener.handleCustomPayload(CustomPacketPayload)` printed nothing while the server answered one
serverbound payload. That reading was wrong, and the base's own bytecode says why: the client's packet entry point is
`ClientCommonPacketListenerImpl.handleCustomPayload(ClientboundCustomPayloadPacket)`, which handles the NeoForge
control payloads (`minecraft:register`, `minecraft:unregister`, `neoforge:common_version`, …), then asks
`NetworkRegistry.isModdedPayload` and hands everything modded to `NetworkRegistry.handleModdedPayload` — and returns.
`ClientPacketListener.handleCustomPayload` is the vanilla tail of that method, so on this base it is never called for a
modded payload. Probing it measures nothing.

With the probe moved to the real entry point the client reports, in order:

    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=minecraft:register
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=minecraft:unregister
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=minecraft:brand
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=neoforge:config_file
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=neoforge:known_registry_data_maps
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=neoforge:registry_data_map_sync
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=curios:sync_data
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=curios:set_icons
    … neoforge:custom_time_packet, jade:server_ping_v1, mezz_config:*, appleskin:*
    [Forbric/Net] client packet handleCustomPayload id=neoforge:auxiliary_light_data

So `curios:sync_data` does arrive, and it is dispatched — `NetworkRegistry.handleModdedPayload` disconnects when a
channel or a registration is missing, and it did neither. The handler chain is there too: `CuriosClientPayloadHandler`
→ `CuriosClientPackets.handle(SPacketSyncData)` → `CuriosSlotManager.applySyncPacket(slotData)` and
`CuriosEntityManager.applySyncPacket(entityData)`.

What is missing is one step further in. The client's heap, while the curios panel is open:

    top.theillusivec4.curios.common.slottype.SlotType        20
    top.theillusivec4.curios.common.data.CuriosSlotManager    2
    top.theillusivec4.curios.common.data.CuriosEntityManager  2
    top.theillusivec4.curios.server.SlotHelper                1
    top.theillusivec4.curios.common.inventory.container.CuriosContainer 2
    top.theillusivec4.curios.common.inventory.CurioSlot        0

Slot definitions arrived (20 `SlotType`), and no `CurioSlot` was ever created — nor is there any `CuriosInventory` /
`CuriosItemHandler` instance at all. `CuriosContainer` builds its slots from the player's curios inventory handler, so
an absent handler means an empty panel no matter how well the slot manager is populated. The next measurement is which
capability/attachment supplies that handler on this base and why it is not attached to the client player.

Nothing here is called fixed. Recorded as measured: the panel opens, the payload arrives, the slot data arrives, the
inventory handler does not exist.
## Round 127 - the curios panel was empty because nothing assigns slots to players

Round 126 ended with the panel opening and holding no slots. The probes now answer the whole
question, and the answer is that the merged base is fine: this curios build ships the ten slot
*types* and no assignment of those types to any entity, so the capability provider has nothing
to hand out.

Measured on the open CuriosScreen before any change:

`| menu=top.theillusivec4.curios.common.inventory.container.CuriosContainer slots=46 ... | curiosSlots=10/10 curiosEntities=0/0 curiosInventory=null`

- `curiosSlots=10/10`: `CuriosSlotManager.CLIENT` and `SERVER` both hold the ten slot types that
  `data/curios/curios/slots/*.json` defines.
- `curiosEntities=0/0`: `CuriosEntityManager.CLIENT` and `SERVER` hold no entity at all.
- `curiosInventory=null`: `CuriosApi.getCuriosInventory(player)` returns empty, because the
  capability provider in `Curios.lambda$registerCaps$1` returns null when
  `CuriosApi.getEntitySlots(entity)` is empty.

The capability itself is registered: a census over
`net.neoforged.neoforge.capabilities.EntityCapability.getAll()` prints
`entity capability census (6): ... curios:inventory=130, curios:item_handler=130` and
`curios:inventory is provided for: [entity.minecraft.player, ...]` - 130 entity types including
the player. So the registration path works and only the data is missing.

Where the data would come from, by bytecode:

- `CuriosEntityManager.apply` reads `curios/entities/*.json` out of every pack, and then does
  `entitySlots.computeIfAbsent(EntityType.PLAYER, ...).putAll(configSlots)`, where `configSlots`
  is `CuriosSlotManager.fromConfig(...)`, i.e. the `slots` list of `curios-common.toml`.
- `CuriosConfig$Common` defines that list with `List.of()` as its default, and the instance
  config was `slots = []`, so both sources were empty.
- The released jar carries no entity file: `curios-neoforge-9.5.1+1.21.1.jar` is 410690 bytes
  with sha1 `418fcd42e3a7844c9bdc71c9b6401fdb3894e0c4`, byte-identical to the Modrinth release,
  and its `data/` holds only `curios/curios/slots/*.json`. Upstream tree HEAD adds
  `data/curios/tags/entity_type/player_like.json` and still no `entities/` directory.

Proof that the pipeline downstream of the data works: dropping a one-file datapack at
`saves/test01 (1)/datapacks/curios_player_slots/` with
`data/curios/curios/entities/players.json` = `{"entities":["minecraft:player"],"slots":["head",
"necklace","back","body","belt","charm","ring","curio","hands","bracelet"]}` and enabling it
in-game (`Pack file/curios_player_slots is already enabled`) changes the same probe line to

`| menu=...CuriosContainer slots=56 ... | curiosSlots=10/10 curiosEntities=10/10 curiosInventory=10 CurioInventoryCapability`

with ten `RenderButton`s in the screen children, and `screenshots/2026-11-13_22.14.49.png`
shows the ten curio slots on the left of the inventory. 46 vanilla slots plus ten curio slots
is 56, so the container, the sync packet, the capability and the GUI all agree.

Conclusion: nothing in the merge or the loader is wrong here. The documented way to give
players slots without a datapack is the `slots` list in `config/curios-common.toml`; the test
instance now lists the ten standard ids there instead of the empty default.

Probes added this round (both behind properties, both off by default):

- `-Dforbric.probeCapabilities` in `RegistrationEventSteps.capabilityCensus`, printing the
  entity capability census and, for `curios`, the entity types it covers.
- `curiosState()` in `KernelScreenProbe`, printing `curiosSlots`, `curiosEntities`,
  `curiosInventory` on any `top.theillusivec4.curios` screen.

## Round 128 - Forge's nine custom registries were never created, because the event went to the wrong bus

`logs/latest.log` said, on every launch of the 1.21.1 instance:

    [Forbric/Forge] created 0 custom registr(ies) via NewRegistryEvent + fired Forge RegisterEvent x81
    [Forbric/Forge] a renamed registry lookup missed both ways: forge:ingredient_serializers -> neoforge:ingredient_serializers (delegate HashBiMap, 30 entries); the map holds minecraft:block, ... minecraft:worldgen/biome

and then, once per decoded ingredient value:

    [Forbric/Ingredients] MinecraftForge's ingredient codec threw - NeoForge's answer is used for this value
    java.lang.NullPointerException: Cannot invoke "IForgeRegistry.getCodec()" because the return value of
      "net.minecraftforge.registries.DeferredRegister$RegistryHolder.get()" is null
        at net.minecraftforge.common.ForgeHooks.lambda$enhanceIngredientCodec$19(ForgeHooks.java:1213)

The registry map holding exactly the 30 `minecraft:` registries `GameData`'s static initialiser builds was the
tell: not one `forge:` registry existed, so every `forge:` lookup ended in the alias fallback and then in a
warning. `forge:nbt`, `forge:intersection`, `forge:difference` and `forge:compound` therefore could not decode on
this base at all, and every recipe that used one fell back to NeoForge and threw on the way.

Javap, in the order that narrowed it down:

- `ForgeRegistries` holds nine `DeferredRegister$RegistryHolder<T>` fields (`INGREDIENT_SERIALIZERS`,
  `CONDITION_SERIALIZERS`, `FLUID_TYPES`, `HOLDER_SET_TYPES`, `DISPLAY_CONTEXTS`, `ENTITY_DATA_SERIALIZERS`,
  `GLOBAL_LOOT_MODIFIER_SERIALIZERS`, `BIOME_MODIFIER_SERIALIZERS`, `STRUCTURE_MODIFIER_SERIALIZERS`) and, in
  `registry(ResourceKey, Supplier)`, does `DeferredRegister.create(...)` -> `registries.add(dr)` ->
  `dr.makeRegistry(supplier)`. Nothing creates the registry; the holder stays null until something posts a
  `NewRegistryEvent` that the registrar is listening on.
- `DeferredRegister.register(IEventBus bus)` is one line: `bus.register(new EventDispatcher(this))`.
  `EventDispatcher.createRegistry(NewRegistryEvent)` then calls `event.create(registryFactory.get())`.
- `ForgeDeferredRegistriesSetup.setup(IEventBus)` walks `ForgeRegistries.registries` and registers each one, and
  refuses to run twice. `ForgeMod`'s constructor calls it with the bus from
  `FMLJavaModLoadingContext.get().getModEventBus()` - the CONTAINER's bus.
- This kernel posted `NewRegistryEvent` on the handle's own `busGroup()`, which is a different `IEventBus` object.
  The registrars were therefore never listening on it, which is exactly what the first measurement said
  (`NewRegistryEvent created nothing although 9 DeferredRegister(s) are waiting on it (9 of them with a registry
  factory)`), and the retry that called `setup` confirmed it from the other side
  (`Forge says its DeferredRegister setup has already run`).

Three changes:

1. `KernelForgeBaseline.fireNewRegistryEvent` now posts on the handle's bus AND on each mod container's
   `getEventBus()` (new `containerBus(handle)` helper, by-reference deduplicated), and
   `registerWaitersThenRefill` no longer calls Forge's setup - it posts a second `NewRegistryEvent` on those buses
   and fills it.
2. `ForgeEventPost.post` no longer filters by `eventBus.isInstance(handle)`. That filter compares against
   `ForeignType.EVENT_BUS`, which on this line resolves to `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.bus.EventBus` - the
   26.2 spelling; the 1.21.1 bus is `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.IEventBus`, so the retry died with
   `ClassNotFoundException` and nothing was posted. It now looks the `post(Object)` method up on the bus's own
   class and its supertypes.
3. `KernelForgeRegistryNames.explainMiss` stays quiet for a name a waiting `DeferredRegister` is still about to
   create (`aboutToBeCreated`). `DeferredRegister.makeRegistry` asks `RegistryManager.ACTIVE` whether the name
   exists *so that* it can create it when it does not, so those nine probes are correct behaviour, not damage -
   measured: nine of them printed before the nine registries appeared, and every name existed a moment later.

Measured on the instance afterwards (22:43:54 launch, `logs/latest.log` 2,444 lines):

    [Forbric/Forge] created 9 custom registr(ies) via NewRegistryEvent + fired Forge RegisterEvent x90
    renamed registry lookup missed: 0        (was 9)
    ingredient codec threw:        0        (was one warning + one stack per decoded value)

Not measured this round: `Preparing spawn area` did not appear before the run was stopped at 22:47:00, so the
world was not re-entered with these changes. The curios config route (`config/curios-common.toml`
`slots = [head, necklace, back, body, belt, charm, ring, curio, hands, bracelet]`) is in place but still unproven;
the datapack route was proven in Round 127 (`CuriosContainer slots=56`, `curiosEntities=10/10`).
## Round 129 - the curios slots come from one config line, and the format is `id=`, not the slot name

Round 127 left one thing unproven: the datapack route filled the panel (`CuriosContainer slots=56`,
`curiosEntities=10/10`), but the documented config route had never been run. Two launches in a row then failed
to reach a world at all, and the reason was this line in `latest.log`:

    Caused by: java.lang.ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 1 out of bounds for length 1
        at top.theillusivec4.curios.common.data.CuriosSlotManager.fromConfig(CuriosSlotManager.java:241)
        at top.theillusivec4.curios.common.data.CuriosSlotManager.apply(CuriosSlotManager.java:161)
        ...
        at net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.worldselection.WorldOpenFlows.loadWorldDataBlocking(WorldOpenFlows.java:201)

`javap -p -c CuriosSlotManager` says why. `fromConfig(Map)` walks `CuriosConfig.COMMON.slots`, tokenises each
entry on `;`, then each token on `=` and reads `parts[0]`/`parts[1]` **before** it checks that the entry has an
`id`:

    String[] parts = token.split("=");
    map.put(parts[0], parts[1]);          // <-- AIOOBE when the entry is a bare name

So `slots = ["head", "necklace", …]` - the shape the docs' prose invites - throws inside a reload listener, the
listener's future completes exceptionally, and the quick-play world load dies with it. Nothing in the game said
"your config is wrong"; it just never opened the world.

The working line, measured on this instance with no datapack present
(`Test-Path saves\test01 (1)\datapacks\curios_player_slots` is False):

    slots = ["id=head", "id=necklace", "id=back", "id=body", "id=belt", "id=charm", "id=ring", "id=curio", "id=hands", "id=bracelet"]

Measured afterwards (`logs/latest.log` 2,610 lines, launched 16:11:36):

    Loaded 10 curio slots
    Loaded 1 curio entities
    Preparing spawn area: 0%            (world opened; [Forbric/Tick] 6 lines)

and the screen probe on the opened panel:

    children of top.theillusivec4.curios.client.gui.CuriosScreen = 13: ... 10x RenderButton ...
    | menu=...CuriosContainer slots=56 ... | curiosSlots=10/10 curiosEntities=10/10
      curiosInventory=10 CurioInventoryCapability

screenshots/2026-11-14_16.17.21.png shows the ten slot column next to the vanilla inventory.

What this settles: curios is not broken on this base and never was. The engine end of the chain - capability
registration (130 entity types, `curios:inventory`), the `curios:sync_data` payload, `CuriosSlotManager`,
`CurioInventoryCapability`, the container's 46 + 10 slots and the button that opens it - all work. What was
missing was one line of user configuration, with a syntax that throws instead of complaining.
## Round 130 - the remove_spawns anchor was gone because this base never had the bug

After Round 128 the ledger still ended with one loud line:

    [Forbric/Anchor] forbric-forge-world-modifiers was handed
    net.minecraftforge.common.world.ForgeBiomeModifiers$RemoveSpawnsBiomeModifier and made no edit -
    its anchor is gone. forge:remove_spawns ClassCastExceptions inside Forge's own lambda

The repair it names was written for a shape where Forge's modifier removes from a `WeightedList$Builder` (an
interface default the merge lost) and has to be rerouted through `KernelForgeWorldgen.removeIfValue`. Javap on
the class this base actually loads says the shape is not there:

    public void modify(Holder<Biome>, BiomeModifier$Phase, ModifiableBiomeInfo$BiomeInfo$Builder)
      53: aload 4
      55: aload 8
      57: invokevirtual  MobSpawnSettingsBuilder.getSpawner:(LMobCategory;)Ljava/util/List;
      60: aload_0
      61: invokedynamic  #0:test:(LForgeBiomeModifiers$RemoveSpawnsBiomeModifier;)LPredicate;
      66: invokeinterface java/util/List.removeIf:(Ljava/util/function/Predicate;)Z

and `net.minecraftforge.common.world.MobSpawnSettingsBuilder` declares
`public java.util.List<MobSpawnSettings$SpawnerData> getSpawner(MobCategory)`, whose body hands back the builder's
own `List` (it checkcasts `java/util/List`). Removing from a real `List` is ordinary Java: there is nothing that
can ClassCastException here, so there was never anything to repair on this base - the REQUIRED anchor was
declaring a bug that only exists in the shape it was written against.

Two changes, both honest about that:

- `routeRemoveIfThroughTheLostDefault` now looks for both shapes. If it finds `java.util.List.removeIf(Predicate)`
  and no `WeightedList$Builder.removeIf`, it says so once -
  `... already removes from a java.util.List - nothing to repair on this base, the WeightedList$Builder.removeIf
  anchor is not present` - and declines, instead of silently reporting a dead anchor.
- the anchor's severity drops from REQUIRED to HEDGE, whose documented meaning is exactly this case: "expected not
  to match on today's carriers; silent when absent, worth a word if it ever wakes up". The repair code stays, so a
  carrier that brings the `WeightedList$Builder` shape back is still repaired automatically.

Measured (16:21:44 launch, `logs/latest.log` 2,602 lines): the ERROR line is gone, the INFO line above is present,
`Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick]` 3, and the ledger's one remaining miss is `forbric:loot-table-event-bridge`
- the documented "this line has no such seam" case, not a regression.

Remaining `made no edit` lines, all three already known and none of them new:
`forbric-forge-part-tracking` on `ServerLevel$EntityCallbacks`, `forbric-common-network-interop#fabricAddonHandle`
on `ServerConfigurationNetworkAddon`, `forbric-client-part-tracking#neoForgePartsTracked` on
`ClientLevel$EntityCallbacks`.
## Round 131 - the part-tracking anchor names a map this base does not have, and a launch lesson worth writing down

The last loud line of Round 130 was:

    [Forbric/Anchor] forbric-forge-part-tracking was handed
    net.minecraft.server.level.ServerLevel$EntityCallbacks and made no edit - its anchor is gone.

Measured on `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar`:

    net.minecraft.server.level.ServerLevel
      final it.unimi.dsi.fastutil.ints.Int2ObjectMap<net.neoforged.neoforge.entity.PartEntity<?>> dragonParts;
      public java.util.Collection<net.neoforged.neoforge.entity.PartEntity<?>> getPartEntities();

There is no MinecraftForge `partEntities` field on ServerLevel — this base keeps NeoForge's parts map under the
vanilla name `dragonParts`, and it is typed to NeoForge's `PartEntity`. The merged `Entity.getParts()` returns
`net.neoforged.neoforge.entity.PartEntity[]` as well, so a `net.minecraftforge.entity.PartEntity` has nowhere to be
tracked and cannot even override `getParts()` with a Forge signature. The repair is not wrong, it is inapplicable:
the field it edits does not exist, and the shape that would make it apply cannot be restored from this seam.

So the two ServerLevel/ClientLevel callbacks became HEDGE ("expected not to match on today's carriers"), and the
silent `return 0` became a `declined(...)` carrying the measurement, which reads in the log:

    [Forbric/Entity] left a MinecraftForge mod's multipart entity as merged: ServerLevel keeps NeoForge's parts map
    under the vanilla name dragonParts, typed to NeoForge's PartEntity, and the merged Entity.getParts() returns
    NeoForge's array type - a MinecraftForge multipart entity has no map here to be tracked in

Measured (17:46:44 launch, `logs/latest.log` 2,605 lines): `Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick]` 6,
`104 of 198 declared repair(s) landed, and 1 did NOT` (the `forbric:loot-table-event-bridge` line, unchanged),
and `made no edit` down to three, none of them this one:
`forbric:loot-table-event-bridge`, `forbric-common-network-interop#fabricAddonHandle`,
`forbric-client-part-tracking#neoForgePartsTracked`.

### The launch lesson (four launches lost to it)

`JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` was set in an earlier PowerShell call and each call here is a fresh process, so the later
launches ran without `-Dforbric.dependencyDialog=off`. The game then forks
`DependencyDialogMain ... --compatibility`, that child waits for input, and the log stops at

    [pool-2-thread-1/INFO]: GL info: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080/PCIe/SSE2 GL version 4.6.0

with two `java.exe` processes alive (the second is the dialog) and no `Preparing spawn area` ever. Reading that as
"my change broke the launch" would have been wrong twice over: the Java option has to be exported in the same
invocation as `Start-Process`, and a second `java.exe` whose command line ends in `DependencyDialogMain` is the
dialog, not a stuck client.
## Round 132 - two claims asked for an edit that this base makes impossible

The ledger still said `made no edit` for three claims after Round 131. Two of them were not repairs that had
stopped working; they were repairs whose target classes cannot be edited on this base at all, and both were
measured before being written down.

`forbric-common-network-interop#fabricAddonHandle` claimed three classes and expected a `handle` of its own in
each. javap on the remapped fabric module (`remapped\b9f567e03bdf7752-fabric-networking-api-v1-0.116.17.jar`)
shows `AbstractChanneledNetworkAddon` declaring `public boolean handle(CustomPacketPayload)`,
`ClientConfigurationNetworkAddon` declaring its override, and `ServerConfigurationNetworkAddon` declaring no
`handle` at all - it inherits the parent's body, and the parent's body is the one this repair rewrites. The
third anchor now carries the reason as a hedge instead of a required edit that can never happen.

`forbric-client-part-tracking#neoForgePartsTracked` was judged by a flow analysis written for the shape where
both ecosystems' `getParts()` exist: NeoForge's array reaching `ClientLevel.dragonParts`, every MinecraftForge
array null-tested first. This base has no `dragonParts` list on `ClientLevel` - javap of
`ClientLevel$EntityCallbacks.onTrackingStart` shows `isMultipartEntity()` then
`getParts()[]Lnet/neoforged/neoforge/entity/PartEntity;` stored and looped, with each part going through
`ClientLevel.partEntities.put(id, part)` into an `Int2ObjectMap`. That is exactly the outcome the repair exists
to produce, written in NeoForge's own shape, and the analysis had no rule for it. `ClientPartTrackingFlow` now
recognises it first (`registersIntoTheMapThisBaseKeeps`) and the claim lands on the measured body rather than on
an edit.

| world | loads | `Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick]` to 2500 tick(s), mean 49.98ms |
| shutdown | clean | `Stopping server` 1, `Failed to apply some object holders` 0 |
