## Round 133 - twenty-five guest mixins are refused by name, with the field and the target named

`InvalidMixinException` sat at 35 across every launch. The largest group was one shape, not one bug: a guest mixin
declares `@Shadow` on a field this base's class does not have, Mixin locates a shadow by name in the target's
hierarchy, and on a miss it refuses the **whole** mixin - every injector in it goes with the shadow, and the log
said so only as a Mixin stack trace.

Measured, javap on `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar` and on the remapped fabric module jars:

| guest mixin | shadows | base class declares |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `fabric-registry-sync-v0.client.mixins.json:BlockColorsMixin` | `IdMapper<BlockColor> field_1995` | `private final Map<Block, BlockColor> blockColors` |
| `fabric-registry-sync-v0.client.mixins.json:ItemColorsMixin` | `IdMapper<ItemColor> field_1996` | `private final Map<Item, ItemColor> itemColors` |
| `fabric-rendering-v1.mixins.json:BlockColorsMixin` | same as above | same |
| `fabric-rendering-v1.mixins.json:ItemColorsMixin` | same as above | same |
| `fabric-registry-sync-v0.client.mixins.json:ParticleManagerMixin` | `Int2ObjectMap<ParticleProvider<?>> field_3835` | `ParticleEngine` has no such field |
| `fabric-model-loading-api-v1.mixins.json:ModelLoaderBakerImplMixin` | `field_40571`, `field_40572` | `ModelBakery$ModelBakerImpl` declares neither |
| `fabric-lifecycle-events-v1.mixins.json:MinecraftServerMixin` | `field_25318` | `MinecraftServer` declares no such field |

Their refmaps do not answer these shadows either - `client-fabric-registry-sync-v0-refmap.json` lists only
`BlockColorsMixin: {"<init>": ...}`, so Mixin remaps nothing and looks for the literal `field_1995`.

`MergedBaseMixinCompat.refusal` now takes this decision before Mixin does: `unresolvedShadow(node, classes)` walks
the mixin's `@Shadow` **fields**, and for each one walks the target's superclass chain (`declaresField`, depth 16);
if no target declares the name, the mixin is refused with the name and the target in the reason, so the log reads
"source @Shadow field(s) field_1995 are not declared by net/minecraft/client/color/block/BlockColors nor by their
superclasses on this base, so Mixin refuses the whole mixin; what it wires is not here to wire". A target this
process cannot read is treated as present - the gate never refuses on evidence it cannot see.

What that means for the mods: these seven mixins wire an `IdMapper`/`Int2ObjectMap` structure this base does not
keep. They were already not applying; what changed is that they now say why, once, by name, instead of arriving as
an exception with a stack. Block and item colours keep working the way the earlier colour repair made them work -
lookup uses the key the registration wrote - and nothing reads the `IdMapper` any more.

| | before | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `InvalidMixinException` | 35 | 10 |
| `@Shadow field` mentions | 13 | 13 in the reasons above, each naming a field and its target |
| world | loads | `Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick]` to 3100 tick(s), mean 49.99ms |
| shutdown | clean | `Stopping server` 1, `Failed to apply some object holders` 0, `handleServerStopped forward failed` 0 |

The ten that remain are `@Accessor`/`@Invoker` shapes (`fabric-particles-v1`'s `ParticleManagerAccessor`),
`@Shadow` on a target this pass cannot read (`fabric-game-rule-api-v1`'s `GameRuleCommandVisitorMixin` on
`GameRuleCommand$1`), and `field_48769` on `RegistryDataCollector$ContentsCollector` - the next round's material.
26.2 census subset: `14 tests, 0 skipped, 0 failed` (26s).
## Round 134 - the shadow gate stops at 25 because it cannot read the file that would let it judge the rest

After Round 133 the gate refused 25 of the 35 guest mixin failures. Two of the ten left looked like they should have
been caught, and both were measured rather than assumed.

`fabric-registry-sync-v0.client.mixins.json:ClientRegistriesDynamicRegistriesMixin` declares

    @Mixin(targets = ["net/minecraft/client/network/ClientRegistries$DynamicRegistries"])

a class 1.21.1 does not have. Its own refmap answers that name:

    "mappings": { "net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/registry/sync/client/ClientRegistriesDynamicRegistriesMixin":
      { "Lnet/minecraft/client/network/ClientRegistries$DynamicRegistries":
        "Lnet/minecraft/client/multiplayer/RegistryDataCollector$ContentsCollector;" } }

Mixin follows it, applies to `RegistryDataCollector$ContentsCollector`, and then refuses the class on
`@Shadow field field_48769`. The gate read the literal annotation, could not open
`net/minecraft/client/network/ClientRegistries$DynamicRegistries.class`, and its own rule - an unreadable target is
treated as present, never refuse on evidence this file cannot see - kept it from judging. Correct rule, wrong reach.

`fabric-game-rule-api-v1.mixins.json:GameRuleCommandVisitorMixin` is the plainer case: its target
`net/minecraft/server/commands/GameRuleCommand$1` **is** in the base and declares exactly one field,
`final com.mojang.brigadier.builder.LiteralArgumentBuilder val$literalargumentbuilder` - no `field_19419`. If the
gate had read that class file it would have refused the mixin. It did not, which is the same blind spot from the
other side: the class file the gate asks for is not served to it.

I tried to close the first case properly, by rewriting `@Mixin(targets = ...)` through the config's own refmap
before judging (`retargetThroughRefmap`, reading the config's `"refmap"` key and its `mappings` class entries through
the same `Function<String,byte[]>` the pass already uses, `ForbricMixinService::readGameResource`). Measured on a
full launch: the redirect log line never fired and the mixin still failed, so that function does not serve a jar-root
`client-fabric-registry-sync-v0-refmap.json` to this pass. **The change was reverted, not shipped**: unproven code
that claims to fix something is worse than the miss it was meant to fix. The rebuilt jar is byte-for-byte the
Round 133 build (7,519,142 bytes).

So the ten that remain are three shapes, and all three want the same missing thing - the mod's own resource view,
not the game class view:

| shape | example | what it needs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| stale `@Mixin(targets = ...)` name | `ClientRegistriesDynamicRegistriesMixin` -> `RegistryDataCollector$ContentsCollector` | the config's refmap `mappings` class entries |
| nested target the pass cannot open | `GameRuleCommandVisitorMixin` -> `GameRuleCommand$1` | that class file served to the pass |
| accessor/invoker, not `@Shadow` | `fabric-particles-v1`'s `ParticleManagerAccessor` | an `@Accessor`/`@Invoker` gate of its own |

Next round starts there: give `MergedBaseMixinCompat` the jar that holds the config - the same view `discover` reads
mixin classes from today - and the first two rows fall out of the gate that already exists.
## Round 135 - the last three guest mixins ask about classes this loader will not hand out

The guest-mixin gate added in Round 133 refuses a mixin by name when a field it shadows is provably absent from its
target. After Round 134 it still let three targets through, and the log now shows exactly which ones:

    net.minecraft.client.multiplayer.RegistryDataCollector$ContentsCollector
    net.minecraft.client.particle.ParticleEngine
    net.minecraft.server.commands.GameRuleCommand$1

Each one has an @Shadow field that the base's version of the class does not declare, so each one should have been
refused. The reason it was not is not the gate but the reader: at gate time the resource lookup answers null for the
class it is asked about, so the gate cannot see the field and treats the target as unjudged. Measured with
-Dforbric.probeMixinResources, one line per entry:

    net/minecraft/server/command/GameRuleCommand$1.class                      unreadable
    net/minecraft/client/gui/screen/world/EditGameRulesScreen$RuleListWidget$1.class  unreadable
    net/minecraft/server/network/ServerPlayNetworkHandler$1.class             unreadable
    net/minecraft/server/world/ServerWorld$ServerEntityHandler.class          unreadable
    net/minecraft/client/world/ClientWorld$ClientEntityHandler.class          unreadable
    net/minecraft/client/network/ClientRegistries$DynamicRegistries.class      unreadable

Two things are worth naming. Every entry is an inner class, and the two names the annotations carry are spelled
net/minecraft/server/command/ and net/minecraft/client/gui/screen/ - the intermediary-era singular spelling, not the
net/minecraft/server/commands/ and .../screens/ that the base uses. That is what a guest jar compiled against yarn says
its target is called, and it is why the probe prints a path that looks like a typo.

What was added, and what it bought. readGameResource now falls back three ways when the loader's own lookup answers
null: the jar that serves the resource (NativeAbsentTargets.servingJar), every jar the loader exposes URLs for, and,
for an inner class, the jar of the outer class it belongs to. The probe above exists to say which of the three
answered, and in this run none of them did - which is itself the result: those classes are served by a loader that
refuses to name its jar, not by a jar that lacks them (the merged base jar holds all three entries, checked).

The count moved from 10 InvalidMixinException to 8, and stays at 8 across two builds and two launches with 3 distinct
targets left. Which two stopped failing is not attributable from this run, so it is recorded as a count, not a cause.

A refmap translation was written and then removed. The guest jars do carry the rename at their root -
fabric-game-rule-api-v1-0.116.17.jar holds client-fabric-game-rule-api-v1-refmap.json with
net/minecraft/server/command/GameRuleCommand$1=net/minecraft/server/commands/GameRuleCommand$1 in its mappings - so
reading the refmap would give the gate the real target name. It never fired: the same findResource that cannot answer
for the target cannot answer for the mixin class either, so the refmap's jar is just as unreachable, and the
diagnostic printed nothing at all. Code that cannot be made to run is worse than no code, so the helper and its call
site were reverted rather than committed behind a flag. What is left is the tri-state judgement - ABSENT, UNREADABLE,
FOUND - so that the field-is-not-there case and the this-pass-cannot-look case are two different answers in the
source, and only the first refuses.

Self-test: launch, log 2,461 lines, Preparing spawn area 1, InvalidMixinException 8, made no edit 1, ledger 104 of 198
landed and 1 did not, Illegal packet received 0. 26.2 census subset 14 tests, 0 skipped, 0 failed.
## Round 136 - the loot bridge had no site at all, and the reload completes in a future nobody wrapped

`forbric:loot-table-event-bridge` was the last anchor reporting `made no edit`, and the line it printed
described the wrong seam: "1.21.1 has no `TagLoader.loadTagsForRegistry` for the bridge to fire it from".
`javap -p` on `net.minecraft.server.ReloadableServerRegistries` in the merged base shows what the class really
has: `reload(LayeredRegistryAccess, ResourceManager, Executor)`, `scheduleElementParse(LootDataType,
RegistryOps, ResourceManager, Executor)`, `apply`, `createUpdatedRegistries`, `validateRegistry` and lambdas.
There is no tag-load call and no `EventHooks.loadLootTable` — loot tables are parsed by `LootDataType` itself,
one registry at a time, and the only place where a registry is known to be finished is the future that
`scheduleElementParse` hands back. Its whole body is

    0: aload_0; 1: aload_2; 2: aload_1; 3: invokedynamic get:(LootDataType;ResourceManager;RegistryOps)Supplier
    8: aload_3; 9: invokestatic CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(Supplier,Executor); 12: areturn

so there is exactly one `ARETURN` and the arguments are never reassigned: at that point slot 0 is the
`LootDataType`, slot 2 the `ResourceManager` and slot 1 the `RegistryOps`.

### First, the claim became a measurement

`-Dforbric.probeLoot=true` installs a `java.lang.reflect.Proxy` on `ALL_LOADED` of both
`net.fabricmc.fabric.api.loot.v3.LootTableEvents` and `...loot.v2.LootTableEvents` and prints a line every time
the proxy is invoked. Two `counting listener installed` lines appeared at 19:12:41 and **not one
`really fired` line followed the world load**: fabric's own dispatch point was not merely suspected silent, it
was measured silent. The reason is next to it in the log:
`guest mixin fabric-loot-api-v3:ReloadableRegistriesMixin applies only partially — 6/7 anchors resolve, missing:
@At(INVOKE) net.minecraft.registry.ReloadableRegistries$ReloadableWrapperLookup.getOps`, followed by
`suppressed mixin ReloadableRegistriesMixin from fabric-loot-api-v3`. Fabric injects its `ALL_LOADED` call into
`reload` at a point this base does not have, so nothing calls the event.

### The fix: bracket the future

`LootTableEventBridgeInjector.bracketTheReload` walks `scheduleElementParse`, and in front of every `ARETURN`
inserts `ALOAD 0; ALOAD 2; INVOKESTATIC LootTableEventDispatch.afterParse(Object,Object,Object)Object;
CHECKCAST java/util/concurrent/CompletableFuture`. `afterParse` only acts when the `LootDataType` is
`LootDataType.TABLE` (compared against the static field, so the predicate and modifier registries are left
alone) and chains a `thenApply` that fires `ALL_LOADED` once the registry is populated, then logs
`the loot-table registry finished loading (N entries) — LootTableEvents.ALL_LOADED was fired at this point,
which is where fabric-loot-api-v3's own mixin fires it`.

### One silent failure fell out of the way first

`resolve()` used to require `net.fabricmc.fabric.impl.loot.FabricLootTable`. This fabric-loot-api-v3 does not
contain that interface (the remapped jar has `api/loot/v3/*`, `impl/loot/LootUtil` and `mixin/loot/*`), so the
`ClassNotFoundException` turned the whole dispatch `ABSENT` and took REPLACE, MODIFY **and** ALL_LOADED with
it. The interface is now optional: without it a loaded table keeps the holder the registry already gave it,
and the three events fire without the extra rebinding step.

### And the world died the moment the events were real

With the dispatch live, the first reload ended in
`Failed to load level data or datapacks, can't proceed with server load`, caused by
`IllegalClassLoadError: Illegal classload request for accessor mixin
net.fabricmc.fabric.mixin.loot.LootTableAccessor. The mixin is missing` raised from
`FabricLootTableBuilder.copyOf(FabricLootTableBuilder.java:103)` under `LootTableEventDispatch.afterLoad`.
The log says why, one line earlier:
`guest accessor mixin fabric-loot-api-v3:LootTableAccessor cannot bind — @Accessor field
LootTable.randomSequenceId:Ljava/util/Optional;`, and `LootPoolAccessor`, `LootPoolBuilderMixin`,
`LootTableBuilderMixin` are suppressed beside it. `copyOf` reads the table through the accessor interface, so
on this base REPLACE/MODIFY cannot run at all — firing them takes the reload down with them.

`LootTableEventDispatch` now asks the class instead of the mixin config: `hasAccessor(LootTable)` looks for
`fabric_getPools` and never loads the accessor interface (that load is the exception). When the accessor is
absent, `afterLoad` returns the table unchanged and says once:
`this base cannot run fabric-loot-api-v3's REPLACE/MODIFY: its accessor and builder mixins are suppressed on
the merged base (@Accessor LootTable.randomSequenceId cannot bind), so FabricLootTableBuilder.copyOf() has no
pools to read and calling it threw IllegalClassLoadError for LootTableAccessor and killed the world load.
A mod's REPLACE/MODIFY listener is registered and stays uncalled; ALL_LOADED still fires`.

### Measured (19:31:59 launch, jar 7,524,902 bytes, installed at both library paths)

- `[Forbric/Anchor] 105 of 198 declared repair(s) landed; 77 target(s) were never loaded on this side, and 16
  hedge(s) correctly found nothing to do` — the `did NOT` clause is gone; the last unlanded anchor landed.
- `[Forbric/Loot] fabric-loot-api-v2 ALL_LOADED really fired (1)` and
  `fabric-loot-api-v3 ALL_LOADED really fired (1)` from the counting proxies: a Fabric mod's listener on either
  event is now called once per reload, measured, not inferred.
- `[Forbric/LootBridge] the loot-table registry finished loading (1178 entries)`.
- The accessor WARN above, and no `Failed to load level data` anywhere.
- World: `Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick] 3100 tick(s): mean 49.99ms, max 260.05ms`.
- Shutdown (WM_CLOSE): `Stopping server` 1, `Unable to find registry` 0, `Failed to apply some object holders`
  0, `handleServerStopped forward failed` 0, `SimpleCommentedConfig cannot be cast` 0, java exited.
- 26.2 census subset: `test: 14 tests, 0 skipped, 0 failed` (37s).

### Not verified

No mod in this instance listens on REPLACE or MODIFY, so the claim "they cannot run here" rests on the four
suppressed mixins and the `IllegalClassLoadError` the accessor produced, not on a mod misbehaving. Fixing them
means making `@Accessor LootTable.randomSequenceId` bind on the merged base, which is its own round.
## Round 137 - the bus a foreign mod registers on is empty, and the cast was only its symptom

Key mapping registration for a mod that is not a NeoForge mod failed with one line:

```
[Forbric/ShimContext] a foreign mod threw while registering key mappings
java.lang.ClassCastException: class net.neoforged.bus.EventBus cannot be cast to class net.neoforged.bus.api.IEventBus
```

**The cast was not the defect.** Removing it and posting by shape (`postOn(Class, Object)` finds the bus's own
single-argument `post`, so no name in this class has to resolve to the same `IEventBus` the bus implements) moved the
failure one step in, to a measurement:

```
RegisterKeyMappingsEvent cannot be built in jdk.internal.loader.ClassLoaders$AppClassLoader@75b84c92, the loader the
bus's post(Event) belongs to; the bus offers net.neoforged.bus.api.Event from …AppClassLoader; here Event is from
net.forbric.kernel.classloading.ForbricClassLoader and RegisterKeyMappingsEvent is from …ForbricClassLoader
```

`net.neoforged.bus.api.Event` exists twice on this base: once through the application class loader (the startup `-cp`
carries `neoforge-runtime-1.21.1.jar`, `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar` and `forbric-kernel-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar`), and once
through `ForbricClassLoader`. A bus built from the first copy takes the first copy of `Event`, so no event the kernel
constructs is ever an instance of it.

`-Dforbric.probeKeys=true` counts the buses instead of arguing about them (`KernelBusSupport.keyCensus`):

```
[Forbric/Bus] key census: cl=ForbricClassLoader@60344d8f, busLoader=ForbricClassLoader@60344d8f, …  sameEventClass=true
… 20 lines with cl=ForbricClassLoader@60344d8f …
…  1 line  with cl=AppClassLoader@75b84c92, busLoader=AppClassLoader@75b84c92 …
[Forbric/ShimContext] 14 non-NeoForge mod(s) ran with a NeoForge ModContainer of their own …
```

21 `makeModBus` calls, 20 of them handed `ForbricClassLoader` — the 21st is the shim: `KernelForeignShimContext.identityFor`
is called with the application loader, and that bus is the one that cannot accept the kernel's event.

**Fix, first half:** a bus is minted in the loader whose `net.neoforged.bus.api.Event` is the one the kernel posts. When
the handed-over loader disagrees with the thread's context loader (the game's, during mod loading) and the context
loader can resolve `BusBuilder`, the context loader wins:

```
[Forbric/Bus] a bus is minted in ForbricClassLoader@67d6007a instead of AppClassLoader@75b84c92 — the loader that was
handed over resolves net.neoforged.bus.api.Event to a second copy, and a bus built there cannot accept an event this
kernel constructs
```

After that: no `ClassCastException`, no `cannot be built in …`, `[Forbric/Anchor] 105 of 198 declared repair(s) landed;
77 target(s) were never loaded on this side, and 16 hedge(s) correctly found nothing to do`, world loads (`Preparing
spawn area` 1), shutdown clean (`Stopping server` 1, `Unable to find registry` 0, `Failed to apply some object
holders` 0).

**Fix, second half — and the reason this round fixes no keybind yet.** With the event finally postable, the delivery
added nothing, so the next measurement counts listeners on the buses themselves (`KernelForeignShimKeys.describe`,
every `Map` field by name and size, `-Dforbric.probeKeys=true`):

```
[Forbric/ShimKeys] delivered to 14 bus(es), 82 -> 82 key mapping(s);
net.neoforged.bus.EventBus@24918730 listeners=0; net.neoforged.bus.EventBus@34ba7650 listeners=0; … (14 buses, each 0)
```

Not one of the 14 buses the shim hands out has a listener on it. The mods never registered on those buses — whatever
path they take to `FMLJavaModLoadingContext.get().getModEventBus()` (or `@EventBusSubscriber` scanning) does not arrive
at the container the shim hands back, so posting a `RegisterKeyMappingsEvent` at them can never add a mapping. That is
the next seam, and it is a routing question, not an event-shape question.

Not verified: that any mod's keybind appears in Controls or fires. The 82 key mappings reported by
`[Forbric/ForgeClient] applied MinecraftForge client init` are the Forge side and were already there.
## Round 138 - a suppressed accessor was defined, then threw from the stub Mixin never filled in

Round 137 left one real error in the log: `fabric-data-attachment-api-v1`'s entrypoint died before it could register
anything. The first cause was the kernel's own suppression: `CustomPayloadS2CPacketAccessor` cannot bind on the merged
base, so `ForbricMixinService` removes it from the config, and Mixin then answers any request for that name with
`IllegalClassLoadError: Illegal classload request for accessor mixin … The mixin is missing`. `AttachmentChange.<clinit>`
references the accessor, so the class-load refusal propagated into `ExceptionInInitializerError` →
`NoClassDefFoundError: Could not initialize class …AttachmentSyncPayloadS2C` and both entrypoints failed.

The loader now defines such a class instead of refusing it (`ForbricClassLoader.buildAndDefineGameClass`, catch on
`IllegalClassLoadError`, gated by `ForbricMixinService.wasDropped(name)`). That alone only moved the failure one step:
the accessor is an interface whose static method body is Mixin's placeholder —

    public interface net.fabricmc.fabric.mixin.attachment.CustomPayloadS2CPacketAccessor {
      public static int getMaxPayloadSize();          // ()I, flags ACC_PUBLIC|ACC_STATIC
      Code: new UnsupportedOperationException("Implemented via mixin"); athrow
      RuntimeVisibleAnnotations: @Accessor(value="MAX_PAYLOAD_SIZE")
    }

— so the entrypoint then failed with `UnsupportedOperationException: Implemented via mixin` at
`CustomPayloadS2CPacketAccessor.getMaxPayloadSize(CustomPayloadS2CPacketAccessor.java:28)`. The `@Mixin` annotation is
`RuntimeInvisibleAnnotations`, not visible, which is why it has to be read from the class file rather than reflection.

`SuppressedAccessorBodies` (new, `forbric-kernel/src/main/java/net/forbric/kernel/mixin/`) rewrites exactly those stub
bodies into calls to `net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelSuppressedAccessor` (new, `runtime/`), which resolves the field on
the first of the mixin's targets that declares it and reads or writes it reflectively. It only touches a method whose
body is exactly the `UnsupportedOperationException("Implemented via mixin")` throw, and only when the class carries
`@Mixin`, so ordinary classes are never rewritten. `@Invoker` gets the same treatment through `KernelSuppressedAccessor.call`.
A field that is genuinely absent fails with the field name and every target that was tried, instead of a bare stub throw.

Measured on the base: `ServerboundCustomPayloadPacket` declares `private static final int MAX_PAYLOAD_SIZE`, and the
accessor's `@Mixin(value=[class Lnet/minecraft/network/protocol/common/ServerboundCustomPayloadPacket;])` is already
mojmap — the merge remapped the class reference, only the accessor could not bind.

Result of one launch (jar 7,542,319B):

- `[Forbric/Mixin] net.fabricmc.fabric.mixin.attachment.CustomPayloadS2CPacketAccessor is a mixin this kernel suppressed
  on the merged base; defined it anyway so a mod that references it still loads — 1 stub(s) implemented; its
  @Accessor/@Invoker bodies now read and write their fields reflectively`
- `Implemented via mixin` 1 → 0
- `main/client entrypoint of fabric-data-attachment-api-v1 failed` 2 → 0
- `Preparing spawn area` 1, `[Forbric/Tick] 100 tick(s): mean 49.25ms (max 267.11ms)`
- `[Forbric/Anchor] 105 of 198 declared repair(s) landed; 77 target(s) were never loaded on this side, and 16 hedge(s)
  correctly found nothing to do` (no `did NOT` clause)
- `InvalidMixinException` 8 (unchanged), `made no edit` 0
- shutdown: `Stopping server` 1, `Unable to find registry` 0, `Failed to apply some object holders` 0,
  `SimpleCommentedConfig cannot be cast` 0, process exits
- 26.2 census subset: `test: 14 tests, 0 skipped, 0 failed`

Not verified: whether attachment sync now completes end to end (the entrypoint registers, but no attachment-carrying mod
is installed, so nothing exercises the round trip); whether other suppressed accessors now return meaningful values —
this run implemented exactly one stub, and a second one (`VarIntsAccessor`, `@Accessor field VarInt.MAX_BYTES:I`) never
reached a call, so its reflective path is untested.
